## Chapter 12.
<note>original: "Adhyaya"</note>

### *The Third House.*
<note>original: "bhava"</note>

| Verse | Description |
|-------|-------------|
| 1 | What can be deduced from the 3rd <term>house</term> <note>original: "bhava"</note>? |
| 2 | <term>The places of brothers</term> <note>original: "Bhratristhanas"</note> enumerated, and the periods when a person will have brothers. |
| 3 | The <term>planetary period</term> <note>original: "dasa"</note> leading to the increase of brothers. |
| 4 | The characteristics of the 3rd house making a person long-lived and powerful. |
| 5 | The characteristics of the 3rd house leading to a person's prosperity or decay. |
| 6-8 | The <term>planetary combinations</term> <note>original: "yogas"</note> leading to the loss of brothers and sisters. |
| 9 | The <term>combination</term> leading to the acquisition of brothers from a step-mother. |
| 10 | The combinations beneficial to brothers. The combinations affecting brothers adversely and leading to their loss. |
| 11 | The conditions under which younger brothers may flourish or cease to exist. |
| 12 | The conditions unfavourable to the growth of younger brothers. |
| 13 | The same matter as that of *Verse 11*. |
| 14 | The combination leading to the birth of one younger brother and no more. |
| 15-16 | The combinations under which may be born a younger brother endowed with health, happiness and long life. |
| 17-20 | The means of ascertaining the number of brothers and sisters a person may have. |
| 21-22 | The means of ascertaining under what conditions planets produce brothers or sisters or both. |
| 23 | Four kinds of planets that affect younger brothers and sisters beneficially or otherwise. |
| 24 | Another method for finding the number of younger brothers and sisters. |
| 25 | The planets that influence a brother's fortunes for the worse or for the better. |
| 26-27 | Union or disunion among the brothers and the time when this may happen. |

<note>Glossary of terms used: **Chapter** (Adhyaya), **House** (bhava), **Places of Brothers** (Bhratristhanas), **Planetary period** (dasa), **Verse** (Sloka), **Planetary combinations** (yogas).</note>

<summary>This page lists the contents for Chapter 12, focusing on the astrological "Third House" which governs siblings. The verses provide methods for predicting the number, health, and lifespan of brothers and sisters, as well as family harmony.</summary>
<keywords>Third House, siblings, astrology, planetary periods, planetary combinations, brothers, sisters, family relations</keywords>

### Chapter 15 <note>original: "Adhyaya"</note>

| Verses <note>original: "Slokas"</note> | Description |
|:---|:---|
| 1-2 | How a horoscope serves as a guide for understanding one's destiny. |
| 3 | How a woman's horoscope should be prepared and the specific details it should reveal. |
| 4-6 | Points to be specially noted in a female's horoscope and the indicators used to interpret them. |
| 7-8 | The specific factors to be examined in a woman's horoscope to determine her personal characteristics and temperament. |
| 9 | The **planetary combination** <term>yoga</term> <note>a specific alignment of planets that produces a particular result</note> under which a woman may be born into a life of dependency or have several husbands. |
| 10 | The **planetary combinations** <term>yogas</term> under which a woman becomes famous for her talents, education, and moral virtue. |
| 11 | Similar in meaning to verses 9 and 10. |
| 12-18 | How a woman's character is shaped by the combination of her birth sign and her **thirty-fold division** <term>Trimsamsa</term> <note>a specific sub-division of a zodiac sign used in Indian astrology to fine-tune character analysis</note>. These results are explained in relation to the signs ruled by Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn. |
| 19 | The influence of planets in the **Seventh House** <term>7th bhava</term> <note>the house of marriage and partnerships</note> that lead to widowhood, marriage, or being cast aside by a husband. The planetary conditions under which a woman might seek relationships outside of marriage. |
| 20 | <insert>✓</insert> Definitions for an **unchaste woman** <term>Swairinee</term> <note>original: "स्वैरिणी"; refers to a woman who follows her own inclinations, often implying infidelity in this historical context</note> and a **remarried woman** <term>Punarbhu</term> <note>original: "पुनर्भू"; literally 'becoming again,' a term for a woman who enters a second marriage</note>. |
| 21 | <insert>✓</insert> The **planetary combination** <term>yoga</term> under which both a woman and her mother lead immoral lives. The combination which, with certain exceptions, indicates diseases of the womb. |
| 22 | The **planetary combination** <term>yoga</term> under which a woman is rejected by her husband or experiences constant conflict with him. |
| 23 | How specific planets located in certain positions within the **Seventh House** <term>7th bhava</term> are responsible for a woman being cast aside, her widowhood, domestic fighting, remarriage, or a long and happy married life. |
| 24 | The first three parts of this verse are similar to verse 19. The final part describes a **planetary combination** <term>yoga</term> that leads to barrenness. |
| 25 | Whether a woman becomes a source of affection or dislike for her husband, based on which of the two **planetary combinations** <term>yogas</term> mentioned in the verse influences her. |

| **9-10** | The sequence to be followed in each wheel <term>chakra</term>. |

| **34-36** | The definition of the "Place of Death" <term>maranasthana</term> for each of the several planets. |
| **37-46** | The specific effects of planets being located in either the body <term>deha</term> or the soul <term>jeeva</term> points. |
| **47-75** | The effects of the "Wheel Periods" <term>chakradasa</term> in relation to the twelve houses <term>bhavas</term> <note>the various life departments like wealth, family, and health</note>. |
| **76-81** | The conditions under which the good, bad, and neutral effects of the wheel periods described above manifest, and the degree to which they are realized. |
| **82-83** | The method for determining whether a particular wheel period <term>chakra dasa</term> will take effect while the person is in their own country or while abroad. |

# Chapter Ten of Jataka Parijata

---

## ॥ The Chapter on Ashtakavarga ॥

### Adhyaya X.

### ASHTAKAVARGA.

<note>The term "Ashtakavarga" literally translates to "The Eight-fold Group." It is a unique method in Vedic astrology where the influences of the seven planets and the Ascendant are plotted to determine the auspiciousness of different zodiac signs for an individual.</note>

The following eleven verses from the <term>Horamakaranda</term> <note>A classic Sanskrit treatise on time-keeping and astrology, whose title translates to "The Nectar of Astrology"</note> are inserted here, because they form an appropriate introduction to this Chapter.

> Each planet moving from the place it occupied at the birth of a person admittedly produces its own peculiar effect varying with its progress through the 12 <term>Rasis</term> <note>zodiac signs</note>. Owing to the admitted variation in this effect, the ancients say that it cannot be laid down as absolutely identical (even) in the case of persons born under the same star.
<note>original: "yadyaccare janmagṛhādgrahāṇāṃ pṛthak phalaṃ dvādaśarāśipuñjam | nṛṇāṃ tadekakṣaṇabhujāṃ phalasya bhedānnaikāntikamuktamāryaiḥ || 1 ||"</note>

> Astrologers declare that to be the <term>Janmarasi</term> <note>the "birth sign" or Moon sign</note> of a person wherein the Moon was at the time of his birth. The several places in which the planets and the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> may be in all possible ways cannot consist of only seven.
<note>original: "yatra sthitaḥ śītakaro narāṇāṃ syājjanmarāśiṃ samudāharanti | yadvā tathā yeṣu khagāḥ salagnāḥ sthitā na te saptakato bhavanti || 2 ||"</note>

> Hence every person is declared to have eight signs as the seats of the seven planets and the <term>Lagna</term>, and it is with reference to these eight places that all the good and evil effects of a person's life due to the disjoined or conjoined states of the planets and the <term>Lagna</term> are calculated—a process which, when completed, the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> result as it is called will become revealed.
<note>original: "ato'ṣṭarāśirmanujo'tra sarvaḥ prokto'tha tebhyaśca śubhāśubhāni | phalāni teṣāṃ ca viyogayogā yadā'ṣṭavargotthaphalaṃ sphuṭaṃ syāt || 3 ||"</note>

<summary>This page introduces Chapter Ten of the Jataka Parijata, focusing on the Ashtakavarga system of astrology. It provides three introductory verses explaining that planetary effects are individualized based on eight points of reference: the seven planets and the Ascendant.</summary>

<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Jataka Parijata, Horamakaranda, Janmarasi, Lagna, Rasis, Vedic astrology, planetary transits</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed technical exposition of the Ashtakavarga system from Chapter Ten. While the previous page introduced the concept of planetary transits and the general theory of individual variations in astrological effects, this page provides the specific numerical data for the Sun, the Moon, and Mars. These verses define exactly which houses (relative to other celestial bodies) are considered beneficial or "auspicious" for a given planet.</meta>

<page-num>650</page-num>

<header>Jataka Parijata Adh. X</header>

->From himself, Mars, and Saturn, the Sun is auspicious in the 1st, 4th, 2nd, 9th, 10th, 11th, and 8th places; from Venus in the 6th, 7th, and 12th; from Jupiter in the 5th, 6th, 9th, and 11th; from the Moon in the 11th, 6th, 10th, and 3rd; from Mercury in the 10th, 9th, 11th, 12th, 5th, 6th, and 3rd; and from the Lagna, the Sun is very favorable in those places which are the 12th and the houses of increase (3, 6, 10, 11). ॥ 4 ॥<-
<note>original: "svārārkibhyo dineśaḥ svasukhabhṛti tapaḥ khāptalābhāspadataḥ śukrādistāririhpheśvaritanayapatau lābhavartī sureśvāt | candrāllābhārikabhetriṣu dakṣitanayātsantyadharmāntyajeṣu jño'ko lagnād vyayābhyupacayagṛhagāḥ supraśastasta evārkān || 4 ||"</note>

With reference to its <term>अष्टकवर्ग</term> (<term>Ashtakavarga</term>) <note>A system of point-based astrology used to predict the strength of planets based on their positions relative to eight points: the seven planets and the Ascendant.</note> the <term>Sun</term> is declared exceedingly auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from itself, <term>Mars</term> and <term>Saturn</term>; in the 6th, 7th and 12th places from <term>Venus</term>; in the 5th, 6th, 9th and 11th places from <term>Jupiter</term>; in the 3rd, 6th, 10th and 11th places from the <term>Moon</term>; in the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th, 11th and 12th from <term>Mercury</term>; and lastly in the 3rd, 4th, 6th, 10th, 11th and 12th places from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or rising sign.</note>.

->From the Moon and the Lagna in the 6th, 11th, 10th, and 3rd; from Mars in the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th, and 11th; from itself in the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 7th, 10th, and 11th; from the Sun in the 3rd, 6th, 7th, 8th, 10th, and 11th; from Saturn in the 3rd, 5th, 6th, and 11th; from Mercury in the quadrants (1, 4, 7, 10), the 3rd, 5th, 8th, and 11th; from Jupiter in the quadrants, 8th, 12th, and 11th; and from Venus in the 5th, 9th, 4th, 10th, 3rd, 11th, and 7th, the Moon is auspicious. ॥ 5 ॥<-
<note>original: "indullagnāt ṣaḍāyatatridaśasu kusutān sammadharmāntyajeṣu svāt sāmantādyu sūryāt samadanamṛtiṣu syādyadhīṣaṭsu mandāt | jñāt kendrādyātmajātriṣu vibudhaguroḥ kendrarandhrāntyalābhe śukrāddhīdharmabandhuprasārasahajanabholābhagaśca praśastaḥ || 5 ||"</note>

The <term>Moon</term> is auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, 10th and 11th places from the <term>Lagna</term>; in the 2nd, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from <term>Mars</term>; in the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 7th, 10th and 11th places from itself; in the 3rd, 6th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from the <term>Sun</term>; in the 3rd, 5th, 6th and 11th places from <term>Saturn</term>; in the 1st, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from <term>Mercury</term>; in the 1st, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10, 11th and 12th places from <term>Jupiter</term>\*; and lastly, in the 3rd, 4th, 5th, 7th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from <term>Venus</term>.

->In the quadrants, the 1st, and the 8th from itself; in the houses of increase (3, 6, 10, 11) and the 5th from the Sun; from the Moon in the 6th, 3rd, and 11th; from the Lagna in the 1st, 3rd, 4th, 11th, and 10th; from Mercury in the 6th, 3rd, 11th, and 5th; from Jupiter in the 12th, 6th, 10th, and 11th; from Venus in the 6th, 11th, 12th, and 8th; and from Saturn in the quadrants, 8th, 11th, and 9th, Mars is auspicious. ॥ 6 ॥<-
<note>original: "kendrāsvāṣṭagaḥ svādupacayatanayeṣvarkataḥ ṣaṭtrilābhe prāleyāṃśoḥ praśastaḥ prathamasahajapadalābhamadhyeṣu lagnāt | jñāta ṣaṭtryāyātmaneṣu vyayaripudaśabhāveṣu jīvācca śukrāt pallābhāntyāṣṭameṣu kṣitija inasutāt kendrarandhrāyadharme || 6 ||"</note>

<term>Mars</term> is auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from itself, in the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 10th and 11th places from

---

\*In the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from <term>Jupiter</term>, according to some.

<summary>This page details the specific auspicious house positions for the Sun, Moon, and Mars within the Ashtakavarga system, calculating their benefits relative to the seven planets and the Lagna (Ascendant).</summary>
<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn, Lagna, Jataka Parijata, planetary strength, auspicious houses</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical enumeration of the Ashtakavarga system, specifically concluding the auspicious house positions for Mars and detailing those for Mercury and Jupiter. This follows the Sun’s calculations established on the previous page.</meta>

<page-num>051</page-num>

# Chapter Ten

<note>The following text concludes the description of Mars's favorable positions relative to the other planets.</note>

...the Sun; in the 3rd, 6th and 11th places from the Moon; in the 1st, 3rd, 6th, 10th and 11th places from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>; in the 3rd, 5th, 6th and 11th places from Mercury; in the 6th, 10th, 11th and 12th places from Jupiter; in the 6th, 8th, 11th and 12th places from Venus; and lastly, in the 1st, 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from Saturn.

->Mercury is favorable in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th and 11th places from Venus; in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th from Mars and Saturn; from Jupiter in the 6th, 8th, 11th and 12th; from the Sun in the 5th, 6th, 9th, 11th and 12th. From himself, the 1st, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th, 11th and 12th; from the Moon, the 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th and 11th; and from the Lagna, in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th and 11th. ॥ 7 ॥<-
<note>original: "svah śukat svāṣṭalābhāṣṭamatayamasukhe śaśiputre kujārgyoḥ sājñādāraiśca jīvād vyayaripunidhanāyeṣu śasto dineśāt..."</note>

Mercury is auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th and 11th places from Venus; in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from Mars and Saturn; in the 6th, 8th, 11th and 12th places from Jupiter; in the 5th, 6th, 9th, 11th and 12th places from the Sun; in the 1st, 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th 11th, and 12th places from itself; in the 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from the Moon; in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from the <term>Lagna</term>.

->Jupiter is auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th from Mars; in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th from the Sun; from himself in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th. From Mercury in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th and 11th; from the Moon in the 2nd, 5th, 7th, 9th and 11th; from Saturn in the 3rd, 5th, 6th and 12th; from Venus in the 2nd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th and 11th; and from the Lagna in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, 6th, 7th, 9th, 10th and 11th. ॥ 8 ॥<-
<note>original: "jīvo bhīma su svakendrāgamamṛtiṣu raveḥ satrirandhraiḥ sva svān saptāṣṭvindujāt pada syasutasukhatanuvyomadharmāgameṣu..."</note>

Jupiter is benefic in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from Mars, in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from the Sun; in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from itself, in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from Mercury, in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 5th, 6th, 7th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from the <term>Lagna</term>. in the 2nd, 5th, 7th, 9th and 11th places from the Moon; in the 3rd, 5th, 6th and 12th, places from Saturn. and lastly, in the 2nd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from Venus.

->Venus is auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th and 11th from the Moon; in the 3rd, 4th, 5th, 7th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th from the Lagna; from itself in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th.<-
<note>original: "indordhyasvārtharigaiṣu vyarimadananabhontyeṣu lagnāt praśasto svāntyābhyāsātiṣu svāt svayapanidhamabhavetkautau daityamantri ।"</note>

<summary>This page lists the specific zodiacal houses where Mercury and Jupiter are considered beneficial based on their distance from other planets and the Ascendant. These calculations are part of the Ashtakavarga system, a method used in Hindu astrology to quantify planetary strength.</summary>

### Chapter X

-> In the 5th, 9th, 8th, 4th, and 10th houses from Saturn; in the 5th, 9th, 2nd, 11th, and 10th from Jupiter; <-
-> From Mercury, in the 5th, 3rd, 11th, and 1st houses; from Mars, in the 5th, 6th, and the <term>Apoklima</term> houses. ॥ 9 ॥ <-
<note>original: "dhīdharmāṣṭasvambuviddaśasu ravijāt dhītapahsvāyalābhe jīvāt jñāt dhītrilābhakṣatanuṣu kujāddhībhayāpoklimeṣu" - This verse lists the auspicious points (Bindus) for Venus (Shukra).</note>
<note>Apoklima refers to the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses in an astrological chart.</note>

Venus is auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th, 11th and 12th places from the Moon; in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th and 11th places from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or rising sign.</note>; in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from itself; in the 8th, 11th and 12th places from the Sun; in the 3rd, 4th, 5th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from Saturn; in the 5th, 8th, 9th, 10th and 11th places from Jupiter; in the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th and 11th places from Mercury; and lastly in the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 9th, 11th and 12th places from Mars.*

-> From himself, Saturn is in the 3rd, 6th, 5th, and 11th; from Mars, in the 11th and 12th; from the Sun, <-
-> In the <term>Kendras</term>, 2nd, and 11th; from Mercury, <unclear>in the 6th, 11th, 3rd, 4th, 10th, 8th, and 9th</unclear> from the Moon; <-
-> In the 6th, 3rd, and 11th; from the Lagna, in the <term>Upachaya</term> and 4th houses; in the 6th, 11th, and 12th from Venus; <-
-> And from Jupiter, in the 12th, 5th, 11th, and 6th houses, Saturn is praised. ॥ 10 ॥ <-
<note>Kendras are the angular houses: 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th. Upachaya are the "houses of growth": 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th.</note>

Saturn is benefic in the 3rd, 5th, 6th and 11th places from itself; in the 3rd, 5th, 6th, 10th and 11th and 12th places from Mars; in the 1st, 2nd, 4th, 7th, 8th, 10th and 11th places from the Sun; in the 6th, 8th, 9th, 10th, 11th and 12th places from Mercury; in the 3rd, 6th and 11th places from the Moon; in the 1st, 3rd, 4th, 6th, 10th and 11th places from the Lagna; in the 6th, 11th and 12th places from Venus; and lastly in the 5th, 6th, 11th and 12th places from Jupiter.

-> These positions are said to grant favorable results; any others are considered harmful. <-
-> Calculations should be made accordingly; greater points yield more results for planets in their signs. <-
-> Situated in a friend's sign, their own exaltation, or growth houses, these planets nourish auspicious gifts; <-
-> Located in debilitation, non-growth houses, or an enemy's sign, the results are not auspicious. ॥ 11 ॥ (Gunakara) <-
<note>Gunakara is an authority cited here to summarize the effects of the Ashtakavarga points.</note>

The benefic positions have been enumerated; the rest are to be understood as malefic. The two-fold distinctions viz. of benefic and malefic should be clearly set forth. The planets produce the effect of whichever of these two preponderate in the [sign].

---
\* But according to <term>Parasara</term> <note>One of the foundational sages of Indian astrology.</note>, the 3rd, 4th, 6th, 9th, 11th and 12th places from Mars.

<meta>This page continues the technical explanation of the Ashtakavarga system, transitioning from the specific point distributions for Venus and Saturn discussed on page 652 to the general rules of application. It focuses on how planets produce results as they move through the signs, based on the auspicious and inauspicious marks calculated from their birth positions.</meta>

<page-num>653</page-num>

### Chapter X

<term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system of "eightfold strength" where points are distributed across the zodiac based on the positions of the seven planets and the ascendant.</note> calculations are made from the <term>Rasi</term> <note>The zodiac sign or "moon sign."</note> they occupy at the time of birth (see the previous verses). In their own, friendly, or <term>Upachaya</term> <note>Houses of "growth" or improvement: the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses.</note> places, the planets invariably advance the benefic effect revealed by the Ashtakavarga. In their depression (debilitation), inimical, or <term>Apachaya</term> <note>Houses of decline or non-growth, essentially the houses that are not Upachaya.</note> places, they generally fail to sustain whatever good effect may appear from the Ashtakavarga process.

-> One should calculate from the places where the planets were situated at the time of birth. <-
-> * The planet produces results through its transit based on the auspicious lines and inauspicious dots located there. (Satyacharya) <-
<note>original: "janmakāle grahā yatra sthitāstatsthānato gaṇayet | rekhābindūṃśca tatrasthe cārāccaivaphalaṃ grahaḥ || (satyācāryaḥ)"</note>

From where the planets are at the time of birth, the benefic and malefic dots should be marked. A planet in its progress through a Rasi produces the effect of the malefic or benefic dots appearing therein (as ascertained from its Ashtakavarga).

<note>Footnote text clarifies terminology variation between texts</note>
* In other books, a "line" (<term>Rekha</term>) is used to denote what a "dot" (<term>Bindu</term>) signifies here—namely, a benefic point. Conversely, the term "dot" (<term>Bindu</term>) in other books is employed in the sense of a malefic point. This variation must be kept in mind when reading quotations from different authors. <note>This identifies a major source of confusion in medieval astrological texts: whether a "dot" represents a good or bad point.</note>

---

-> Having determined the favorable and unfavorable results, the final effect is known by the difference between them. <-
-> The wise say that the planets grant these results according to their movement through the signs. (Devakirti) <-
<note>original: "kaṣṭeṣṭaphalaṃ jñātvā tadantaravaśātphalam | cārakrameṇa khacarā yacchantīti jagurbudhāḥ || (devakīrtiḥ)"</note>

The several places detailed above are to be reckoned with respect to the Rasi (and not the <term>Bhava</term> <note>The "house" calculated based on the degree of the horizon, as opposed to the whole sign/Rasi.</note>) occupied by each of the seven planets and the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or rising sign.</note> at the time of birth. These places should be understood as benefic and the rest malefic. As each Rasi happens to be counted eight times (once for each of the seven planets and the Lagna) for ascertaining the benefic as well as the malefic places, the maximum number of countings cannot in any case exceed eight. If, after the computation of a planet's Ashtakavarga, it is found that a particular Rasi has more countings on the benefic side than on the malefic, it means that the net result is beneficial to the native.

If we should denote a benefic place by a dot (.) and a malefic one by a vertical stroke (|), we can at a glance understand the difference between these two kinds of countings in any Ashtakavarga.

<summary>This page explains the general principles of applying Ashtakavarga points, noting that planetary transits yield results based on whether they move through signs with high benefic counts. It also clarifies a significant historical variation in astrological notation where 'dots' and 'lines' can have reversed meanings across different texts.</summary>

<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Rasi, Upachaya, Apachaya, Rekha, Bindu, Satyacharya, Devakirti, transit results, astrological notation</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Ashtakavarga system from page 653. While the previous page established the general rules of planetary strength based on their birth positions, this page introduces the quantitative method for calculating the specific degree of "good" or "bad" results a planet produces during its transit, using a base-four neutrality scale.</meta>

<page-num>654</page-num>

# Jatakaparijata, Chapter X

### The Effects of Planetary Transits (Ashtakavarga)

The effect of a planet's arrival in any <term>Bhava</term> <note>An astrological "house" or division of the zodiac representing specific life themes.</note> in its progress through the orbit—known as <term>Gochara</term> <note>The transit of planets as they move through the signs in real-time, relative to the birth chart.</note>—is beneficial, mixed, or harmful according to the number of "benefic dots" in the <term>Rasi</term> <note>A zodiac sign.</note> representing that house in the <term>Bhinnashtakavarga</term> <note>The "individual" Ashtakavarga chart for a single planet.</note> of that specific planet. 

If the dots are greater than, equal to, or less than 4, the results vary accordingly. Dots falling short of the prescribed minimum of 4 indicate an untoward or negative effect produced by a planet, even if it appears to be in a favorable position; conversely, dots in excess of the prescribed minimum signify good results, despite any otherwise unfavorable position of the planet. 

For example:
* If all 8 dots are benefic, the result is fully beneficial.
* If 7 are benefic and 1 is malefic, the result is (7 minus 1) divided by 8, which equals 6/8 or 3/4 beneficial.
* If in any sign the number of benefic dots is 6 and malefic strokes is 2, the result is (6 minus 2) divided by 8, which equals 4/8 or 1/2 beneficial.
* If the number of benefic dots is 5, the effect will be (5 minus 3) divided by 8, which equals 2/8 or 1/4 beneficial.
* If the count is 4, the result is (4 minus 4) divided by 8, which is 0; this means the transit is neutral—neither good nor evil.

> *That which is auspicious with one point becomes a giver of evil through five positions. That which is lost with four—the calculation of all results is similarly determined.*
<note>original Sanskrit: "ekena yaḥ śubhaḥ syātpañcabhiḥ sthānaiḥ sa pāpado bhavati | yastu चतुर्भिर्नष्टः सर्वफले कहपनाप्येवम् ||"</note>
->(**Yavaraavana**)<- <note>Likely a reference to the Yavana school of Indian astrology, which incorporated Greek influences.</note>

Take for example the horoscope mentioned in the *Brihat Jataka* <note>A foundational 6th-century Sanskrit text on astronomy and astrology by Varahamihira.</note>, Chapter VII, Verse 6, reproduced below:

| **Rasi Kundali** | <column-break/> | **Mars' Ashtakavarga** |
| :--- | :---: | :--- |
| <note>Birth Chart showing planetary positions</note> | | <note>Point distribution for the planet Mars</note> |
| [ Pisces ] [ Aries ] [ Taurus ] [ Gemini ] | | [ 4 ] [ 3 ] [ 2 ] [ 3 ] |
| [ Aquarius ] [ **Birth Chart** ] [ Cancer ] | | [ 4 ] [ **Mars** ] [ 3 ] |
| [ Capricorn ] [ **Diagram** ] [ Leo ] | | [ 3 ] [ **Points** ] [ 4 ] |
| [ Sagittarius ] [ Scorpio ] [ Libra ] [ Virgo ] | | [ 4 ] [ 5 ] [ 2 ] [ 2 ] |

<meta>The diagrams above represent a sample Rasi Kundali (zodiac chart) and the corresponding Bhinnashtakavarga for Mars, where the numbers in the second table indicate the benefic points assigned to each sign. This provides a visual example of the mathematical principles discussed in the text.</meta>

<summary>This page explains the quantitative calculation of astrological results during a planet's transit, establishing that four benefic points represent a neutral state, while more or fewer points dictate the degree of success or failure.</summary>
<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Bhava, Gochara, transit, Bhinnashtakavarga, Rasi, Brihat Jataka, Mars, benefic dots, Yavana astrology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Ashtakavarga system, moving from the general mathematical principles of "benefic dots" (bindus) and "malefic strokes" (rekhas) to specific examples involving Mars. It further clarifies how the inherent strength of a planet at birth (its natal position) modifies the results of its current transit (Gochara).</meta>

<page-num>055</page-num>

# Chapter X

In the sign <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note>, there are 3 benefic dots and 5 malefic strokes. The 3 benefic dots neutralize with 3 of the malefic strokes. The net result is 2 malefic strokes, which means that Mars, when passing through this sign, will be productive of 2/8 or 1/4th evil. Similarly for the other signs. There is one peculiarity to be noticed in the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system of point-based calculation used to predict the effects of transiting planets.</note> of Mars. In the sign <term>Kumbha</term> <note>Aquarius</note>, in the example, there are 3 dots and only 4 strokes instead of 5. This is because the 10th place from the Moon has been declared to be ineffective—that is, neither benefic nor malefic—<note>original: "चन्द्राद्दिग्विफलेषु Chandraddigviphaleshu"</note>—for the Ashtakavarga of Mars. That place has therefore not been taken for the calculation. The net evil arising there is thus only $\frac{4-3}{8}$ or 1/8th.

In the case of a planet with more benefic dots, the benefic influence will be all the greater and more marked if the planet concerned should also be posited in an <term>Upachaya</term> <note>Literally "increasing" houses; these are the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses from the Ascendant or Moon, where planets tend to grow stronger over time.</note> house (with respect to the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note> or the Moon), or occupy a friend's house, or his own sign, or his exaltation <term>Rasi</term> <note>Zodiac sign.</note>.

->If a planet is situated in an Upachaya house from the Lagna, or stays in its own house or a benefic sign, or enters a friend’s sign, it shall bestow exceedingly good results.<-
-><note>original: "लग्नादुपचयसंस्थश्चेद्ग्रहो स्वगृहशुभराशस्थः । मित्रक्षेत्रगतो वा फलमतिशयितः शुभं दद्यात् ॥ (मेषरीतिः)"</note><-

If, on the other hand, the planet, though getting more benefic dots, should happen to be in an <term>Apachaya</term> <note>Houses that are not Upachaya; generally considered to have less immediate strength for material growth.</note> house (reckoned either from the Lagna or the Moon), or in his depression sign <note>The sign where a planet is at its weakest; its "fall."</note> or inimical sign, the good effect will be considerably reduced. If in the above case there be more malefic strokes instead of benefic dots, it will worsen the bad effect.

->One who at the time of birth is in an Apachaya house, in its sign of depression, or in an enemy’s sign, shall give but little fruit, even when the time for its result arrives.<-
-><note>original: "अपचयराशौ नीचे शत्रुक्षेत्रे च जन्मकाले स्यात् । यस्तु स दद्याल्पं फलमतिशयितौ यथाकालम् ॥"</note><-

**N. B.**—The <term>Upachaya</term> or <term>Apachaya</term> places have to be reckoned with respect to the Lagna or the Moon at the time of birth and not by the position of the planets at the time of <term>Gochara</term> <note>The current movement or "transit" of planets across the sky.</note>.

Compare <term>Yavaneshwara</term> <note>An ancient authority in Indian astrology, often associated with the Indo-Greek tradition.</note>:
->Even if a planet has overcome its misfortunes, if it is weakened or broken by being in its sign of depression or an enemy's house at birth, even if the lord of that birth is strong, its fruit when transiting over its birth position is declared to be very little.<-
-><note>original: "यस्तु स्वनीचारिगृहोपभोगैर्जितारिष्टोऽप्यतनुर्विदीर्णः । सूतावभुज्जन्मपतौ बलस्थे स जन्मगो स्वल्पफलो निरुक्तः ॥"</note><-

<summary>This page explains how to calculate the net benefic or malefic effects of Mars in transit using the Ashtakavarga system, emphasizing that a planet's strength at birth (natal position) significantly modifies its transit results.</summary>
<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Mars, Mesha, Kumbha, Upachaya, Apachaya, Lagna, Gochara, Yavaneshwara, planetary strength</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on how to interpret planetary strength. While the previous page focused on the mathematical calculation of "dots" in the Ashtakavarga system, this page transitions into how the planet's condition at the actual moment of birth (the natal chart) dictates how much power it has during its current movements through the sky (transits).</meta>

<page-num>660</page-num>

# Jataka Parijata: Chapter X

> If a planet is in its sign of exaltation, its own sign, or a friendly sign, and is located in an "increase" house <term>Upachaya</term> <note>Houses 3, 6, 10, and 11, which signify growth and improvement over time.</note> relative to the birth sign, it is considered very powerful. If a planet was auspicious in the birth chart, it will grant good fortune when it occupies that birth position; otherwise, even a naturally good planet can cause trouble. <note>original: "ईप्सुमुहृत्स्वोच्चभृदिष्टगे मित्रर्क्षजन्मोपचये बलीयान् । यो जातकेऽभूरस तु जन्मसंस्थो दद्याच्छुभं न स्वशुभोऽप्यनिष्टम् ॥"</note>

Also Satyacharya <note>A celebrated ancient authority on astrology often cited for his scientific approach.</note>:
> In the birth chart, if even a single planet is in a house of growth, it yields robust results. However, a planet in a house of growth that is specifically in the sixth house leads to loss. <note>original: "जन्मन्युपचयभवने ह्येको ग्रहो ह्युपचयेषु पुष्टफलः । उपचयभवनोपेतः पाष्ठस्थाने ह्यपचयाय ॥"</note>

These authors have in their turn specifically stated, by the words <term>Janmakale</term> <note>At the time of birth.</note>, <term>Sutau</term> <note>In the birth/offspring.</note>, and <term>Janmani</term> <note>At the birth-position.</note>, that good and bad places have to be determined with respect to the positions of planets at birth-time only. But at the time of producing the effects, all planets excepting the Moon will cause their full good or bad effect to come to pass provided they are strong and not otherwise. In the case of the Moon, even if she is benefic (i.e., placed in places such as a house of growth <term>Upachaya</term>, etc., and the zodiac sign <term>Rasi</term> containing the Moon happening to be associated with benefic dots) if she does not possess strength, she produces only evil effects; for Devakirti <note>An ancient astrological commentator.</note> says —

> The Moon yields auspicious results when strong; but if it lacks strength, the Moon, like any other planet, becomes a source of distress. <note>original: "शुभमुडुपः शुभफलं दद्यात्सबलो बलेन हीनस्तु । ग्रह इव सर्वश्चन्द्रः कष्टफलो बलविहीनश्च ॥"</note>

Varahamihira <note>The most famous 6th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer.</note> too has said in his work <term>Yatra</term> <note>A text specifically dealing with expeditions and journeys.</note> about the inability to produce effects on the part of planets devoid of strength owing to their being in depression <note>debilitated</note>, defeat in planetary war <note>A calculation where two planets occupy the same degree, and one "wins" based on latitude.</note>, or combustion <note>Being too close to the Sun, thus losing visibility and power.</note>, etc.

> Planets that are in their sign of depression, defeated in planetary war, or eclipsed by the Sun's brilliance lose their power and become like snakes rendered helpless by medicine—incapable of producing results in the Ascendant. <note>original: "नीचस्था ग्रहविजिता रव्याभिभूता विरश्मयो ह्रस्वाः । भुजगा इव भेषहता भवंति कार्याक्षमा लग्ने ॥"</note>

Thus it will be seen that whatever effects—good or bad—have been ascribed owing to the planet being placed in a sign of exaltation, its own sign <term>Swakshetra</term>, a house of growth <term>Upachaya</term>, etc., it always refers to the position of the planet at the time of birth; and whatever effects, good or bad, that have been ascribed to the planet itself generally without reference in any way to the particular place of occupation should be construed as indicating the result of the planet's transit to the concerned place.

It may be mentioned here that in several almanacs <term>Panchangams</term> the benefic planetary transit <term>Gochara</term> results of planets are mentioned; i.e., if—

<summary>This page establishes that a planet's power to provide good results during its transit is entirely dependent on its strength in the original birth chart. It cites authorities like Satyacharya and Varahamihira to explain that weak, debilitated, or "eclipsed" planets are as ineffective as incapacitated snakes.</summary>
<keywords>Janmakale, Upachaya, Varahamihira, Gochara, planetary strength, natal chart, transits, Devakirti, Satyacharya</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion on the Ashtakavarga system, specifically moving from theoretical calculations to practical application in predictive astrology. It explains how a planet’s current movement through the sky (transits) is filtered through the strength it was assigned at the moment of birth (the natal chart).</meta>

<page-num>667</page-num>

<header>Chapter Ten 667</header>

...particular planets in their orbit in the zodiac pass through particular places from the Moon, they become auspicious and so on. It will be seen that these are simply the benefic positions of that particular planet with respect to the Moon in that planet's <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A Sanskrit system (meaning "eight-fold categorization") that uses a point-based system to determine the strength of planets based on their relationship to eight key points in a horoscope.</note> and do not completely represent the entire benefic results of that planet.

The <term>Ashtakavargas</term> are used in connection with transits <note>The current positions and movements of planets in the sky compared to their positions at birth.</note>. For example, Saturn transitting the 3rd, 6th, and 11th places from the Moon is good, provided the benefic dots in those places are above 4. Suppose he <note>Astrological texts traditionally refer to planets using masculine pronouns, reflecting the personified deities associated with them.</note> is transitting the 3rd house from the Moon in a nativity where the benefic dots are below 4. He will not be very good. His coming in the 3rd house is no doubt good; but much cannot be expected of him because of the small number of benefic dots <note>These "dots," or *Bindus*, act like a battery charge; even if a planet is in a "good" room, it cannot do much work if its battery is low.</note>. The position in the 3rd house though good, the small number of benefic dots therein disqualifies him to do good.

It is a fact indisputable that planets wield a certain amount of influence, whether for good or evil, during their transits at the time of consideration in accordance with the number of benefic dots which the planets gain to their credit in their respective <term>Ashtakavargas</term> based on the position each of the planets occupy at the time of birth.

For example, let us consider the influence which the planets may produce on the sample horoscope given under, say, about the first week of December 1932.

<summary>This page explains that a planet's favorable house position during a transit is only effective if it possesses a high number of benefic points (dots) in the Ashtakavarga system. <header>Jataka Parijata Adh. X.</header>

It will be seen in the above that the Sun is occupying <term>Scorpio</term> <note>original: Vrischika</note> and the Sun's <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system that assigns points to planets based on their positions; "Ashta" means eight and "Varga" means division.</note> figure in Scorpio is 5. Mars is in <term>Leo</term> <note>original: Simha</note> and the number of benefic dots in Mars's <note>original: Kujas</note> Ashtakavarga is 6. Mercury <note>original: Budha</note> is posited in Scorpio and the number of benefic dots under Mercury's Ashtakavarga is 5. Jupiter is in Leo and the number of benefic points in Jupiter's Ashtakavarga is 3. The planet Venus occupies <term>Libra</term> <note>original: Tula</note> and the number of benefic dots there in the Ashtakavarga of Venus is 6. Saturn <note>original: Sani</note> is in <term>Capricorn</term> <note>original: Makara</note>; and in Saturn's Ashtakavarga, the number of benefic dots in Capricorn is 4. Thus, the sum total of benefic dots for the said week for all the six planets taken together comes to 29.

Now 48 benefic dots are the maximum fixed to pass a <term>nativity</term> <note>A person's birth chart.</note> for good in all respects. It may however be safely predicted that the native will be free from worry, loss, etc., and his situation will only be of a normal nature if the sum total of the benefic dots of all the six planets taken together comes to 24. In this specimen horoscope, as the number of benefic dots comes to 29, it can safely be said that the native will be free from anxiety and worry in the said period. The less the number of benefic dots, the worse will be the effects to be experienced by the native.

Bhattotpala <note>A famous 10th-century commentator on Indian astrological texts.</note> adds:
Varahamihira <note>The influential 6th-century astronomer and astrologer.</note> has stated (in the *Brihat Jataka*, Chapter VIII, Verse 23) that when two similar effects but contrary in nature are produced by one and the same planet (owing to its ownership of two houses, or ownership of one house and occupation of another house), the result will be nil when the two effects referred to are equal in degree and that it will partake of the nature of the preponderating effect in case they are not of equal degree.

->When a single planet produces two results of a similar nature but opposing influence, they cancel each other out; if one is greater, that effect is what will come to fruition. <note>original: *Ekagrahasya sadrshe phalayo-virodhe nasham vadedyadhikaṃ paripacyate tat*</note><-

A question may now be asked what the author's object was by making a similar declaration again through the words: "Thus is the destiny declared; other interpretations in horoscopy are not desired, for they are fruitless and do not lead to actual results." <note>original: *iti nigaditadiṣṭaṃ neṣṭamanyannahorāḥ viphalamavipāka janayamādatta dyuḥ*</note> The answer is—
"No, it is no repetition at all. What was stated before without the aid of the Ashtakavarga Table relates to the cancellation of two similar effects but of a contrary character. For example, a planet in one capacity may be a giver of wealth; the same planet...

<summary>This page provides a practical calculation of Ashtakavarga points (benefic dots) to predict a person's well-being for a specific week, explaining that a score of 24 is average and 29 is positive. It also discusses a rule where a planet’s conflicting influences can cancel each other out unless one influence is significantly stronger.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the Ashtakavarga system, moving from the calculation of points to the philosophical and practical rules of how conflicting planetary influences are resolved. It specifically addresses the reconciliation of "benefic" (good) and "malefic" (bad) effects and explains the relationship between the Ashtakavarga system and the more common transit system known as Gochara.</meta>

<page-num>689</page-num>
# Chapter Ten 689

...in another capacity may cause loss of wealth. In such a case, both the effects are similar in character and become neutralized, with the consequence that the native neither acquires wealth nor incurs any losses. But if the planet's capacity to give wealth be in any way stronger for more reasons than one, the same will of course predominate over the opposite current (of the other effect) and the result will be an influx of some money. 

In the present verse, it has been stated that there is cancellation in the case of good and bad effects even if they are not similar in nature. This view is further supported by <term>Badarayana</term> <note>An ancient sage and authority in Indian philosophy and astrology.</note> and <term>Yavana</term> <note>Literally "Greek" or "Ionian," referring to foreign astrological traditions that influenced early Indian practices.</note> as they have admitted this principle while speaking about <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>A system of "eightfold divisions" used to predict the strength of planets based on their positions relative to eight points of reference.</note> and their several effects. 

Thus, for example, a certain planet may be the giver of gold on account of a certain reason. The same planet may, on account of some other reason, cause loss in silver. Although the effects in these two cases are not similar (because one involves gold and the other silver), merely on account of the effects being opposite in nature (one giving and the other taking away), the result is neither benefic nor malefic. After such considerations of each <term>Rasi</term> <note>A zodiac sign.</note> on account of its eight countings, if it is found that benefic points outnumber the malefic, the effect of the planet on that zodiac sign should be declared as benefic to the extent of the excess benefic dots as already stated.

Now the following question may be asked: Well, if the Eightfold Division system (<term>Ashtakavarga</term>) then is so important for ascertaining the nature of the effects, what was the necessity for the sage <term>Varahamihira</term> <note>A celebrated 6th-century Indian astronomer and astrologer.</note> treating separately in his *Great Compilation* (<term>Brihat Samhita</term>) the results of transits (<term>Gocharaphala</term>) of planets with respect to the Moon's place at birth? 

The answer is, the intention of Varahamihira is that such of the effects which have not been stated in connection with the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> should be stated in the <term>Gochara</term> <note>The study of planetary transits; how planets moving through the sky today affect a person based on their birth chart.</note>. Further, as the transit system (<term>Gochara</term>) is widely known to all, and as it has been treated by many authors in their works, it has to be inferred that Varahamihira too has adopted the same course followed by his predecessors. For he has said in his work *The Royal Journey* (<term>Yatra</term>):

->For one who considers the evidence of transit effects, is the effect of obstruction desired or not? Generally, this is not a universally accepted stance, for transits provide results based on a broad or approximate method.<-
<note>original: "यस्य गोचरफलप्रमाणता तस्य वेधफलमिष्यते न वा । प्रायशो न बहुसम्मतं स्थितं स्थूलमार्गफलदो हि गोचरः ॥" (yasya gocaraphalapramāṇatā tasya vedhaphalamiṣyate na vā | prāyaśo na bahusammataṃ sthitaṃ sthūlamārgaphalado hi gocaraḥ ||)</note>

# <term>Jataka Parijata</term> <note>A major 15th-century Sanskrit treatise on Indian astrology.</note> Adhyaya X.

<term>Yavaneswara</term> <note>original: "Yavaneswara"; literally "Lord of the Greeks," a title referring to ancient authorities of the Yavana school of Indian astrology, which blended Greek and Indian methods.</note> too, after describing the effects separately, has accepted this <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system of "eightfold divisions" used to calculate the strength and auspiciousness of planets based on their positions relative to eight points of reference.</note> system alone as of primary importance; for he has said:

> When observing the auspicious and inauspicious results in the eightfold division of fruits, if they are equal, it is desired that the two results be considered neutralized. Having considered the remaining fruit, one should then judge the outcome for the undertaking of journeys and for general prosperity. <note>original: "फलाष्टवर्गे शुभपापलक्ष्ये समानास्तुल्यफलौ मदिष्टौ । जवास्तु यस्तस्य फलं विचार्य यात्राविधाने च समुन्नवे च ॥"</note>

<term>Badarayana</term> <note>An ancient sage and philosopher often cited as an authority in astrological and Vedantic literature.</note> also has said:

> If the results in the eight rows are found to be equal, the destruction of those fruits should be declared. The row that contains the surplus should be proclaimed as the result; this method of calculation is prescribed for every position. <note>original: "अष्टश्रेणी तुल्यसंस्थे फले चेत् स्यातां नाश: फल्योस्तत्र वाच्य: । वाच्या पंक्तिस्त्वतिरिक्तमयो: स्यात् स्थाने स्थाने कल्पनेयं प्रदिष्टा ॥"</note>

and has thus accepted only the Ashtakavarga method.

---

### THE ASHTAKAVARGA PLATE.

The process of finding the benefic dots in the Ashtakavargas of the several planets in accordance with the method given in pages 650-652 *supra* <note>Latin: "above."</note> involves heavy labour and considerable time especially when it has to be done for several horoscopes. Some methods of simplifying this work have been suggested in the past, but a new contrivance has now been devised which has several merits. It not only does away with the patient labor involved in the dull and routine process, but also shows the results in such a way that one can see at a glance how the benefic dots in any particular house have been contributed. The <term>Sarvashtakavarga</term> <note>The "Total Eightfold Division," representing the collective sum of all benefic points from all planets for each house in a birth chart.</note> figures can also be very easily read from the plates at a glance. The practical uses to which the results can be put are, as is known to astrologers, several. The more important of these uses have been explained in this <term>Adhyaya</term> <note>Chapter.</note> in their appropriate places.

The construction of the Ashtakavarga plate and the method of reading the results from the same will now be explained.

The contrivance consists of nine circular plates (discs of zinc, copper or brass), of successively larger radii placed one over the other in the order of their size, the largest being placed at the bottom and the smallest at the top. A radius of one inch

### Chapter Ten <note>original: "Dashamo'dhyayah"</note> 661

...for the smallest and three inches for the largest, the difference between the radii of successive plates being 1/4th of an inch will be found suitable. The bottom most plate has an axle with screw arrangement fixed to its centre about which the other plates revolve. By tightening the screw at the top, the plates can be fixed up in any desired position. Each plate covers the central portion of the next bigger plate, exposing to view only a circular strip or rim. The top plate is of course fully exposed.

The top plate and the narrow strips of other plates are each divided into 12 equal parts. The dividing lines are so drawn that they form a straight line when the plates are adjusted for any horoscope. The twelve divisions of the top plate are assigned to the twelve signs of the zodiac. In the visible rim of the next bigger plate are arranged the figures indicating the benefic dots <note>A point system in Indian astrology where "bindus" (dots) represent positive planetary influences.</note> counted from the Sun in the several <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>A numerical system used to assess the strength of planets relative to eight specific points in a chart.</note>. The next plate contains figures of benefic dots counted from the Moon; and the next one, from Mars; and so on in the order of the Sun, the Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, Saturn and the <term>Lagna</term> <note>The Ascendant; the specific point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>. One of the divisions of each of these plates contains a dot denoting the position of the planet to which the plate relates. Thus the dot in the largest plate indicates the position of the <term>Lagna</term>.

To adjust the plates for any horoscope, keep the bottom-most plate in position and rotate the next plate (relating to Saturn) until the division containing the dot is as many divisions removed from the dot in the <term>Lagna</term> plate as Saturn is removed from the <term>Lagna</term> in the horoscope under consideration. For example, if Saturn occupies the 8th house from the <term>Lagna</term>, move the plate relating to Saturn till its dot comes to the 8th division from the dot in the <term>Lagna</term> plate. Then hold the two plates in position firmly and proceed similarly with the third plate. Then hold the three plates in position and proceed with the fourth and so on. When the Sun's plate also has been thus fixed, rotate the topmost plate until the name of the particular <term>Rasi</term> <note>A sign of the zodiac.</note> representing the <term>Lagna</term> in the horoscope under consideration is brought directly above the division containing the dot in the <term>Lagna</term> plate. It will now be found that the divisions containing the dots in the several plates are...

The number of '3's will similarly yield the results for Mars, '4's for Mercury, '5's for Jupiter, '6's for Venus, and '7's for Saturn. # In the *Jataka Parijata*, Chapter 10 <note>original: "जातकपारिजाते Adh. X."</note>

->Having drawn the chart with the rising sign, the Sun, and the other planets,<-
->one finds the houses of the sky-rangers from the Sun to the rising sign.<-
->By applying the characters assigned to the eight-fold formulas,<-
->all the dots produced by the individual Ashtakavargas are obtained.<- (1)

->*Devo*, *ghano*, *dhigavashas*, *tamorama*, and *dhritih* are the dots for the Sun and others, in order.<-
->Calculated by these numbers, the collective dots are found;<-
->the total of all eight-fold groups is known as the "Aggregate."<- (2)

*Slokas 1 and 2* If a diagram of the zodiac <note>a birth chart or horoscope</note> with the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> <note>the sign ascending on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> and the positions of the Sun and other planets at the time of a person's birth is drawn, and if all the benefic dots <note>auspicious points</note> produced by the separate <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>an eight-fold system of calculating planetary strength</note> are set down according to the directions in the formulas, we can calculate the results. These formulas contain eight divisions for the eight "sky-rangers" <note>the seven traditional planets plus the ascending sign/Lagna</note>. Based on these, we obtain the figures 48, 49, 39, 54, 56, 52, and 39 to represent the benefic dots of the Sun and the other planets. <note>The Sanskrit words used in the verse—Devo, Ghano, etc.—are mnemonics using the Katapayadi system to represent these specific numbers.</note> The figure 337 represents the aggregate benefic dots of all the planets. This total of all the <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>original: "Sarvashtakavarga"</note> is called the <term>Samudaya</term> <note>original: "समुदाय"</note>, meaning the "Aggregate."

->In whatever sign from Aries onward the planets grant eight dots,<-
->they serve to support, strengthen, and increase the power of that house.<-
->Six, five, or seven dots together provide auspicious results,<-
->but those with three, two, or one dot are not favorable.<- (3)

*Sloka 3.* In whatever house <term>Bhava</term> <note>an astrological house representing specific life areas</note> represented by Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note> and the other signs there occur eight benefic dots given by planets, that house is supported, strengthened, and made to flourish. Combinations of six, five, or seven dots are considered lucky <note>original: "शुभ" (Shubha)</note>, while houses containing only three, two, or one dot are not considered favorable or beneficial.

<header>Verses 4–5 Chapter Ten 665</header>

...prosper. Houses containing 5, 6 and 7 dots <note>original: *bindu*</note> become beneficent. Those that have 3, 2 or 1 dot are not auspicious.

Mixed results arise when there is a union of four dots <note>original: *sāgara-bindu*, where "sāgara" (ocean) is a symbolic name for the number 4</note>. If the houses are empty, they grant disease, infamy, and fear. I shall now declare the full results arising from the separate eight-fold divisions <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>a system used to calculate the strength of planets based on their positions relative to other planets</note> of the Sun and other planets when associated with one or more dots. (4)

*Sloka* 4. When a house has 4 dots, it produces mixed effects. Those houses that have no figure in them are productive of disease, infamy and danger. I now proceed to explain the full effects of the Sun and other planets being associated with dots 1, 2, etc., as a result of the separate Ashtakavargas.

A single dot causes various kinds of disease, sorrow, fear, and wandering; while two dots lead to mental distress, censure by the king or by thieves, infamy, and the loss of food. (5)

*Sloka* 5. A planet associated with one dot produces various kinds of diseases, miseries, dangers, wanderings and similar hardships, while with two, it leads to mental anguish, condemnation by one’s king (censure by the sovereign) and deprivation of food by robbers.

->NOTES.<-

The excess of benefic over malefic dots may be 2, 4, 6 or 8 which when put in ordinary language mean 1/4 or 1/2, 3/4 or 1. <gloss>One-quarter</gloss> or, <gloss>one-half</gloss> or, <gloss>three-quarters</gloss> or, <unclear>full</unclear> or full. <note>The vertical bars represent traditional Sanskrit fractional notation where । = 1/4, ॥ = 1/2, etc.</note> Hence the following verses <note>original: *slokas*</note>:—

In the calculated position of marks and dots, where the marks exceed three:
Two marks grant wealth, riches, rising prosperity, and kinsmen.
Six marks grant great valor, fame, enjoyment, and renown.
Eight marks make one an incomparable lord of the earth with various virtues.

<header>Slokas 9-11, Chapter 10</header>

the planet brings honors such as accrue from the possession of horses and other means of transport, an army, and superior riches. When the maximum of eight dots is reached, it opens out a prospect of kingly glory graced with its seven-fold adjuncts <note>original: *svāmyamātyasuhṛtkośarāṣṭradurgabalāni*; these refer to the traditional Indian components of a kingdom: the ruler, ministers, allies, treasury, territory, forts, and military force</note>.

### NOTES.

Some books read "graced by every quality" <note>original: *sarvaguṇābhi*</note> which would mean kingly glory graced with every one of its adjuncts.

A planet in a sign with five or more dots is always auspicious in its own divisional chart <term>varga|Vedic astrology uses sub-charts called vargas to refine predictions beyond the main birth chart</term>. If it is otherwise, it yields no good effect. In its transit <note>original: *gochara*</note> through a sign which is without dots, it becomes positively hurtful and leads to fruitless labor. || 9 ||

*Sloka 9.* A planet is invariably benefic in his own <term>varga</term> in any sign which has five or more dots. If otherwise, it yields no good effect. In its range in a sign which is without dots, it becomes positively hurtful.

->Even if planets are in their exaltation signs, in a friendly sign, or other benefic places, and are possessed also of the strength which a <term>Kendra|the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, considered the 'pivots' or strongest points of a chart</term> or similar position gives,<-
->they all produce untoward effects if they happen to be associated with dots short of the required minimum. || 10 ||<-

*Sloka 10.* All planets without exception, standing, it may be, in their exaltation signs, in a friendly sign or other benefic places and possessed also of the strength which a <term>Kendra</term> or similar position gives, produce untoward effects if they happen to be associated with dots short of the required minimum.

->But planets, on the other hand, which may be in bad positions and have even reached their sign of fall, or an inimical sign, or unfavorable portions of the zodiac,<-
->are all invariably auspicious if they are associated with an abundance of dots. || 11 ||<-

*Sloka 11.* But planets on the other hand which may be in bad positions and have even reached their depression point <note>the 'sign of fall' or 'neecham', where a planet is at its weakest</note>, or an inimical sign or portions of the

<summary>This page explains that numerical strength (dots) in the Ashtakavarga system can override traditional planetary weaknesses like being in a "sign of fall" or an enemy's sign. Conversely, even exalted planets can fail to provide good results if they lack sufficient dots.</summary>

->When Saturn passes through an empty house in the Ashtakavargas of the Sun and other planets,<-
->he causes extreme disease, fear of enemies, and distress for the father and other relations. || 12 ||<-

*Sloka 12.* When Saturn <term>Shani|the planet associated with challenges, time, and karma</term> passes through an empty house (one void of <term>bindus|benefic points or "dots" used to measure a planet's strength</term>) in the <term>Ashtakavarga|a predictive system that calculates the collective influence of the eight primary points of the horoscope</term> of the Sun and other planets, he occasions on a large scale diseases, dangers from enemies, and other troubles to the father and other relations whom the several planets represent <note>In this system, each planet acts as a "karaka" or signifier: the Sun represents the father, the Moon the mother, Mars siblings, etc.</note>.

---

### || Results of the Sun ||

->When the Sun is in the rising sign in an enemy's sign or a place of depression,<-
->one born with three or two dots will be sickly.<-
->With five or more dots in the rising sign, if the Sun is<-
->in his exaltation or own house, the person is a long-lived king. || 13 ||<-

*Sloka 13.* When the Sun <note>original: *Dinakara*, literally "the day-maker"</note> is in the rising sign <term>Lagna|the zodiac sign that was ascending on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term> and in that part of it which either belongs to an enemy or forms his <term>depression point|the specific degree where a planet is at its weakest; also called debilitation</term>, and when he happens to be associated with 3 or 2 dots, the person born is sickly. But if the same Sun should be in the rising sign in which 5 or more dots appear and which happens to be his <term>exaltation|the sign where a planet expresses its highest, strongest energy</term> or his own house <note>the sign of Leo</note>, the person born becomes a king and is endowed with long life.

---

->NOTES.<-

Parasara <note>a legendary sage and one of the founding fathers of Vedic astrology</note> adds:
->After placing the Sun's Ashtakavarga in the twelve signs,<-
->the ninth sign from the Sun's position is known as the father's house.<-
->Multiply the dots in that sign by the reduced total.<-
->Divide by twenty-seven; the remainder is the star Saturn will transit.<-
->At that time, one should predict distress for that specific house.<-

<header>Sl. 13 Chapter Ten 669</header>

---

At that time, there is no doubt that the father will experience distress <note>original: *pitṛ-kleśa*</note>. || 13 (cont.) ||

->If Saturn transits the trine signs <note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th signs from a specific point</note> relative to that house,<-
->one should recognize the death of the father or a father-figure.<-
->This should be calculated during the "vulnerable gaps"<-
->of the planetary periods. || 14 ||<-

*Sloka 14.* When Saturn transits through the trines of the house or sign mentioned, or during the <term>Dasha-chidra|the final, often unstable portion of a major planetary period</term>, the death of the father or a paternal relative is likely to occur.

If Rahu, Saturn <note>original: *Manda*, "the slow-moving one"</note>, or Mars <note>original: *Bhuminandana*, "son of the earth"</note> are in the 4th house from the Sun, and are not aspected by Jupiter or Venus, the person becomes a cause of the father's end <note>original: *pitṛ-hā*, literally "father-killer," though astrologically this implies a birth that coincides with the father's decline</note>. || 15 ||

If Saturn <note>original: *Sūryasuta*, "the son of the Sun"</note> is in the 4th house from the Ascendant or the Moon, and is aspected by or joined with malefic planets, then the destruction of the parents occurs at that time. || 16 ||

The expert in the knowledge of time <note>an astrologer</note> should correlate this with the favorable planetary periods. The loss of the father <note>original: *pitṛ-kṣaya*</note> occurs during the period of the lord of the 4th house from the Ascendant. || 17 ||

During the period of the lord of the 4th house <note>original: *sukheśa*, "lord of happiness," here referring to the 4th house of the home and parents</note>, there are many gains; of this, there is no doubt. If one is born in the father’s birth-star <note>original: *janma-rkṣe*, the lunar mansion where the father's moon was placed at birth</note> or if that star's lord is in the Ascendant, || 18 ||

Then that person will surely perform the funeral rites for the father. If the lord of the 4th house is in the 11th house, especially from the Moon sign, || 19 ||

And is associated with the house of the father, the person born will be obedient to the father. He alone will complete the remaining duties and ancestral work <note>original: *karma-śeṣa*</note> of the father. || 20 ||

Born in the 3rd sign from the father's birth-star, one is dependent on the father’s wealth. Born in the house of the father's profession, one is endowed with qualities equal to the father. || 21 ||

Even if the lord of that house is in the Ascendant, the son brings welfare to the father. Regarding the "empty" spaces <note>signs with zero dots or benefic points</note> in the Sun's <term>Ashtakavarga|a system of eight-fold strength calculation</term>, during those months or years: || 22 ||

One should always avoid marriage, business transactions, and the like in such a month. Quarrels and monthly sorrows occur during these "empty" months. || 23 ||

Having known these results, one should act accordingly each month. One should take the total points <note>original: *pinda*</note> of the Sun and multiply/reduce them by the measurement of the 8th house. || 24 ||

Divide the remainder by twelve and count from Aries. In that month, or in its trine months, one should expect the death of the father. || 25 ||

One should calculate starting from the Sun; when the Sun transits that point, death occurs. || 26 ||

->When the Sun <note>original: *Dineśa*, "Lord of the Day"</note> occupies a cardinal <note>original: *Kendra*; houses 1, 4, 7, or 10</note> or trine <note>original: *Trikona*; houses 1, 5, or 9</note> position,<-
->and is associated with 6, 5, 7, or 8 benefic points in its point-chart,<-
->then in the 22nd, 35th, 30th, or 36th year, the person born or their father will face death. || 14 ||<-

*Sloka 14.* When the Sun, occupying a cardinal or trine position, is associated there with 6, 5, 7, or 8 benefic dots <note>points contributed by other planets in the *Ashtakavarga* system</note>, the person born or his father will have his death in his 22nd, 35th, 30th, or 36th year respectively.

->If the Sun is in a cardinal house with the Moon, Saturn, and Mercury,<-
->possessing only 2 net benefic points after the final reductions,<-
->the wise say the father’s administrative glory and prosperity<-
->will flourish abundantly after the child’s first ten years. || 15 ||<-

*Sloka 15.* If, at a person's birth, the Sun occupying a cardinal position in conjunction with the Moon, Saturn, and Mercury should have in that position 2 net benefic dots after the necessary reductions are made (<term>Trikonasodhana|The reduction of points shared by signs of the same element</term> & <term>Eakadipatyasodhana|The reduction of points between two signs ruled by the same planet</term>), the father of the person, say the wise astrologers, will have a lease of abundant administrative power <note>original: *rājya-śriyam*, royal or administrative splendor</note> setting in after the completion of the first ten years of the person born.

### || Results of the Moon <note>original: *Chandra-phalam*</note> ||

->If a house in the eight-fold system of the Sun or Moon is empty of points,<-
->the corresponding month and sign should be avoided for all auspicious deeds.<-
->If the Moon is in the Ascendant with 1, 2, or 3 points,<-
->death occurs in the 37th year if joined by two or three other planets. || 16 ||<-

*Sloka 16.* If, in the <term>Ashtakavarga|A system of calculating planetary strength through points</term>—whether of the Sun or of the Moon—there be a house void of benefic dots, the solar month corresponding to that sign and the zodiac sign itself are to be avoided for the purpose of every auspicious undertaking in the person's life. If the Moon occupying the Ascendant <note>original: *Lagna*</note> be associated with 1, 2, or 3

...benefic dots, the person born will suffer from disease and debility. If the Moon in the above position be also in conjunction with 2 or 3 planets, the person will meet with his death in his 37th year.

->NOTES<-

Parasara <note>A legendary sage and the father of Vedic astrology</note> adds:
> From the Moon, if a planet occupies the fourth house, one should first consider the mother's residence. <note>original: *candrāccaturthage mātuḥ prāsādamagracintanam*</note> When the Moon’s point-chart <note>original: *candrāṣṭavargaṃ*, the Moon's Ashtakavarga</note> is empty or when the Moon enters a sign with zero points, one should avoid that constellation for performing any auspicious deeds. <note>The Moon's transit through signs where it contributed no points is considered highly inauspicious.</note> Especially concerning the three constellations belonging to the lord of the eighth house from the Moon, one will undoubtedly experience fatigue, losses, and sorrows. One should calculate the total sum <note>term: *Piṇḍa*|A specialized sum of points used for calculating the timing of life events</note> of the Moon's "house of happiness" <note>the 4th house</note> as previously described. When Saturn transits through the sign represented by the remainder of that sum, one should predict the loss of the mother. Alternatively, some say this occurs when Saturn transits the trine positions <note>houses 1, 5, or 9 relative to that sign</note> or during vulnerable periods <note>term: *daśā-chidreṣu*|The transitional "gap" between two major planetary cycles, often considered unstable</note>. If Mars or the Sun is in the place of the mother <note>the 4th house</note> from either the Moon or the Ascendant, or if they aspect <note>glance at with planetary influence</note> that place, the loss occurs at the time previously mentioned. In the absence of this, the person may face their own death or exile to another country. When the lord of the house of happiness from the Moon is in a trine to the Sun, one should predict separation from the mother; such is the opinion of the father <note>referring to the sage Parasara</note>. In matters regarding the mother's welfare, one should calculate using the Sun and other planets just as one does for the father's welfare.

**When the Moon, occupying a cardinal house, a trine, or the 11th house, is in its sign of debilitation or an enemy's sign and is waning, and when the benefic dots associated with the house occupied by the Moon are only 2 or 3, those who know the subject say the significance of that house is destroyed. 17** <note>original Sanskrit: *keṇdratrikoṇāyagate śaśāṅke nīcārige vṛddhikalāvihīne | bindudvike vā yadi sa tribindau tadbhāvanāśaṃ kathayanti tajjñāḥ 17*</note>

*Sloka 17.* When the Moon occupying a Kendra <note>cardinal houses: 1, 4, 7, or 10</note>, Trikona <note>trine houses: 1, 5, or 9</note> or the 11th bhava <note>house of gains</note> is also in its depression <note>sign of weakness</note> or inimical sign and is not waxing and when the benefic dots associated with the bhava occupied by the Moon happen to be 2 or 3, the bhava in question, say the astrologers acquainted with the subject, is extinct.

<header>From the Jātaka Pārijāta, Chapter X</header>

->If the strong Moon is placed in a trine, a quadrant, or the eleventh house,<-
->and is accompanied by four or more points, there is growth in the prosperity of that house.<-
->If the Moon is in a quadrant with eight points, those born under it<-
->become powerful kings, eminent in learning, fame, wealth, and strength. (18)<-

*Verse 18.* If the Moon, possessed of strength, is in a <term>Trikona|The "trine" houses (1st, 5th, and 9th) which are considered highly auspicious</term>, <term>Kendra|The "quadrant" or "angular" houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th) which represent the pillars of a person's life</term>, or the 11th <term>Bhava|An astrological "house" representing specific life areas</term>, and if the number of benefic dots <note>These are points in the Ashtakavarga system used to measure planetary strength</note> in the house in question is four or more, that house becomes advanced. If the Moon in a quadrant position becomes associated with eight benefic dots, the persons born under this <term>Yoga|A specific planetary alignment or combination</term> will be eminent for their learning, fame, wealth, and strength, and will become masters of men.

## || The Effects of Mars ||

->If Mars is in its exaltation or its own sign, occupying the ninth, fourth,<-
->first, or tenth house with eight points, one becomes the master of tens of millions.<-
->If Sagittarius, Aries, Leo, Capricorn, or Scorpio are the rising signs,<-
->and Mars occupies them with four points, the person born will be a king. (19)<-

*Verse 19.* When Mars is in its exaltation <note>The sign where a planet performs at its peak; for Mars, this is Capricorn</note> or in its <term>Swakshetra|A planet’s "own house" or home sign where it feels comfortable and strong</term> and occupies the 9th, 4th, 1st, or 10th house, and is associated with eight benefic dots, the person born will be a lord of wealth to be counted by the millions. If Sagittarius, Aries, Leo, Capricorn, or Scorpio is the rising sign, and Mars occupies it and is associated with four benefic dots, the person born will be a king.

->NOTES<-

The sage Parasara <note>A legendary founding father of Indian astrology</note> adds:
> After considering the point-chart of Mars <note>original: *bhaumāṣṭavargaṃ*</note>, one should determine matters regarding brothers, prowess, and courage. The sign where Mars is located is regarded as the "house of the brother" <note>original: *sahajo rāśirbhrātṛgṛhaṃ*</note>.

<page-num>678</page-num>
<header>Verse 20. Tenth Chapter. 678</header>

->Having performed the reduction of the trines <note>Trikona-shodhana: A specialized calculation in the Ashtakavarga system where points in houses of the same astrological element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water) are compared and reduced to determine final strength</note>, wherever the dots are most numerous,<-
->one should predict the acquisition of land, a wife, wealth, and a home.<-
->In the opposite case, where the points are few, one should instead predict injury or loss through weapons.<-
->Having performed the reduction for common lordship <note>Ekadhipatya-shodhana: A secondary reduction based on pairs of zodiac signs ruled by the same planet</note>, if no points remain,<-
->there will be the destruction of land and property; even a prince may meet his end.<-
->Wherever the points are abundant after all reductions have been made,<-
->if Mars is otherwise weak in strength, the person will have brothers who live a long life.<-
->Where the points are diminished, those specific lands are said to be lost.<-
->One should increase the result by the number associated with the sign and reduce as before.<-
->When Saturn transits the sign indicated by the remainder, the loss of a brother should be predicted.<-

<margin>Also, calculate the distance from Mars to the lord of the Ascendant; in the year of life corresponding to that number, there is constant suffering and fear arising from weapons and fire. When the combined points of Saturn and Mars are greater than the points of Mercury, there is fear of weapons. If the combined points of Saturn and Mars equal the points of the Sun, Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, associated with sudden upsets</note> causes a loss of happiness.</margin>

->If Mars possesses eight points in the system of eight-fold points, one is very greedy for wealth;<-
->if this occurs in the tenth house or the Ascendant, he becomes a ruler of men.<-
->If born into a royal lineage, he becomes the master of the entire country;<-
->if Mars is also in its sign of exaltation or its own sign, he becomes an emperor. (20)<-

*Verse 20.* If Mars be associated with 8 benefic dots <note>original: "rekhas" or "bindus"</note> the person born will become a petty prince; if the above be in the 10th or in the 1st <term>Bhava|An astrological "house" or house-cusp representing a specific area of life</term>, the person born will be a king. If he be already a scion of a royal family, he will become lord of the whole country. If Mars be in exaltation <note>The specific zodiac sign where a planet’s influence is most powerful; for Mars, this is Capricorn</note> or in <term>Swakshetra|The "own sign" or home territory of a planet; for Mars, these are Aries and Scorpio</term> in addition to the above, the person born will be an emperor.
