## ॥ The Results of Mercury ॥

When <term>Mercury</term> <note>Budha</note> is located in an angular house <note>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> or a trine <note>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note> and is associated with eight benefic dots <note>the maximum possible points in this system</note>, the person born will be pre-eminent in the learning peculiar to their social standing and will enjoy great pleasures. If Mercury is in its sign of exaltation but is associated with only one, two, or three benefic dots, the <term>Bhava</term> <note>the specific house or area of life influence</note> occupied by the planet is actually strengthened rather than harmed. — Verse 21.

->NOTES.<-

<margin>See the teachings of *Parasara* <note>An ancient sage and foundational figure in Vedic astrology</note></margin>
> <note>original: "बुधात्तर्थं कुटुम्बं च धनपुत्रादिमातुलाः । तत्पञ्चमे मन्त्रविद्यालिपिसुद्ध्यादि चिन्तयेत् ॥"</note>
> From the position of Mercury, one should consider wealth, family, riches, children, and maternal uncles. In the fifth house from Mercury, one should contemplate secret knowledge <note>Mantras</note>, learning, and the mastery of writing.
> <note>original: "बुधाष्टवर्गं संशोध्य शेषराशिगते शनौ । बन्धुमित्रविनाशादील्लभते नात्र संशयः ॥"</note>
> Having refined the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>the "eight-fold" system of calculating planetary strength</note> of Mercury, when <term>Saturn</term> <note>Shani</note> transits the sign indicated by the remainder, the person will experience the loss of kin and friends; of this, there is no doubt.

Identify the house that contains the highest number of benefic dots in the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> of Mercury. In the month corresponding to the sun's transit through that house, the commencement of any serious study will lead to the mastery of every branch of learning. Note the house which is empty of benefic dots in Mercury’s system; when <term>Saturn</term> passes through that house in its orbit, a <term>Bandhu</term> <note>a close relative or friend</note> or a <term>Jnati</term> <note>a paternal kinsman</note> will pass away, and some advantage enjoyed until then will be lost. — Verse 22.

<header>Sloka 23, Chapter X</header>

## ॥ The Results of Jupiter ॥
<note>original: "Guruphalam"</note>

Identify the zodiac sign that possesses the highest number of benefic dots in the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>an "eight-fold" astrological system of calculating planetary strength</note> of <term>Jupiter</term> <note>Guru</note>. When the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the rising sign or ascendant</note> corresponds to this particular sign, <term>conception</term> <note>Nisheka</note> will prove successful and result in the birth of an offspring. Furthermore, cattle, wealth, and vehicles located in the direction associated with that specific sign will begin to multiply and prosper. — Verse 23.

->NOTES.<-

The sage *Parasara* <note>a foundational authority in Indian astrology</note> adds:
> <note>original: "जीवात्पञ्चमतो ज्ञानं पुत्रधर्मधनादिकम् ।"</note>
> From the position of Jupiter, one should understand matters regarding knowledge, children, duty <note>Dharma</note>, and wealth.
> <note>original: "गुरोरष्टवर्गेषु सन्तानमपि कल्पयेत् ॥"</note>
> One should also determine the prospects of progeny through the Ashtakavarga of Jupiter.
> <note>original: "गुरुस्थितसुतस्थाने यावच्च विद्यते फलम् ।"</note>
> Whatever benefic result exists in the house of the son <note>the 5th house</note> relative to where Jupiter is placed...

*Balabhadra* <note>a later commentator and scholar</note> suggests a variation in the reading, using "in even signs" <note>original: "yugmasthane"</note> instead of "in the place of qualities" <note>original: "gunasthane"</note>.
> <note>original: "शत्रुनीचग्रहं त्यक्त्वा तावन्तश्च सुताः स्मृताः ॥"</note>
> Excluding the houses of enemies or planets in their state of debilitation, the number of children corresponds to the remaining benefic dots.

Also from *Balabhadra*:
> <note>original: "गुरुस्तुङ्गयुतस्थाने यदि स्यात्त्रिगुणं तदा ।"</note>
> If Jupiter is located in its sign of exaltation <note>its position of highest strength</note>, then the result is tripled.
> <note>original: "स्वर्क्षममूलत्रिकोणे वा यदि स्याद्विगुणं तदा ॥"</note>
> If it is in its own sign or in its <term>Mulatrikona</term> <note>a specific favorable area of a zodiac sign</note>, the result is doubled.
> <note>original: "शुभदृष्टे च तत्रैव वृद्धिः स्यात्कथितापि ।"</note>
> If that position is also aspected <note>viewed</note> by a benefic planet, further growth is predicted.
> <note>original: "यावन्तोऽंशभागाश्च तावन्तः पुरुषा मताः ॥"</note>
> As many as there are male portions <note>Amshas</note>, that is the number of male children expected.
> <note>original: "यावन्तो युग्मभागाश्च तावन्त्यश्च कन्यकाः ।"</note>
> As many as there are even-numbered portions, so many will be the daughters.
> <note>original: "गुरोरष्टवर्गेषु सुतराशौ त्रिकं फलम् ॥"</note>
> In the Ashtakavarga of Jupiter, three points in the house of children...
> <note>original: "यस्याल्पतनयः स स्याद्देशा<unclear>उ</unclear> प्रमाणतः ।"</note>
> ...for one who has few children, it should be judged by the evidence <unclear>of the region</unclear>.
> <note>original: "संख्यांशतुल्या वा न हीनफला पुनः ।"</note>
> The number is equal to the portions, and will not be of lesser result.
> <note>original: "सुतभेषांशदेशाश्च समानानि विचिन्तयेत् ॥"</note>
> One should consider the house of children, the portions, and the region as being of equal importance.
> <note>original: "गुरोरष्टवर्गेषु शोध्य शेषफलानि च ।"</note>
> After refining the Ashtakavarga of Jupiter, one should analyze the remaining results.

<summary>This page details how Jupiter's planetary strength in the Ashtakavarga system determines the success of conception and the number of offspring, while also influencing the growth of family wealth and assets.</summary>

<header>Jataka Parijata Adh. X.</header>

<warning>The image is slightly tilted and the bottom text is cut off mid-sentence.</warning>

Excluding the results of planets associated with cruel influences <note>original: "krura-ashrita"</note>, the remaining planets are said to represent one's own children. When malefic planets are situated in the twelfth, eleventh, and fifth houses, the lineage may be diminished. In the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>an "eight-fold" system of planetary strength</note> of Jupiter, if the points in the house of the son are equal, one is known to have few children, even if Jupiter itself is in the fifth house. However, if the lord of that house is in conjunction with or aspects it, then one should predict the birth of sons. A master of time <note>an expert astrologer</note> should determine this through these many methods. When many such signs coincide, then one should make a definitive prediction.

---

->When the Sun enters a house that has received the fewest<-
->benefic dots in the Ashtakavarga of Jupiter,<-
->all auspicious deeds performed are destroyed.<-
->If Jupiter, having five or more dots, enters the houses of<-
->enemies, loss, or death, the one born becomes long-lived,<-
->exceedingly wealthy, and a conqueror of foes. ॥ 24 ॥<-

> ***Verse 24.*** If, at a person's birth, the Sun occupies a house which in the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> of Jupiter gets the least number of benefic dots, the person born will be luckless in that every undertaking for his benefit will fail. If Jupiter occupying the 6th <note>original: "ripu"</note>, the 12th <note>original: "vyaya"</note> or the 8th <note>original: "randhra"</note> <term>bhava</term> <note>astrological house</note> be associated with 5 or more benefic dots, the person born will be long-lived, very opulent and victorious over his enemies.

---

->If Jupiter is in its sign of exaltation or its own home with eight dots,<-
->positioned in a central house or the house of the teacher,<-
->avoiding degrees of debilitation or enmity and not being eclipsed,<-
->the one born shall become a lord of the earth through his own fame. ॥ 25 ॥<-

> ***Verse 25.*** If Jupiter, whether in exaltation, in <term>Swakshetra</term> <note>one's own house or sign; original: स्वक्षेत्र</note>, in a <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th house; original: केन्द्र</note>, in the 9th <term>bhava</term> <note>the house of fortune or the teacher; original: "guru-bhava"</note> or in a portion of some <term>Rasi</term> <note>zodiac sign; original: राशि</note> unconnected with the planet's depression or inimical house and not in eclipsing proximity to the Sun—if Jupiter in any one of the...

<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter X</header>

wealth and precious stones and have unfailing enjoyment up to the end of his life.

->**NOTES.**<-

Parasara <note>referring to Maharishi Parasara, the traditional father of Vedic astrology</note> adds:

Having projected the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>a "system of eight-fold strength" where planets gain points (bindus) based on their positions</note> of Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note> among the moving celestial bodies, one should perform the <term>Trikona-shodhana</term> <note>triangular reduction of points</note> and then the <term>Ekadhipatya-shodhana</term> <note>reduction based on a planet owning two signs</note>.

In whichever houses there are many benefic points, one should indicate for the person matters regarding the physical body, the spouse, and wealth in the directions corresponding to those signs.

The direction from which the spouse is obtained is found from the seventh house from Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note>. The sign where the lord of the seventh house is situated is known as the birth sign of the wife.

Some experts in this science also consider the lord's sign of exaltation or debilitation as the birth sign. Others say the birth of the wife occurs in a sign that is in a <term>Trine</term> <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions</note> from the <term>Navamsa</term> <note>the ninth-part divisional chart</note> of that lord.

The most excellent of sages say that the ninth house <note>the house of fortune</note> from the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note> or the Moon indicates the birth sign. By the methods mentioned above, the birth sign of the wife is determined.

A wise person should calculate the sign of union from those signs. Whether the planet is in its own sign, its exaltation, or a friend’s sign, the characteristics of the spouse must be described based on those influences.

The characteristics of the wife are also seen through the <term>Trines</term> from the seventh house of Venus. When the predicted sign is the actual birth sign of the wife, there will be progeny; if it is not the predicted sign, there will be no offspring.

Those who know the science of astrology understand these results through the signs occupied by Venus <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note>. In the acquisition of land and fortune, the results for a common person are as stated, but for a King, they are doubled.

When Venus is in a <term>Navamsa</term> of Saturn <note>original: "Manda"</note>, or is associated with Saturn, or in a sign ruled by Saturn, or if Venus is in its sign of debilitation or joined with malefic planets, the person desires the enjoyment of low-born women.

If a person dwells in the influence of the Son of the Earth <note>original: "Medinitanaya," referring to Mars</note>, they will possess a fine residence. Possessing the characteristics of a wrestler, and though given to the use of intoxicants <note>original: "Sitala-dajya," likely referring to wine or spirits</note>, they remain handsome but are prone to attacking others.

This is the case when Venus is in a <term>Navamsa</term> of Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</term> or in a sign ruled by Mars.

<page-num>679</page-num>
<header>Chapter Ten</header>

If Venus is joined with or aspected <note>in astrology, "aspect" refers to a planet 'looking' at another house or planet, projecting its influence there</note> by Mars <note>original: "Bhaumena"</note>, the man will desire the company of other men's wives.

If the seventh house <note>the house of marriage</note> is in a sign or <term>Navamsa</term> <note>a one-ninth divisional chart used for fine-grained analysis</note> of Saturn, or in a Navamsa of Mars, and is aspected by both Saturn and Mars, the wife of that man will be unfaithful, fickle, a servant, or one who destroys the husband’s happiness.

If the seventh house falls in a Navamsa owned by Saturn or Mars, and the lord of that house is also situated in a sign owned by Saturn or Mars, his wife will undoubtedly be unfaithful or of ill-repute.

If the Moon is in an unfavorable Navamsa, or in the seventh or twelfth house, and Venus is associated with malefic <note>harmful or "cruel"</note> planets, the man will suffer grief on account of a woman.

The wife's complexion, form, and qualities will be similar to the planet ruling the Navamsa occupied by Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note>; or she may resemble the Moon, or possess the qualities of the lord of the seventh house.

If the Moon is in a malefic Navamsa and is placed in the twelfth or seventh house, or is influenced by malefic paths, it is a cause for sorrow arising from women.

The wives are of good character if Venus is strong; if situated in a movable Navamsa, they possess virtues equal to their husband <note>original: "Swanatha"</note>.

If Saturn <note>original: "Mande"</note> is in a <term>Trine</term> <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions</note> from Venus, it is considered unfavorable; however, if Saturn is in its sign of depression <note>debilitation</note>, it may provide happiness. One should describe the characteristics of the wife based on the relative strength or weakness of these planets.

Having understood these and similar effects, one should then indicate the results based on the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> of Venus.

->If Venus <note>original: "Kavye"</note> is in its sign of depression, or is combust <note>too close to the sun</note>, or occupies the eighth or twelfth house,<-
->the previously mentioned <term>Rajayoga</term> <note>a "royal union" or combination for high status and success</note> will be destroyed.<-
->In the direction indicated by the house with the least number of benefic dots in the <term>Sukrashtakavarga</term>,<-
->it is best to have the chamber which is to contain the bed by means whereof women are to be won and kept in willing bondage. ॥ 29 ॥<-

*Sloka 29.* But if Venus be in depression or occupy the 7th, the 12th or the 8th <term>bhava</term> <note>astrological house</note>, the aforesaid **Rajayoga** (royal combination) will be destroyed; in the direction indicated by the house with the least number of benefic dots in the **Sukrashtakavarga** (the eight-fold strength chart of Venus), it is best to have the chamber which is to contain the bed by means whereof women are to be won and kept in willing bondage.

---

# The Results of Saturn

->If Saturn is in a sign that is completely void of benefic dots,<-
->the person born will meet a swift death or suffer the loss of wealth.<-
->If the planet occupies a pivot house associated with only one, two, three, or four dots,<-
->even if Saturn is in its sign of exaltation, the person will have a short life.<-

*Verse 30.* If in the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>a predictive system using a grid of points or "dots" based on planetary positions</note> of Saturn <note>original: "Mande"</note>, there be a sign thoroughly void of benefic dots, the person born will meet with death or sustain loss of wealth when the planet passes through the sign in question; if the planet occupying a <term>Kendra</term> <note>a "pivot" or angular house: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th</note> be associated with one, two, three or four benefic dots and even if that pivot house be the planet's exaltation sign <note>the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is most powerful</note>, any child delivered from the womb at such a time will be short-lived.

->NOTES<-

<margin>The sage Parasara adds:</margin>
->The eighth house from the position of Saturn is called the house of death.<-
->In the Ashtakavarga of Saturn, one should determine the span of life.<-
->Calculate the sum of the dots from the Ascendant to the sign held by Saturn.<-
->In the year of life equal to that sum, predict disease and enmity.<-
->Combine the dots from the sign of Saturn up to the Ascendant.<-
->In the year equal to that total of dots, predict sickness for the person.<-
->In the year equal to the sum of both these figures, a time of death is foretold.<-
->After performing the mathematical reductions and carefully establishing the sum,<-
->multiply it by the dots in the eighth house and divide by twenty-seven.<-
->If the resulting total is over one hundred, the wise should subtract one hundred.<-
->One should thus understand the remaining life, calculating it as before,<-
->after performing the reductions for trines and ownership of signs.<-

<note>This passage describes a complex astrological calculation where the "dots" (benefic points) earned by Saturn are used to count out specific years in a person's life that may be dangerous or fatal. The "reductions" mentioned refer to the process of <gloss>Trikona Shodhana</gloss> and <gloss>Ekadhipatya Shodhana</gloss>, which are standard mathematical steps in this system to refine the predictive data.</note>

<summary>This page details the dire effects of a weak Saturn in the Ashtakavarga system, specifically how a lack of benefic points in certain houses can predict poverty, illness, or a shortened lifespan.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, Shani, Ashtakavarga, longevity, death, Kendra, Parasara, astrology, benefic dots</keywords>

->Having established the total sum <note>original: "Pinda"</note>, one should multiply it by 18.<-
->A wise person should divide the result by 27 and use the remainder to predict the time of death.<-
->In the complete <term>Ashtakavarga</term>, if there is a house that contains no benefic dots,<-
->no benefit will come to the person when Saturn <note>original: "Shanaishchara"</note> passes through that house.<-
->If the Sun and Moon are in that house during a <term>Dasha-chiddra</term> <note>a vulnerable "gap" or transition period between two major planetary cycles</note>, predict death.<-

->Destruction occurs in those signs of Saturn’s Ashtakavarga that have the lowest number of dots.<-

->When the planetary period of a planet lacking strength arrives, death will surely occur.<-

->**Verse 31.** When Saturn, endowed with great strength, occupies the first house <note>original: "Tanuge," meaning the body or Ascendant</note><-
->and is associated with five or six benefic dots,<-
->the person suffers immense sorrow from birth and the loss of wealth.<-
->However, if Saturn is in its sign of depression or an inimical house but has five or more dots,<-
->and the Moon occupies an auspicious division or pivot house, the person will live a long life.<-

---

> *Sloka 31.* When Saturn with abundant strength occupies the 1st bhava <note>house</note> and is associated with 6 or 5 benefic dots, the person born will sustain loss of wealth accompanied by much suffering from his very birth. If the planet in depression <note>the zodiac sign where a planet's power is weakest</note> or in an inimical house be associated with 5 or more benefic dots and if the Moon occupies an auspicious Varga <note>divisional chart</note>, the person will have long life.

<page-num>682</page-num>
<header>The Adoration of Birth (Jataka Parijata), Chapter 10</header>

->When Saturn is in the 5th or 1st house <term>bhava</term><note>The 1st house represents the self/body, and the 5th represents children or intelligence.</note>,<-
->and is combust, in an enemy’s sign, or in debilitation, but has 5 or 4 benefic dots,<-
->the person will possess female servants, camels, and wealth.<-
->If Saturn in that same position has 8 benefic dots, they will be a lord of cities and villages,<-
->skilled in administration; if there are 7 dots, they will be very wealthy.<-

**Verse 32.** When Saturn, occupying the 5th or the 1st house <term>bhava</term>, is in an eclipsed state <note>combust, or too close to the sun</note>, in the house of an enemy, or in its lowest point of strength <note>depression/debilitation</note>, yet is associated with 5 or 4 benefic dots <note>points of planetary favor</note>, the person born under this combination <term>yoga</term> will possess female servants, camels, and property. If Saturn in the same position is associated with 8 benefic dots, the person born will be a lord of townships, possessed of the administrative machinery <note>original: "mantra-tantra," implying both political counsel and technical systems</note> characteristic of cities; if the number of benefic dots is 7, the person affected by this combination will possess abundant wealth.

## ॥ The Spread Table of Eightfold Strength ॥
<note>original: "Prastārāṣṭakavarga"</note>

->Draw a diagram with nine parallel lines running East to West<-
->crossed by thirteen parallel lines running North to South.<-
->This creates a table of ninety-six squares with eight rows,<-
->forming the spread for the eightfold system of strength.<-

**Verse 33.** To calculate the eightfold strength of the planets, one must draw a diagram consisting of 9 parallel lines at equal intervals from East to West, crossed by 13 parallel lines from North to South. This creates a table containing 96 squares <note>12 columns for the zodiac signs multiplied by 8 rows for the planets and Ascendant</note> divided into 8 rows, which displays the results for each of the several systems of planetary strength <term>Ashtakavarga</term>.

->The Ascendant, Moon, Mercury, Venus, Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn<-
->are the lords of the eight rows extending from the South;<-
->they are the ones who provide the results of the benefic dots.<-

**Verse 34.** The Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>, the Moon, Mercury, Venus, the Sun, Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn are the rulers of the divisions indicated in the 8 rows extending from the South <note>this refers to the vertical orientation of the table</note>. In their respective order, they distribute the fruits of the benefic dots.

<page-num>683</page-num>
<header>Verse 34, Chapter 10 — 683</header>

to the North of each sign and yield, every one of them, the benefic dots <note>These are points of positive influence, often called "bindus" in Sanskrit.</note> appearing in the 12 houses of its row.

---

->**NOTES.**<-

Note that the planets here have been mentioned in the order of their orbits round the Zodiac <note>This refers to the ancient "Chaldean order" of planetary spheres, arranged from the slowest-moving (Saturn) to the fastest (the Moon).</note>.

Let us now draw the <term>Prastharashtaka Varga</term><note>The "Spread Table of Eightfold Strength," a spreadsheet-like method to track planetary influences across the twelve signs.</note> Table and set therein the benefic dots for the Sun's <term>Ashtakavarga</term><note>A system where eight sources of influence (seven planets plus the Ascendant) contribute points to a sign.</note> in the sample horoscope referred to in page 657 supra.

*The 48 benefic dots will be distributed as shown below :*

There are three benefic dots in <term>Mesha</term><note>Aries</note>, and it will be seen that these have been contributed by the Sun, Mercury and the <term>Lagna</term><note>The Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>. While the other five planets have each contributed a malefic one <note>A "malefic" contribution is simply the absence of a benefic dot in this system.</note>.

When the Sun transits through the sign <term>Mesha</term><note>Aries</note>, he will give benefic results only during three periods viz., the 4th, the 6th and the 8th portions, that is when he is passing from (1) 11 1/4° to 15° (2) 18 3/4° to 22 1/2° and (3) 26 1/4° to 30° of that sign; the remaining five periods will prove malefic. Similarly in the case of the rest.

<page-num>684</page-num>
<header>The Essence of Victory in Birth: Chapter X</header>

<margin>Compare with the *Phaladeepika* <note>original: "Phaladeepika," a classic 15th-century astrological treatise.</note></margin>

Once the benefic dots <note>original: *bindu*; points of positive influence.</note> are placed in the "Spread Table of Eightfold Strength" <note>original: *Prastara Ashtakavarga*.</note> to determine the timing of their effects, the signs are divided into eight portions. Other authorities state that the results manifest according to the sequence of the planetary orbits <note>This refers to the *Kaksha* system, which divides a sign's 30 degrees into eight equal parts of 3 degrees and 45 minutes each.</note>.

In the first eighth-part of a sign's transit, Saturn provides the results; in the second part, Jupiter. Following this orbital sequence, the Ascendant <note>original: *Lagna*.</note> is considered to yield its intended fruit in the final portion.

In the spread table for all the planets, when one calculates the sum of the benefic dots situated in a sign at that time: if the count is eight or more <note>This likely refers to the total points contributed by the eight sources (7 planets + Ascendant) within a single planet's chart.</note>, the result is auspicious. If the count is less than that, it leads to a sequence of difficulties.

A planet associated with benefic dots will grant its full rewards; if it lacks these dots, it grants no beneficial effects. However, a planet occupying an inimical sign, its sign of depression <note>original: *neecha*; the sign where a planet is weakest.</note>, or in an eclipsed state <note>original: *asta*; combust or too close to the Sun.</note> will cause sorrow even if it possesses benefic dots. (35)

> *Sloka 35. If a planet be associated with benefic dots, it produces its full beneficial effect; if it be not connected with benefic spots, there is no beneficial effect produced; a planet in its depression or inimical sign or in an eclipsed state causes sorrow even if it be associated with benefic dots.*

## ॥ Reduction for Trines ॥
<note>original: *Trikona Shodhana*. This is the first step in refining the Ashtakavarga scores by comparing signs of the same element.</note>

Draw five parallel straight lines at equal intervals from East to West; and across these lines draw five others with the same intermediate space, in such a manner that the four inner squares (out of the 16 resulting ones) should appear omitted. In this diagram, you have the 12 celestial signs that rule over the East and other quarters and are the joint owners of the starry zodiac. (36)

> *Sloka 36. Draw five parallel straight lines at equal intervals from East to West; and across these lines draw five others with the same intermediate space, in such a manner that the four inner squares (out of the 16 resulting ones) should appear omitted. You have in the diagram the 12 celestial signs that rule over the East and [other] quarters and are the owners (jointly) of the starry zodiac.*

->Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius; Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn;<-
->Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius; Cancer, Scorpio, and finally Pisces.<-
<note>These are the four "Trines" (Trikonas), grouped by the elements of Fire, Earth, Air, and Water respectively. The reduction process involves comparing the scores within these groups.</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Ashtakavarga system, a method used to determine planetary strength. This process simplifies the data to reveal the net influence of planets across related groups of zodiac signs.</meta>

<page-num>685</page-num>
<header>Verses 37-38 Chapter Ten 685</header>

In the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>original: "Ashtakavarga," the 'System of Eightfold Strength' used to predict the effects of transiting planets.</note> of the group of planets beginning with the Sun, the benefic dots <note>original: *bindus*; these are points of positive planetary influence.</note> located in the triangular groups <note>original: *trikona*; signs that are 120 degrees apart and share the same elemental nature.</note> must be reduced, whether they are fewer, equal, or zero. ॥ 37 ॥

*Verse 37.* In the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> of any one of the Sun and other planets, take the signs by the triangular groups:
(1) **Aries** <note>original: *Mesha*</note>, **Leo** <note>original: *Simha*</note>, **Sagittarius** <note>original: *Dhanus*</note> (The Fire signs)
(2) **Taurus** <note>original: *Vrishabha*</note>, **Virgo** <note>original: *Kanya*</note>, **Capricorn** <note>original: *Makara*</note> (The Earth signs)
(3) **Gemini** <note>original: *Mithuna*</note>, **Libra** <note>original: *Tula*</note>, **Aquarius** <note>original: *Kumbha*</note> (The Air signs)
(4) **Cancer** <note>original: *Kataka*</note>, **Scorpio** <note>original: *Vrischika*</note>, **Pisces** <note>original: *Meena*</note> (The Water signs)

Examine the benefic dots in the three signs composing any group. If in any one sign of a group, the number of benefic dots is less than that in each of the remaining two, or equal to that in each of the other two, or is zero, that same value must be subtracted from the number of benefic dots in each of the three signs of the chosen group.

---

Among the houses in a triangular group, the dots in the sign with the fewest dots become the value for both the others. If a sign has no dots, the others are not reduced. If all signs have the same number, discard them all. ॥ 38 ॥

*Verse 38.* If a sign in a triangular group has the least number of benefic dots, the other two signs must have their benefic dots altered <insert>by subtraction</insert> into this least number. If a sign has no benefic dots <note>a value of zero.</note>, the remaining signs of the same group will keep their figures unaltered. If all the signs of a triangular group have the same number of benefic dots, they must all be removed (i.e., replaced by zeros).

->**NOTES.**<-

<margin>Compare with **Parashara** <note>original: *Parashara*, the legendary founding father of Vedic astrology.</note></margin>
> In the trines, whatever is the minimum, subtract that same amount from all three. 
> If one house is empty <note>original: *shunye*; zero dots.</note>, that triangle should not be reduced. 
> If there is equality across all houses, then one should clear them all out.

To this, **Mantreswara** <note>original: *Mantreswara*, a 16th-century astrologer.</note> adds in his **Phaladeepika** <note>original: *Phaladeepika*, or 'Lamp of Fruitful Results'.</note>:
<margin>from *Phaladeepika*</margin>
> If two houses are empty, then one should clear <note>original: *shodhayet*; reduce to zero.</note> the remaining house as well.

<note>This process, called *Trikona Shodhana* (Trinal Reduction), is the first step in refining the Ashtakavarga chart. It operates on the principle that planets in signs of the same element "share" their energy; by subtracting the minimum value, the astrologer finds the unique, surplus strength of each sign within that elemental group.</note>

It provides specific rules for handling signs with minimal points, equal points, or zero points based on classical authorities like Parashara.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of "reduction" (shodhana) in the Ashtakavarga system. While the previous page defined the four triangular groups of zodiac signs, this page addresses a scholarly disagreement on how to mathematically apply the reduction rules and introduces the next step in the process: the reduction based on shared planetary ownership.</meta>

<page-num>686</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter X</header>

The two verses represent the opposite views of two distinct schools of thought. The conflicting views rest on two distinct interpretations put upon the verse enunciating the rule for <term>Trikona-shodhana</term> <note>Reduction of the Triangular Groups</note>. "In the three triangular houses, whichever value is the least, that same amount should be subtracted from all three" <note>original: "त्रिकोणेषु च यन्न्यूनं तत्तुल्यं त्रिषु शोधयेत्" (trikoṇeṣu ca yannyūnaṃ tattulyaṃ triṣu śodhayet)</note>. When translated, the rule stands thus: When one of three triangular houses contains the least number of benefic dots <note>bindus</note>, that same number is to be subtracted from the figure in each house of that triangular group.

This rendering is not accepted by some who maintain that "subtracting the same amount across the three" <note>original: "तत्तुल्यं त्रिषु शोधयेत्" (tattulyaṃ triṣu śodhayet)</note> means that the figures in the three signs of a triangular group are to be altered so as to *equal* the least number, rather than being subtracted from. The latter view is advanced by <term>Balabhadra</term> <note>A renowned 17th-century Vedic scholar and author of the *Horaratnam*</note> and appears to be current in Southern India.

For the example referred to in verse 34 above, the subjoined charts show the <term>Suryashtakavarga</term> <note>The Sun's eight-fold strength chart</note> figures before and after the <term>Trikona</term> reduction:

| 5 | 3 | 7 | 2 | | 2 | 3 | 2 | 2 |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|---|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 5 | <insert>Ashtakavarga of the Sun. Total: 48 dots.</insert> | | 2 | | 2 | <insert>After Triangular Reduction (Trikona Shodhana)</insert> | | 2 |
| 2 | | | 7 | | 2 | | | 3 |
| 4 | 5 | 3 | 3 | | 3 | 2 | 2 | 2 |

# ॥ Reduction Based on Common Ownership ॥
### (Ekadhipatya-shodhana)

> Leaving aside the signs of Leo and Cancer, the figures in the five pairs of remaining signs belonging to the five planets starting from Mars,
> whether they are equal, zero, or unequal, and whether the signs are occupied by planets or not—
> these should be processed to find the remainders after the reduction of common ownership. ॥ 39 ॥
<note>original Sanskrit verse: कण्ठीरवं कठकभं च विना कुजादि- कावासराशियुगलोपगबिन्दुसंख्याः । तत्तुल्यशून्यविषमाग्रहसग्रहाद्या- स्त्वेकाधिपत्यपरिशोधितशेषिताः स्युः ॥ ३९ ॥</note>

*Verse 39.* Leave out the signs <term>Simha</term> <note>Leo, ruled by the Sun</note> and <term>Kataka</term> <note>Cancer, ruled by the Moon</note>. The figures in the five pairs of the remaining signs belonging to the five planets reckoned from **Mars** <note>Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn each rule two signs; this step accounts for that shared ownership</note>,

<summary>This page discusses a scholarly debate between two methods of triangular reduction (Trikona Shodhana) and introduces the next mathematical step, Ekadhipatya Shodhana, which accounts for planets that rule two different zodiac signs.</summary>

<header>Slokas 40-41 Chapter 10</header>
<page-num>687</page-num>

whether alike or unlike, represented by zero, associated with planets or not associated with them, have to be subjected to the process of the reduction styled <term>Ekadhipatya-shodhana</term> <note>Reduction based on shared ownership: a process where points are adjusted between two zodiac signs owned by the same planet, such as Mars owning both Aries and Scorpio</note>, and their net value obtained.

Compare the words of <term>Parasara</term> <note>The traditional father of Vedic astrology and author of the Bṛhat Parāśara Horāśāstra</note>:
->Thus, after reducing the triangular groups, one should then perform the reduction for shared ownership.<- <note>original: "एवं त्रिकोणे संशोध्य पश्चादेकाधिपत्यता"</note>
->When there are two signs owned by one planet, the wise astrologer should then reduce the points.<- <note>original: "क्षेत्रद्वयं फलानि स्युस्तदा संशोधयेद्बुधः"</note>
->If one sign is occupied by a planet and the other is vacant, reduce the vacant sign by the value of the occupied one.<- <note>original: "क्षीणेन सह चान्यस्मिन् शोधयेद्ग्रहवर्जिते"</note>
->If the occupied sign has fewer points and the sign without a planet has more points...<- <note>original: "ग्रहयुक्ते फले हीने ग्रहाभावे फलाधिके"</note>
->...reduce the vacant sign using that same [lesser] value.<- <note>original: "अनेन सह चान्यस्मिन् शोधयेद्ग्रहवर्जिते"</note>
->If the occupied sign has more points, discard the points in the vacant sign entirely.<- <note>original: "फलाधिके ग्रहैर्युक्ते चान्यस्मिन् सर्वमुत्सृजेत्"</note>
->If both signs are occupied by planets, no reduction should ever be made.<- <note>original: "उभयोर्ग्रहसंयुक्तौ न संशोध्यः कदाचन"</note>
->If both signs are without planets and the points are equal, discard all points in both.<- <note>original: "उभयोर्ग्रहहीनाभ्यां समत्वे सकलं त्यजेत्"</note>
->Because of the equality between occupied and vacant signs, the vacant one should be fully reduced.<- <note>original: "सग्रहाग्रहतुल्यत्वात् सर्वं संशोध्यमग्रहात्"</note>
->For the signs of Cancer and Leo, their ownership and points are treated individually.<- <note>original: "कुलीरसिंहयो राश्योः पृथक् क्षेत्रं पृथक् फलम्" — note: This is because the Moon (Cancer) and Sun (Leo) own only one sign each, unlike the other planets which own two.</note>

->If two signs are occupied by planets, do not reduce. If one of the two is a sign with zero points, also do not reduce. If the sign with planets has more points, discard the other; if they are equal, discard the points in the sign without a planet. 40<- <note>original Sanskrit verse 40</note>

**Sloka 40.** If the two <term>Rasis</term> <note>zodiac signs</note> having a common lord be both occupied by planets, no reduction need be made. If one of the two Rasis (whether occupied or unoccupied) have no benefic dots, then also there is no reduction. If one of the two Rasis be occupied and contain more benefic dots than the other, remove the figure in the latter. If both the Rasis be unoccupied and have the same number of benefic dots, remove both.

->When the points are equal in a sign with a planet and a sign without a planet, reduce the points in the vacant one. In two signs without planets, whether the points are more or less, they should be made equal to the lesser value. 41<- <note>original Sanskrit verse 41</note>

**Sloka 41.** When one of the two Rasis is occupied

...by a planet and the other not, and both have the same number of benefic dots, remove the figure in the unoccupied sign <note>original: Rasi</note>. If both the signs be unoccupied and have an unequal number of benefic dots, the greater figure is to be replaced by the less.

->If a planet occupies a sign with fewer dots, the other [unoccupied] sign’s dots are made equal to that number.<- <note>original: "खेटोपयाते लघुबिन्दुराशौ तत्तुल्यमायान्ति तदन्यसंख्याः"</note>

*Sloka 42.* When only one of the two signs <note>original: Rasis</note> with a common lord is occupied by a planet and has a smaller number of benefic dots than the unoccupied sign, the figure in the latter is to be made equal to the former. The triangular <note>original: Trikona</note> reduction is to be made first of all; the net figures are then to be subjected to the <term>Ekadhipatya</term> <note>Ekadhipatya-shodhana: A mathematical adjustment between two zodiac signs owned by the same planet, such as Mars owning both Aries and Scorpio, applied after the first stage of reduction</note> (Ekadhipatya) reduction.

->Multiply the remaining figures after reduction by the factors of the signs beginning with Aries.<- <note>original: "शोध्यावशिष्टानि गुणीकुर्वानि मेषादिमानैर्गुणकं हि भानाम्"</note>
->The Sun and other planets, multiplied by their own factors, are said to be the planetary multipliers. 43<- <note>original: "सूर्यादिकास्ते गुणिताः स्वमानैरैषां ग्रहाणां गुणकं वदन्ति ॥ ४३ ॥"</note>

*Sloka 43.* The net figures after the two reductions in the several signs are to be multiplied each into its <term>Rasimana</term> <note>राशिमान; Sign-factor or multiplier</note> (Rasimana), *i.e.* Rasi factor; and the products made into a total is called <term>Rasigunaka</term> <note>राशिगुणक; The sum of the products of the signs</note> (Rasigunaka) *i.e.* the sum of the Rasi-products. The net figures associated with the Sun and other planets in the several signs are to be multiplied each into its appropriate planetary factor or <term>Grahamaana</term> <note>ग्रहमान; Planetary factor or multiplier</note> (Grahamaana). The total of these products is called <term>Grahagunaka</term> <note>ग्रहगुणक</note> (Grahagunaka), *i.e.* total of planetary <note>The source text contains a typo: "Graba"</note> products.

->Seven, ten, eight, four, zero, five; seven, eight, nine, five,<- <note>original: "शैलाशावसुसागराम्बरशरैः शैलाहिगोसायकै-"</note>
->eleven and twelve: these are the sign multipliers for Aries and the rest in order.<- <note>original: "ईशद्वादशभिश्च राशिगुणकैर्मेषादिमानां क्रमात्"</note>
->Five, five, eight, five, zero, seven, and five: these are for the Sun<- <note>original: "बाणैः पञ्चभिरष्टकैः शरनभःशैलेषुभिर्भास्करा-"</note>
->and other planets, as the factors declared in the method for determining longevity. 44<- <note>original: "देवं व्योमतलाधिवासगुणकैरायुर्विधानोदितैः ॥ ४४ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page explains the final mathematical adjustments in the Ashtakavarga system, focusing on the Ekadhipatya reduction and the specific numerical multipliers used for each zodiac sign and planet to determine astrological results.</summary>
<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Ekadhipatya reduction, Trikona reduction, Rasimana, sign multipliers, Grahamaana, planetary factors, Jataka Parijata</keywords>

--- Page 68 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the Ashtakavarga system of calculation. Having completed the first stage of reduction (Trikona-shodhana) and the second stage (Ekadhipatya-shodhana) on previous pages, the author now provides the specific mathematical multipliers for zodiac signs and planets to determine a person's life expectancy.</meta>

<page-num>589</page-num>
<header>Slokas 44-45 Chapter Ten</header>

## Take the sum of the products of the zodiac signs and planets,
## And divide the result by thirty to find the years, months, and days of life.
## If the years resulting from these sign-factors exceed twelve, 
## One should adjust them to find the correct total of the lifespan. ॥ 45 ॥

**Slokas 44 & 45.** Determine the total sum of the <term>Rasi</term> <note>Zodiac sign</note> products. This is done by multiplying the numerical values in each of the twelve signs—starting with <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note>—by their specific multipliers. For the twelve signs beginning with Aries, these multipliers are 7, 10, 8, 4, 10, 5, 7, 8, 9, 5, 11, and 12. 

Next, determine the sum-total of the <term>Graha</term> <note>Planet</note> products. This is done by multiplying the values in the signs occupied by the planets by their respective multipliers. For the seven planets starting from the Sun, these multipliers are 5, 5, 8, 5, 10, 7, and 5. 

Add the total of the sign products to the total of the planet products. Divide this final sum by 30. If the resulting number <note>the quotient</note> is more than 12 years, divide it by 12; the remainder represents the <term>Ayus</term> <note>Lifespan or period of life</note> contributed by that calculation.

->Notes.<-

<margin>*compare* Parashara</margin>
<note>The following passage is a citation from the sage Parashara, a foundational authority in Indian astrology, providing a second source for these multipliers.</note>
> Set down the remainder after the reductions and increase it by the multiplier for the zodiac sign.
> If a planet is present in that sign, increase the value by the multiplier for that planet as well.
> Taurus and Leo are multiplied by ten; Gemini and Scorpio by eight <note>original: "vasubhih," a poetic term for the number 8</note>.
> Libra and Aries are multiplied by seven <note>original: "munibhih," referring to the seven sages</note>; Virgo and Capricorn by five <note>original: "sharaih," referring to the five arrows of the love-god</note>.
> The remaining signs are multiplied by their own specific values; these are the sign-multipliers in their proper order.
> For Jupiter, Mars, Venus, and Mercury, the multipliers are 10, 8, 7, and 5 respectively.
> The numbers for Mercury and the others should be multiplied individually by the planetary factors. 
> In the distributed <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>A system of "eight-fold points" used to predict the strength of planets</note> on the zodiac wheel, proceed as described before.

<sig>87</sig>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for calculating the span of life (Ayurdaya) using the Ashtakavarga system. The previous page detailed the specific multipliers for the zodiac signs and planets. This page applies those multipliers to a specific example—the Sun's chart—and introduces an alternative calculation method favored by the classical astrologers Balabhadra and Manthreswara.</meta>

<page-num>690</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter X</header>

->After performing the reduction for trines and the reduction for common lordships, multiply the results by the designated multipliers.<-
<note>original: "त्रिकोणैकाधिपशुद्धिं च कृत्वा तु गुणयेद्गुणैः"</note>
->The results produced from the individual point systems, starting with Saturn, should be divided by thirty.<-
<note>original: "खवह्निभक्तमन्दाद्याः क्रमाद्भिन्नाष्टवर्गजाः"</note>

| 2 | 2 | 0 | 2 | 24 | 14 | 0 | 16 |
| :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| 2 | <note>Sun's individual point system (Ashtakavarga) after the Common Lordship reduction (Slokas 39-42)</note> | | 2 | 22 | <note>Sun's individual point system after the Zodiac Multiplier (Slokas 44-45). The Sign Multiplier total = 162.</note> | | 8 |
| 0 | | | 3 | 0 | | | 30 |
| 2 | 2 | 2 | 0 | 18 | 16 | 14 | 0 |

Similarly, the <term>Planet Multiplier</term> <note>Graha Gunaka: a numerical factor assigned to planets based on their position</note> will be 90. This is composed of:
* 10 for Mercury and 14 for Venus in Gemini
* 10 for the Sun in Cancer
* 20 for Jupiter in Libra
* 16 for Mars in Scorpio
* 10 for Saturn in Aquarius
* 10 for the Moon in Pisces.

The sum of the <term>Sign Multiplier</term> <note>Rasi Gunaka: numerical factors assigned to each zodiac sign</note> and the <term>Planet Multiplier</term> is 162 + 90, which equals 252.

The <term>Lifespan</term> <note>Ayus</note> contributed by the Sun is 252 divided by 27, resulting in 9 1/3 years. A similar process is followed for the other planets.

However, both **Balabhadra** and **Manthreswara** <note>famous 15th and 16th-century authors of astrological treatises</note> prescribe a different method from this for calculating the <term>lifespan derived from individual point systems</term> <note>Bhinnashtakavargayurdaya</note>.

->Having multiplied and combined the values in this way, multiply the total by seven.<-
<note>original: "एवं गुणित्वा संयोज्य सप्तभिर्गुणयेत् पुनः"</note>
->Divide that result by twenty-seven; the quotient represents the years of life.<-
<note>original: "सप्तविंशतिहृल्लब्धवर्षाण्यत्र भवन्ति च"</note>
->Multiply the remainder by twelve to find the months, and continue for smaller units of time.<-
<note>original: "द्वादशाद्गुणयेल्लब्धमासाद्यघटिकाः क्रमात्"</note>
->If the total exceeds twenty-seven years, subtract the cycle of twenty-seven again.<-
<note>original: "सप्तविंशतिवर्षाणि मण्डलं शोधयेत्पुनः"</note>

Multiply the sum total of the two products (specifically, those for the Zodiac Signs and the Planets) by 7 and divide that product by 27. The quotient will give the duration in years, months, and so on. When the number of years obtained this way exceeds 27, subtract 27 (or multiples of 27) from it. The result will be the <term>Lifespan</term> <note>Ayus</note> granted by that planet, provided it does not require the specific reductions mentioned in the notes for the next verse.

<summary>This page illustrates the mathematical process of determining longevity by applying multipliers to the Sun's planetary points and introduces an alternative formula (Sum * 7 / 27) supported by masters like Manthreswara.</summary>

# Chapter Ten
### Verse 46

->If a planet is in its exaltation <note>the sign where it is most powerful</note>, double the number of years obtained for it by the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>a system of points based on the eight-fold positions of planets</note> process. If it is in its depression <note>the sign where it is weakest</note> or eclipsed <note>combust, or too close to the Sun</note>, the number of years in its case is to be halved. Obtain by proportion the <term>Ayus</term> <note>lifespan</note> when the planet occupies an intermediate position. In the case of Mars when retrograde <note>appearing to move backward in the sky</note>, the lifespan obtained is to be doubled.<-

->### NOTES<-

Compare what the classical authorities **Mantreshwara** and **Balabhadra** say on the subject:

> <note>original: "अन्योऽन्यमर्द्धहरणं ग्रहयुक्ते तु कारयेत् । नीचेऽर्द्धमस्तगेऽप्यर्द्धहरणं तेषु कारयेत् ॥"</note>
> One should perform a reduction by half if a planet is associated with another. A reduction by half should also be made if it is in its sign of depression or if it is eclipsed.
> <note>original: "शत्रुक्षेत्रविभागेने दृश्यार्द्धहरणं तथा । हरणं व्येशोनमर्केन्द्वोः पातसंश्रयणादपि ॥"</note>
> Likewise, a reduction is made for being in an enemy’s house or in the visible hemisphere. A reduction of one-half is also made for the Sun and Moon when they are associated with the nodes <note>Rahu or Ketu</note> during an eclipse.
> <note>original: "बहुत्वे हरणे प्राप्ते कारयेद्बलवत्तरम् । पश्चात्तान् सकलान् कृत्वा वेराङ्केन विवर्द्धितम् ॥"</note>
> When several reductions are applicable, one should only apply the most significant one. Afterward, having calculated all these, the result is multiplied by 324 <note>the "Vera" number</note>.
> <note>original: "मातङ्गलव्धं शुद्धायुर्भवतीति न संशयः । पूर्ववद्दिनमासान्दं कृत्वा तस्य दशा भवेत् ॥"</note>
> When divided by the "Matanga" number <note>likely 27, though some traditions vary</note>, the result is undoubtedly the purified lifespan. One should then calculate the days, months, and years to determine the <term>Dasha</term> <note>planetary period</note>.
> <note>original: "एवं ग्रहाणां सर्वेषां दशां कुर्यात् पृथक् पृथक् । अष्टवर्गदशामार्गः सर्वेषामुत्तमोत्तमः ॥"</note>
> In this way, one should calculate the period for all planets separately. This path of the Ashtakavarga system is the best of all methods.

The lifespan obtained is to be halved when the planet yielding the <term>Ayurdaya</term> <note>determination of longevity</note> has another planet associated with it in the same <term>Bhava</term> <note>astrological house</note>. The same reduction is to be made when a planet is depressed or obscured by the Sun's rays. If the planet occupies an inimical house <note>a sign ruled by a planet it is unfriendly toward</note> or be in the visible hemisphere, the reduction is by 1/3. When the Sun and the Moon being in the nodes suffer eclipse, the reduction is also by 1/2. Where several reductions crop up, it will be enough if the greatest of them be made. The net years, months, etc. thus obtained is to be multiplied by 324 and divided <meta>The sentence continues on the next page with the divisor used in this calculation.</meta>

# Jataka Parijata, Chapter X

Multiply by 365. The life-period will be obtained in years (of 365 days), months, and so on. In this way, ascertain individually the <term>Daśa</term> <note>planetary period</note> lengths for all the planets. The <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>a system where points are assigned to planets based on their positions relative to eight specific reference points</note> process is considered the best of all methods for this.

->For planets situated in their <term>Moolatrikona</term> <note>a specific sign where a planet is particularly strong, often called its "root-trine"</note>, their own signs, or the signs of friends,<-
->And for those placed in favorable divisions like their exaltation, or seen or joined by beneficial planets,<-
->The lifespan is exactly as calculated by the mathematical methods previously mentioned;<-
->But when a planet is in the division of a malefic or an enemy, that calculated lifespan must be reduced.<-

**Verse 47.** In the case of planets that have attained their <term>Moolatrikona</term>, <term>Swakshetra</term> <note>one's own sign or house</note>, or a friendly house, or occupy such benefic <term>vargas</term> <note>sub-divisional charts used to fine-tune planetary strength</note> as their exaltation, and are in conjunction with benefic planets or are aspected <note>viewed across the zodiac</note> by them, the <term>Ayus</term> <note>lifespan</note> is to be calculated in the manner prescribed above. When a planet is in the <term>varga</term> of a malefic or inimical planet, the <term>Ayus</term> arrived at must undergo reduction.

->The maximum lifespan of men is the sum of the years contributed by the Sun and the other planets;<-
->Some say, following the authority of great teachers, that the years for the <term>Lagna</term> should be added;<-
->According to the methods of Parasara, one arrives at the correct lifespan for the horoscope.<-

**Verse 48.** The number of years representing the maximum period of life in the case of men is obtained by putting together the figures denoting the years given by the Sun and other planets. These latter, some say upon the authority of great teachers (like **Parasara** <note>one of the legendary fathers of Vedic astrology</note>) should be supplemented by the number of years for the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</note> (deduced from the <term>Lagna Ashtakavarga</term>) in order to get the <term>Ayus</term> conformably with the conditions of any horoscope that may be under examination.

->### NOTES<-

The following verses from **Parasara** give the details of the <term>Lagnashtakavarga</term> <note>the specific Ashtakavarga point-system calculated for the Ascendant</note>.

->Now I shall describe the positions of the <term>Lagna</term>, O best of the twice-born!<-
<note>original: "अथ स्थानं प्रवक्ष्यामि लग्नस्य द्विजपुङ्गव ।" The term "twice-born" is a traditional honorific for those who have undergone spiritual initiation.</note>

*Verse* 50. ## ॥ The Destructive Asterisms of Saturn ॥
<note>original: Manda-vaināśika-nakṣatrāṇi. This section deals with specific lunar mansions that portend ruin when associated with Saturn's calculations.</note>

->Multiply the remainder after reductions by eight and divide the result<-
->by twenty-seven; the remainder indicates the star starting from Ashwini.<-
->Father, mother, siblings, relatives, sons, wife, or oneself—<-
->those corresponding to the result meet their end, or it causes the loss of great wealth. ॥ 51 ॥<-
<note>original: svasvasvarūpāṣṭabiñduguṇitaṃ śodhyavaśiṣṭaṃ phalaṃ / viṃśatyāsahasrasaptabhiśca vihṛtaṃ taccheṣatārāśvanī / tātastajjananī mahodarajano bañdhuḥ sutaḥ strī svaghaṃ / tattulyā vilayaṃ prayāñti vipulaśrīṇāśahetava vā</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for timing significant life events using the Ashtakavarga (eightfold classification) system. While the previous page focused on calculating total longevity (Ayurdaya), the text now shifts to predicting the timing of death for specific relatives or the native themselves, based on Saturn’s transit through specific lunar mansions. It then transitions into the Samudaya Ashtakavarga, or the collective point system.</meta>

<page-num>695</page-num>
<header>Verse 51-52 Chapter Ten 695</header>

*Verse 51.* In the <term>Ashtakavarga</term> <note>a system of points or 'benefic dots' assigned to each house in a horoscope based on planetary positions</note> of any planet, perform the two reductions <note>the technical processes known as Trikona Shodhana (triangular reduction) and Ekadhipatya Shodhana (reduction due to shared lordship)</note> and multiply the total of the remaining benefic dots by the number of dots (as they were before reduction) in the specific <term>bhava</term> <note>astrological house</note> of which that planet is the <term>Karaka</term> <note>significator; a planet that naturally represents a specific person or area of life</note>, counted from the position occupied by that planet. Divide the resulting product by 27 and identify the asterism <note>Nakshatra or lunar mansion</note> corresponding to the remainder. When Saturn passes through this specific asterism, death will occur for the person represented by that <term>bhava</term> and its <term>Karaka</term>. If the Sun is the <term>bhavakaraka</term> <note>house-significator</note>, it is the father who will pass away; if the Moon, the mother; if Mars, a brother; if Mercury, a relative; if Jupiter, a son; if Venus, a wife; and if Saturn, it is the owner of the horoscope himself who will die. If, at the time calculated, these persons do not die, then someone of similar standing will pass, or there will be a significant loss of wealth and prosperity.

### NOTES

For example, using the chart on page 657, the 9th house <note>which represents the father</note> counted from the Sun’s position is Pisces (Meena). The value in that house in the Sun’s <term>Ashtakavarga</term> is 5 (see the chart on page 686). The total sum of benefic dots remaining after the two reductions is 19 (see page 690). By multiplying 5 by 19 and dividing the result by 27, we receive a remainder of 14. The 14th star counted from Ashwini is <term>Chitra</term>. When Saturn transits through the <term>Chitra</term> asterism, we may predict that the father of the person, or someone of equal status, may pass away, or that a great loss of fortune will occur.

->।। The Collective Ashtakavarga (Samudayashtavarga) ।।<-
<note>original: atha samudāyāṣṭavargaḥ</note>

->Next, having drawn a diagram of the zodiacal sphere,<-
->showing the positions of all the points within the house-cells,<-
->I shall describe the results of the points attained by the Sun and others,<-
->starting from the sign Aries (Aja). ॥ 52 ॥<-
<note>original: tataḥ saṃmālikhya bhagolacakraṃ samastabindusthitirāśikoṣṭham / ravyādikānāmajapūrvakāṇāṃ bindūpagasthānaphalaṃ vadāmi</note>

*Verse 52.* Draw up a diagram of the zodiac exhibiting in its several houses the aggregate benefic dots...

<summary>This page explains how to predict the timing of a relative's death by calculating a specific lunar mansion (Nakshatra) from the Ashtakavarga points and tracking Saturn's transit. It also introduces the Samudaya Ashtakavarga, the collective point system for the entire horoscope.</summary>

<page-num>696</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter 10.</header>

...given by the Sun and other planets and arranged appropriately in <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> and the other <term>Rasis</term> <note>zodiac signs</note>. I shall now proceed to treat of the effect upon each <term>bhava</term> <note>astrological house</note> of the benefic dots grouped in it.

### NOTES.

The following two mnemonic <term>slokas</term> <note>verses</note> from Mantreswara’s *Phaladeepika* <note>a classic 13th-century astrological treatise; the name translates to "Light on Fruits (Results) of Destiny"</note> give the aggregate of the benefic dots appearing in the 12 places with reference to the seven planets and the <term>lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or rising sign</note> in the several <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>individual point systems for each planet</note>. The order of the planets is from the Sun onwards, and the <term>Lagna</term> appears as the 8th.

The figures are indicated by the convention commonly called <term>Katapayadi-sankhya</term> <note>a Sanskrit numerical notation system where specific letters represent digits</note> given below: (1) <note>original: "Kadinava"</note> The nine letters starting with 'ka' represent 1 through 9; (2) <note>original: "Tadinava"</note> The nine letters starting with 'ta' represent 1 through 9; (3) <note>original: "Padipancha"</note> The five letters starting with 'pa' represent 1 through 5; (4) <note>original: "Yadyashtow"</note> The eight letters starting with 'ya' represent 1 through 8.

<gloss>3 3 3 3 2 3 4 5 3 5 7 2</gloss>
The child, the strongest, the salt, the food, the sky-lord, the passions, the deer and others, the peaks, the wealth, the destroyer. <note>These words serve as mnemonic codes for the numbers 3, 3, 3, 3, 2, 3, 4, 5, 3, 5, 7, and 2 respectively, representing the Sun's points.</note>
<gloss>4 5 3 5 2 3 4 4 4 6 7 2</gloss>
<gloss>2 3 5 2 2 5 2 2 3 7 1</gloss>
Mars, the lord of elephants, the state of lightness, the one who moves in the rays, the cow's ear, the jewel, the ancient Maithili. <note>These phrases encode the points for the Moon and Mars.</note>
<gloss>3 1 5 2 6 6 1 2 5 5 7 3</gloss>
<gloss>2 2 1 2 3 4 2 4 2 4 7 3</gloss>
<gloss>2 3 3 3 4 4 2 3 4 3 6 3</gloss>
The Rudra, the others, the deep abyss, the place of nectar, the passionate one, the auspicious one, the radiant cow, the mountain of the earth. <note>These phrases encode the points for Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus.</note>
<gloss>3 2 4 4 4 3 3 4 4 4 6 1</gloss>
<gloss>5 3 5 5 2 5 1 2 2 6 7 1</gloss>
With words, with pure knowledge, with the knowledge of the soul, the wielder of the trident, the one who gives me joy, and the maker of holy fords. <note>These phrases encode the points for Saturn and the Lagna.</note>

* The figures in the 12 places, from the Sun are respectively 3, 3, 3, 3, 2, 3, 4, 5, 3, 5, 7, 2; from the Moon, 2, 3, 5, 2, 2, 5, 2, 2, 3, 7, 1; from Mars, 4, 5, 3, 5, 2, 3, 4, 4, 4, 6, 7, 2; from Mercury 3, 1, 5, 2, 6, 6, 1, 2, 5, 5, 7, 3; † from Jupiter, 2, 2, 1, 2, 3, 4, 2, 4, 2, 4, 7, 3; from Venus, 2, 3, 3, 3, 4, 4, 2, 3, 4, 3, 6, 3; from Saturn 3, 2, 4, 4, 4, 3, 3, 4, 4, 4, 6, 1; from Lagna, 5, 3, 5, 5, 2, 5, 1, 2, 2, 6, 7, 1.

<page-num>698</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Adh. X.</header>

The number of <term>benefic dots</term> <note>auspicious points known as bindus</note> up to 25 appearing in any <term>bhava</term> <note>astrological house or sign</note> are void of effect. From 25 to 30 benefic dots in a house produce a medium effect. More than 30 benefic dots in any house produce happiness, wealth, and fame while serving to advance the affairs of the house wherein they appear.

<page-num>699</page-num>
<header>Verses 54–56 Chapter Ten 699</header>

->**The Country of Nativity**<-
<note>original: "Jataka Desha"</note>

Signs that possess more than thirty points are considered the best; those with less than twenty-five are deemed inferior. This is the established order of importance. One should choose the best signs for all significant actions. Avoid inferior signs for travels and other important works, as they lead to hardship. In the union of a man and a woman, prosperity or calamity follows the high or low point-counts of their respective signs.
<note>original: "त्रिंशद्वयो येऽधिकाक्षा अपि शरकृतितो राशयो ये तदुना: श्रेष्ठा मध्याश्च कथा: क्रमशः इति मता गृह्यतां श्रेष्ठराशिः । सर्वांसिष्टक्रियासु त्यजतु न गमनाद्येषु कार्येषु कष्टान् सम्बन्धे संपदापत्सति युवतिनृणां श्रेष्ठष्टर्क्षकानां ॥"</note>

*Verse* 54. Planets occupying, it may be, their exaltation, their own, a friend’s house, a <term>Trikona</term> <note>a "trine" or the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>, a <term>Kendra</term> <note>an "angle" or the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note>, or an <term>Upachaya</term> <note>houses of "growth" or improvement: 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th</note> place or possessed of such strength as is derived from being in benefic <term>vargas</term> <note>favorable sub-divisional charts</note>, may do evil if associated with insufficient benefic dots in the <term>Sarvashtakavarga</term> <note>the collective chart showing the total points for each sign</note>.
<note>original: "ये तुङ्गराशिखसुहृद्‌गृहास्था ये कोणकेन्द्रोपचयस्थिताश्च । ये सौम्यवर्गादिबलोपयातास्ते नाशदा लाघवबिन्दुकाश्वेत् ॥ ५४ ॥"</note>

*Verse* 55. Planets which as occupying portions of the 12th, the 6th, the 8th or the 7th <term>bhavas</term> <note>astrological houses</note> are considered malefic; secondly, those that are in the <term>vargas</term> of their depression signs <note>signs of debility where a planet is weakest</note> or of malefic or inimical planets; 3rdly and lastly, those that conspire in conjunction with the lord of the sign occupied by <term>Mandhi</term> <note>a sensitive mathematical point in a chart often associated with obstacles</note> to work evil—these three classes of planets, if only associated with a higher number of benefic dots, produce all important beneficial results to the person concerned.
<note>original: "ये चावसानरिपुरन्ध्रतुरङ्गभागा: ये नीचपापरिपुखेचरवर्गयुक्ता: । ये मान्दिराशिपतिना सह बाधकाश्च ते सर्वमुख्यफलदास्त्वधिबिन्दुकाश्वेत् ॥ ५५ ॥"</note>

*Verse* 56. If the 11th house from the sign occupied by the Sun contains a high number of points, and if that number is greater than the points in the 12th house; and further, if the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>the 1st house or rising sign</note> has more points than the 12th house, the person born will be happy, wealthy, and possessed of great strength and fame.
<note>original: "मानस्थिताल्लाभगृहे बहुत्वे लाभाल्लघुत्वे यदि रिःफराशौ । रिःफोपयातादधिके विलग्ने जातः सुखी वित्तयशोबलाढ्यः ॥ ५६ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page explains that numerical point totals (Ashtakavarga) can override a planet's dignity; even a planet in a "bad" house can produce great results if its point total is high, and a planet in a "good" house can cause harm if its point total is low.</summary>
<keywords>Ashtakavarga, Bindus, Sarvashtakavarga, Trikona, Kendra, Upachaya, Mandhi, Bhava strength</keywords>

<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter X</header>

*Verse 60.* If a person is born under a planetary combination signifying great prosperity, they will—after a number of years matching the points in the <term>Sarvashtaka Varga</term> <note>a collective numerical system used to weigh the strength of the twelve houses</note> of their <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or 1st house</note>—be well on their way to acquiring royal transport (such as a palanquin), great wealth, children, and immense knowledge.

*Verse 61.* When the ruler of the 12th <term>bhava</term> <note>the house of loss or expenditure</note> resides in the 1st house—provided that sign is one ruled by Saturn <note>Capricorn or Aquarius</note>—and when the rulers of the 1st and 8th houses are weak, the person will live for as many years as there are benefic points in the <term>Lagna</term> within the <term>Sarvashtaka Varga</term> chart.
<note>original: "रिःफाधीशो मन्दगेहोदयस्थे होरारन्ध्रस्वामिनो दुर्बलौ च । लग्ने यावद्विन्दुसंख्यास्तदीया जातस्यायुर्वत्सराः सम्भवन्ति ॥ ६१ ॥"</note>

*Verse 62.* When the ruler of the 4th house <note>the house of vehicles and property</note> is in the 1st house, and the ruler of the 1st house is in the 4th, and if the number of benefic points in each of the signs occupied by these two planets is 33, the person born will become a leader of men and a source of kingly glory.
<note>original: "यानाधीशो लग्ने चाङ्गस्थे लग्नाधीशो तद्ग्रहोपेतराश्योः । त्रिंशत्त्रयसंख्याविन्दवः स्युर्यश्चेत् जाता राजश्रीनिदाना नरेन्द्राः ६२"</note>

*Verse 63.* When the 1st, 4th, and 11th <term>bhavas</term> <note>the houses of self, comforts, and gains</note> are each associated with more than 30 benefic points, the person born will, after their 40th year, attain abundant power and prosperity, eventually reaching the status of a king.
<note>original: "होरान्धुप्राप्तिभावत्रयेषु त्रिंशन्मानाधिक्यविद्वृपगेषु । जातस्तेजःश्रीबहुत्वं च राज्यं चत्वारिंशद्वत्सरादूर्ध्वमेति ॥ ६३ ॥"</note>

->Regarding counts ranging from twenty-five to thirty,<-
->and the points found in the 4th and 9th houses...<-
<note>original: "यत्पञ्चविंशतिमुखास्त्रि दशान्तसंख्या बन्धुस्थिता नवमराशिकबिन्दवश्च ।" This fragment appears to be the beginning of the next rule regarding mid-range point counts.</note>

<summary>This page details how specific point totals in the Sarvashtaka Varga system determine the timing of prosperity, the length of life, and the potential for achieving high political or social status.</summary>
<keywords>Sarvashtaka Varga, Lagna, longevity, prosperity, 4th house, kingship, timing of success</keywords>

### Chapter Ten
<sig>Verses 64-67</sig>

->If, according to this eight-fold system, a person has between twenty-five and thirty benefic points in the fourth and ninth <term>Rasis</term> <note>zodiac signs</note> from the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant or 1st house</note>, they will become as wealthy as <term>Kubera</term> <note>the Vedic god of wealth and treasures</note> at the end of their twenty-eighth year, or in the years following. — 64<-

*Verse 64.* <note>original: "यद्यष्टकेन सह विंशतिवत्सराणा- मन्ते परे शरदि वा नरवाहनाढ्यः ॥ ६४ ॥"</note> If the number of benefic dots in the 4th <note>the house of comforts and property</note> and in the 9th <term>Rasi</term> <note>the house of fortune and father</note> from the <term>Lagna</term> lie between 25 and 30, the person born will be as rich as <term>Kubera</term> at the end of 28 years or in some year subsequent thereto.

->When <term>Devacharya</term> <note>the "Teacher of the Gods," another name for the planet Jupiter</note> occupies the fourth house in his state of exaltation <note>the sign of Cancer, where Jupiter is strongest</note> and is associated with forty benefic points, and when the Sun is in <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> as the <term>Lagna</term>, the person born will become a king in command of a hundred thousand horses. — 65<-

*Verse 65.* <note>original: "देवाचार्ये वाहनस्थे स्वतुङ्गे चत्वारिंशद्विन्दुसंख्यासमेते । मेषागारे लग्ने वासरेशो जातो राजा लक्षसंख्याश्वनाथः ॥ ६५ ॥"</note> When Jupiter occupying the 4th <term>bhava</term> <note>house</note> in exaltation is associated with 40 benefic dots and the Sun is in <term>Mesha</term> identical with the <term>Lagna</term>, the person born will become a king and be in command of a 100,000 horse.

->With forty benefic points in the <term>Lagna</term>, with Jupiter in <term>Dhanus</term> <note>Sagittarius</note>, with Venus in <term>Meena</term> <note>Pisces</note>, with Mars in his exaltation <note>Capricorn</note>, and with Saturn in <term>Kumbha</term> <note>Aquarius</note>, the person born will be an emperor and a "bearer of all prosperity." — 66<-

*Verse 66.* <note>original: "चत्वारिंशद्विन्दुयुक्ते विलग्ने चापे जीवे भार्गवे मीनराशौ । स्वोच्चे भौमे कुम्भगे भानुपुत्रे जातः सर्वश्रीधरः सार्वभौमः ॥ ६६ ॥"</note> With 40 benefic dots in the <term>Lagna</term>, with Jupiter in <term>Dhanus</term>, with Venus in <term>Meena</term>, with Mars in exaltation and with Saturn in <term>Kumbha</term>, the person born should become an emperor blessed with every kind of good fortune.

->The four cardinal directions, beginning with the East, are assigned to the four groups of three signs each, starting with Aries. In whichever direction there is an abundance of points, the person will find an increase in wealth and prosperity. — 67<-

*Verse 67.* <note>original: "क्रियादिराशित्रितयोपयाता भवन्ति पूर्वादिचतुर्दिशश्च । फलाधिकं यद्दिशि तत्प्रदेशे धनादिवृद्धिं समुपैति जातः ॥ ६७ ॥"</note> The four quarters beginning with the East have been assigned to the four triads of <term>Rasis</term> <note>groups of three signs</note> whereof the first are <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note>, <term>Vrishabha</term> <note>Taurus</note>, <term>Mithuna</term> <note>Gemini</note> and <term>Kataka</term> <note>Cancer</note> (*See Chapter I, verse 13*). With reference to a horoscope that may be under examination, ascertain the quarter in which there is an abundance of benefic dots;

<summary>This page describes specific high-status planetary configurations involving the 4th and 9th houses, noting that totals of 25-40 points in these areas produce wealth comparable to kings or the god Kubera. It also explains how to determine the geographical direction of one's future prosperity by grouping the zodiac signs into directional triads.</summary>

<page-num>700</page-num>
# Jatakaparijata, Chapter X

**Verse 56.** If in the **Sarvashtaka Varga** <note>the collective table of benefic points from all planets</note> the number of benefic dots in the 11th <term>Bhava</term> <note>the house of gains</note> be greater than that in the 10th <note>the house of profession</note>, but the figure in the 12th <note>the house of loss</note> lower than that in the 11th, and if the figure in the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the 1st house or Ascendant</note> exceed the figure in the 12th <term>Bhava</term>, the person born will be happy with abundance of wealth, fame and strength.

<margin>Compare with the text *Phaladeepika*</margin>
->If the points in the house of gains exceed those of the middle house <note>original: "मध्यात्" (madhyat); the 10th house is the meridian or 'middle' of the sky</note>, and if the expenditure is much less than the gains, and if the points in the Ascendant exceed the expenditure, one becomes wealthy and a seeker of pleasures.<-

->Divide the twelve signs into three blocks, beginning with Pisces, Cancer, and Scorpio respectively.<-
->Each block contains four houses and their associated benefic points.<-
->These represent the beginning, middle, and end of one’s life.<-
->Some scholars in this world say these divisions should be calculated starting from the house of expenditure. — 57<-

**Verse 57.** Three divisions made of the 12 signs, the first commencing with <term>Meena</term> <note>Pisces</note>, the second with <term>Karkataka</term> <note>Cancer</note> and the third with <term>Vrischika</term> <note>Scorpio</note> and exhibiting each its own benefic dots in its respective 4 houses are made to indicate the first, the middle and the concluding portion of the life under examination. Some opine that the three grand divisions are made from the **Vyayabhava** <note>the 12th house of expenditure</note> to begin with.

<margin>Compare with the text *Jataka Desha*</margin>
->One should arrange the three divisions starting with Pisces, Cancer, and Scorpio.<-
->Abundance of points in the first division brings happiness in the first third of life.<-
->Abundance in the middle brings it in middle age, and in the final division for the last third of life.<-
->Where the points are deficient, that portion of life will be marked by the occurrence of misery and disease.<-

->Some wise men prefer to calculate the segments after setting aside the points found in the 12th and 8th houses.<-
->Based on whether the remaining points are equal, few, or many, men experience a mixture of sorrow or prosperity. — 58<-

**Verse 58.** Excluding the dots located in the <term>Riph</term> <note>the 12th house</note> and the <term>Randhra</term> <note>the 8th house</note>, some virtuous sages prefer to examine the remaining segments. According to the sequence of equal, few, or more dots, there will be a mixture of misery or prosperity for men.

<vocab>Sarvashtaka Varga: a system of points used to predict the strength of houses; Bhava: an astrological house; Lagna: the Ascendant or 1st house; Meena: Pisces; Karkataka: Cancer; Vrischika: Scorpio; Vyayabhava: the 12th house of loss; Jatakaparijata: a classic 15th-century Sanskrit work on astrology; Phaladeepika/Jataka Desha: famous astrological treatises; झष (Jhasha): Pisces; करकट (Karkata): Cancer; कीटक (Keetaka) / इन्द्रालय (Indralaya): Scorpio; रिःफ (Riph): the 12th house; रन्ध्र (Randhra): the 8th house.</vocab>

<keywords>Sarvashtaka Varga, wealth prediction, life stages, Sanskrit astrology, Lagna, houses of gain and loss</keywords>

# Chapter Ten: The Collective Ashtakavarga

**Verse 58.** Some authorities recommend omitting the benefic dots <note>auspicious points in the horoscope</note> in the 12th as well as in the 8th <term>bhava</term> <note>house</note> from their respective divisions; the aggregate benefic dots in any one division should then be compared with those in the other two. If the totals in the three divisions are equal, the person's life will be a mixed one, with joys and sorrows evenly distributed. If they are unequal, a lower figure implies a decrease in comfort—that is, misery—while a higher figure betokens increased comforts and prosperity.

->If a division is occupied by benefic planets, it brings happiness; a mixed occupancy brings mixed results.<-
->A division <note>original: "खण्डं" (khanda)</note> that is combined with the movement of malefic planets becomes a source of affliction and pain.<-
->If the benefic dots are few, the person suffers from disease, mental anguish, and speaks ill of others.<-
->If there is an abundance of dots, the person born will be endowed with immense prosperity during that stage of life. — 59<-

**Verse 59.** If benefic planets <note>such as Jupiter or Venus</note> occupy the signs of any division, the portion of life corresponding to this <term>khanda</term> <note>a section or block of time</note> will be happy. If the signs of a <term>khanda</term> have both malefic and benefic planets in them, the effect on that portion of life will be mixed; if any division contains malefic planets exclusively, the period of life represented will abound in various afflictions. If the number of benefic dots in any division is small, the person born will suffer from disease and mental pain and will speak evil things. If there is a preponderance of benefic dots in a <term>khanda</term>, the person concerned will enjoy extensive prosperity in the period of life thus distinguished.

->According to the number of benefic dots obtained, after that many years have passed,<-
->in that specific year, one acquires a royal vehicle <note>original: "राजयानम्" (rajayanam); a symbol of high status or state carriage</note>.<-
->One attains wealth, children, and exceptional knowledge,<-
->provided the person is born with a combination for prosperity. — 60<-

**Verse 60.** When the number of benefic dots is favorable, after that specific number of years has passed, the individual attains a "royal vehicle" <note>in a modern context, this implies high-status transport or official honors</note>. If the person is born under a <term>Yoga</term> <note>astrological combination</note> for prosperity, they will also acquire wealth, children, and advanced education during that time.

<summary>This page explains how the three divisions of the zodiac (khandas) predict the quality of different life stages and identifies specific years when a person might achieve high status and wealth based on point totals.</summary>
<keywords>Sarvashtaka Varga, bhava, khanda, prosperity, royal vehicles, life stages, Vedic astrology, Jatakaparijata</keywords>
