<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter 10</header>

...it is there that the owner of the horoscope will get an accession to his wealth, and other benefits.

---

**Verse 68.** Find the aggregate of benefic dots <note>the auspicious points known as *bindus* in the Ashtakavarga system</note> starting from the **Ascendant** <note>the *Lagna*, or the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note> and counting up to the sign occupied by **Saturn** (including both). Multiply this total by 7 and then divide the product by 27. When the Sun or other malefic planets <note>malignant planets like Mars or Saturn</note> pass through the **asterism** <note>the *Nakshatra* or lunar mansion</note> indicated by the remainder (counting from the first star, *Ashwini*) or through its trine positions <note>the *Trikona* stars, located at intervals of 120 degrees</note>, the person concerned will suffer from diseases and other significant hardships.

**Verse 69.** Perform a similar calculation with the aggregate of benefic dots counted from **Saturn** to the **Ascendant**, from **Mars** to the **Ascendant**, and from the **Ascendant** to **Mars**, respectively. Additionally, find the total number of benefic dots in the zodiac signs <term>Rasis</term> occupied by benefic planets <note>auspicious planets like Jupiter or Venus</note> and identify the star indicated by that number. When a benefic planet passes through this specific star, it is said that the person will experience periods of happiness and prosperity.

### NOTES

Compare this with the text **Phaladeepika**:
> <note>original: "लग्नात्प्रभृति मन्दान्तमेकीकृत्य फलानि वै..."</note>
> Starting from the Ascendant, total the points up to Saturn. Multiply this by seven and divide the result by the twenty-seven stars. In the year corresponding to that final number, the person may encounter suffering or disease. One should perform the same calculation from Saturn to the Ascendant, and likewise for Mars and Rahu.

Also see the text **Jatakaadesha**:
> <note>original: "लग्नादारभ्य सूर्यात्मजगतभवनाक्षान्तमेकत्र युक्त्या..."</note>
> By totaling the points starting from the Ascendant up to the house occupied by the son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>, and multiplying that sum... when the person reaches the age equal to that resulting number, they will face disease and grief.

<page-num>705</page-num>
<header>Verse 70, Chapter Ten</header>

->Having thus calculated the distance from Saturn to the Ascendant, and similarly from Mars to the Ascendant and from the Ascendant to Mars, one should apply the prescribed method and predict unfavorable outcomes in the specific years indicated by those totals.<-

The following additional information based on the <term>Sarvashtakavarga</term> (**the collective table of auspicious points**) figures are extracted from the treatise **Jatakadesha**.

->One should sum the auspicious points of the Ascendant, the 5th, 7th, 9th, and 10th houses. If the majority of points are found within these internal houses, it indicates a state of mental contentment. One will be devoted to learning, wisdom, virtuous deeds, and charity. However, if the majority of points fall in the other houses, it results in mental distress and a tendency toward hypocrisy.<-

->One should also group the houses into four categories: the "Relative" <note>Bandhu</note>, the "Servant" <note>Sevaka</note>, the "Nourisher" <note>Poshaka</note>, and the "Destroyer" <note>Ghataka</note>, by summing the points in the four sets of trine signs <note>Trikona houses: 1-5-9, 2-6-10, 3-7-11, and 4-8-12 respectively</note>. If the points in the "Nourisher" group exceed those in the "Destroyer" group, the person will be wealthy. Conversely, if the points in the "Destroyer" group are higher than those in the "Nourisher" group, poverty is certain.<-

->By summing the points in the Angular houses <note>Kendra: 1, 4, 7, 10</note>, the Succedent houses <note>Panapara: 2, 5, 8, 11</note>, and the Cadent houses <note>Apoklima: 3, 6, 9, 12</note>, one can determine the auspicious or inauspicious nature of the first, middle, and final stages of life respectively, based on which group has more or fewer points.<-

->In the year of life corresponding to the number of points in the sign occupied by Rahu <note>referred to here as Phani, the Serpent</note>, a person may suffer from a snake bite or the effects of poison, especially if the lord of the sign is in an unfavorable position. In the year corresponding to the points in the sign occupied by Mars, there may be wounds from weapons <note>original: shastra-kshati</note>. In the year corresponding to the points in the sign occupied by Saturn, one may suffer from diseases, grief, and similar afflictions.<-

**Having performed the "Reduction of Trines" and the "Reduction of Common Lordship," and having set aside the values associated with the Sun, one should proceed.**
**If the number 12 does not divide the remainder exactly, the result of the Sarvashtakavarga reduction for Aries and the other signs is established. ॥ 70 ॥**

*Verse 70.* Make the <term>Trikona</term> <note>Reduction based on the four triangular groups of signs</note> and <term>Ekadhipatya</term> <note>Reduction based on planets that rule two different zodiac signs</note> reductions in regard to the <term>Sarvashtakavarga</term> (**collective auspicious points**) figures. Out of the figures left after the two reductions in the several <term>Rasis</term> <note>zodiac signs</note>, go on subtracting 12 as often as possible. If the reduced figure happens to be 12 itself, it is not to be taken away. The figures contained in <term>Mesha</term> <note>Aries</note> and other <term>Rasis</term> are to be thus treated.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions from Chapter Ten (and into Chapter Eleven) regarding the Sarvashtakavarga system. While the previous page discussed the psychological and material impacts of point distributions in trines and house groups, this page shifts focus to the specific mathematical "reductions" and multipliers used to calculate a person's total lifespan (Ayurdaya).</meta>

<page-num>706</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter XI</header>

---

->After calculating the Eight-fold Chart (<term>Ashtakavarga</term>) of the planets across the zodiac,<-
->one should first perform the trinal reduction <note>original: "Trikona Shodhana"; the process of comparing signs 120 degrees apart and subtracting the smaller value from the larger</note>, and then the reduction based on shared lordship <note>original: "Ekadhipatya Shodhana"; reduction applied to planets like Mars or Venus that rule two zodiac signs</note>.<-
->If the points in a single sign exceed the zodiacal cycle of twelve, one should perform a further reduction.<-
->One should take only twelve as the base for all signs in this calculation.<-
->Multiply the remaining points in each sign individually by the multipliers mentioned previously.<-
->Sum all these results together and then multiply the total by seven.<-
->The quotient obtained by dividing this sum by twenty-seven <note>representing the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note> is praised as the Longevity Aggregate (<term>Ayus Pinda</term>).<-
->From the remainder, one can determine the months, days, and <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>.<-
->If that aggregate exceeds one hundred, one should subtract the cycle of one hundred from it.<-
->One hundred years is to be taken as the limit for the long life promised by this planetary combination.<-
->Taking that total, multiply it by 324 <note>original: "Khara-anga"; a numerical code</note>.<-
->Divide that result by 365 <note>original: "Matanga"</note>; the resulting lifespan is highly esteemed as the solar-year calculation.<-

->Multiply the net figures in the trinal and lordship reductions by the zodiacal and planetary factors, then find their sum.<-
->Multiply this by seven and divide by the number of lunar mansions (27); the result provides the years of life.<-
->If the total exceeds one hundred, remove the excess; if not, use the result to find the "Star-Lifespan" (<term>Nakshatra Ayus</term>).<-
->By multiplying this by the days of the lunar year and dividing by the solar year, the true lifespan is purified. || 71 ||<-

**Verse 71.** Multiply the net figures in the Collective Table of Auspicious Points (<term>Sarvashtakavarga</term>)—after the reductions mentioned in the previous verse have been made—by the zodiacal and planetary factors and make an aggregate of the products. Multiply this by 7 and divide the product by 27. The quotient, consisting of years, months, days, etc., if in excess of the standard lifespan (<term>Ayus</term>), i.e., 100 years, should be diminished by 100 years; if it is less than 100, it should be kept unaltered. This will represent the Star-Lifespan (<term>Nakshatra Ayus</term>) which, when multiplied by 324 and divided by 365, will give the period of life correctly in solar years.

<note>This calculation is a classic example of ancient Indian mathematical astronomy, reconciling the lunar calendar (324 days) with the solar calendar (365 days) to determine human longevity.</note>

<summary>This page outlines the complex mathematical procedure for calculating a person's lifespan (Ayurdaya) by applying trinal and planetary reductions to the Sarvashtakavarga points and converting the results into solar years.</summary>

->The years of life—whether short, medium, or long—calculated through the cycles of the zodiacal wheels,<-
->will align perfectly in every way due to the auspicious glance of the planets' virtuous qualities. 72<-

**Verse 72.** The years making up the life whether short, middle or long of a person (obtained by the process above mentioned) will square with the period otherwise obtained, by the diminution or addition as the case may be of a multiple of 27 ascertained upon an examination of the aspect <note>the "glance" or influence one planet exerts on another or a house</note>, if any, upon the <term>Lagna</term> <note>the Ascendant; the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth</note>, by benefic <note>favorable or "good-doing"</note> planets.

->In the <term>Jatakaparijata</term> <note>a classic 15th-century astrological treatise</note> composed by <term>Vaidyanatha</term>,<-
->which attains the essence of the results taught by sages such as <term>Parasara</term>,<-
->the nature of the dots in the spread, individual, and collective charts<-
->has been declared by the grace of all the celestial wanderers. 73<-

->**|| Thus Ends Chapter Ten ||**<-

**Verse 73.** In the work of <term>Jatakaparijata</term> compiled by <term>Vaidyanatha</term> and containing the essence of treatises on planetary effects such as <term>Parasara's</term> <note>referring to the Brihat Parasara Hora Shastra, the foundational text of Indian astrology</note>, the chapter on benefic dots, appearing in the <term>Ashtakavargas</term> <note>an eight-fold system of points used to weight the strength of houses</note> designated <term>Prasthara</term> (Spread), <term>Bhinna</term> (Individual) and <term>Samudaya</term> (Collective) has been treated by the grace of the entire body of planets.

# || Now, the Knowledge of the Month of Death ||

->From the position of Saturn to the lord of the eighth house from Saturn, find the total sum of points.<-
->Multiply that by two, and then by the points located specifically in the eighth house.<-

<meta>This page continues the technical mathematical formulas for predicting the timing of death (Marana) found at the conclusion of Chapter Ten. While the previous page suggested the end of the chapter after Verse 73, these subsequent verses provide specific methods using the Sun, Rahu, and the Moon to narrow down the month and specific lunar influence of the final hour.</meta>

<page-num>708</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata <note>original: "जातकपारिजाते"</note> Adh. X.</header>

---

Multiply the remainder <note>derived from the previous lifespan calculations</note> by twelve. When the **Sun** enters the zodiac sign corresponding to that result, one should recognize the timing of death; or perhaps when it enters a **Trine** <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th positions from that sign</note> to that result.

In this very same manner, one should determine the timing regarding the houses of the **father, mother**, and others. One should deliberate on all these matters from the **Lagna** <term>Ascendant</term> or from the lord of the **eighth house** <note>the house traditionally associated with longevity and death</note>.

Set down the minutes of the **Sun’s longitude** and multiply them by the minutes of **Rahu’s longitude** <note>Rahu is the North Node of the Moon</note>. Divide the product by the minutes of a full circle <note>21,600 minutes</note> and add the resulting quotient to the Sun’s current position.

When the Sun reaches such a calculated degree, in that month one should predict death. Or, one may point to the time of passing when the Sun enters a **Trine** position relative to that degree.

If the **lord of the eighth house** is in its sign of **debilitation** <note>its weakest state</note>, and the **Moon** is in the sixth or eighth house, or if it occupies a house inimical to the Lagna, one should predict death in that month.

## Determination of the Moon at Death <note>original: "अथ निधनचन्द्रज्ञानम्"</note>

When the lord of the **eighth house** is in a **Trine**, and the **Moon** is also there, one may predict death. This is calculated from the Navamsa <note>the 1/9th divisional chart</note> of the birth Lagna or from the Navamsa of the Moon.

When **Rahu** reaches the **64th Navamsa** <note>a highly sensitive mathematical point in Vedic astrology used for predicting mishaps</note>, one should point to death. It may occur when the sign of the eighth house from the birth Lagna is rising, or during the rise of the birth Lagna itself.

Alternatively, death occurs during the rise of the sign where the Lagna is **debilitated**, specifically when the transition of these signs is "pure" <note>free from the interference of other planetary influences</note>.

->End of the 10th Adhyaya.<-

# Chapter Eleven of the Jataka Parijata <note>original: "जातकपारिजाते एकादशोऽध्यायः"</note>

->॥ Now, the Fruits of the Houses ॥<-

->Adhyaya XI.<-

->THE EFFECTS OF THE 1ST AND THE 2ND BHAVAS <term>Houses</term>.<-

> I shall now describe all the various effects—good and otherwise—arising from the different houses <term>Bhavas</term> for men, which are influenced by the strength of the planets and serve as the primary ornaments of the science of astrology. Among the houses, starting with the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, those that are occupied or aspected by **Venus**, **Mercury**, **Jupiter**, or the **Lord of the House** <term>Bhavapathi</term>, and are neither aspected by nor associated with other planets, will produce fortunate results. ॥1॥

<margin>Compare with the *Uttarakalamrita* <note>A classic 17th-century astrological treatise</note>:
"Whichever house is aspected by or joined with its own lord or with benefic <note>auspicious</note> planets, that house shall experience prosperity. One must predict the loss of that house if it is similarly influenced by malefic <note>inauspicious</note> planets, whether in a birth chart or a question."</margin>

> When the houses, starting with the body <note>the first house or Ascendant</note>, are influenced by auspicious risings and are aspected by their respective house lords, the full prosperity of those houses is said to occur. This prosperity does not exist when the houses are aspected by or joined with malefic planets <term>kheta</term>. ॥ 2 ॥

<summary>This page introduces Chapter Eleven, focusing on how the prosperity of astrological houses is determined by the presence or "aspect" (glance) of beneficial planets and the house's own lord.</summary>
<keywords>Bhavas, Houses, Lagna, Ascendant, Bhavapathi, House Lord, Venus, Mercury, Jupiter, Uttarakalamrita, planetary aspects, malefic planets, benefic planets</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "fruits" or outcomes of the twelve astrological houses (bhavas) from the beginning of Chapter Eleven. It specifically elaborates on the conditions—such as planetary placement in specific signs or houses—that lead to the prosperity or the destruction of a house's influence.</meta>

<page-num>710</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Adhyaya XI <note>Chapter 11</note></header>

### Sloka 2.
When the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> and some of the other **houses** <term>bhavas</term> have **auspicious** <term>benefic</term> planets appearing in them or are occupied or aspected by their lords, a wealth of the said **houses** <term>bhavas</term> may be declared to exist, provided they are neither aspected nor occupied by **inauspicious** <term>malefic</term> planets.

---

->A planet in its sign of depression or an enemy’s sign is a destroyer of the house.<-
->But one in its root-trine, its own, its exaltation, or a friend’s sign, promotes the house. ॥ 3 ॥<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "नीचस्थो रिपुराशिस्थः खेटो भावविनाशकः । मूलस्वतुङ्गमित्रस्थो भाववृद्धिकरो भवेत् ॥ ३ ॥"</note>

### Sloka 3.
A planet in its depression <note>the sign where a planet is weakest</note> or inimical sign destroys the **house** <term>bhava</term> that it owns. But in its <term>Moolatrikona</term> <note>a specific favorable zone in a sign, often called the "root-trine"</note>, exaltation, or friendly sign, it advances the same.

<margin>Compare with the *Uttara Parasara* <note>A classical text attributed to the sage Parasara</note>:
Whichever house lord is in an enemy or depression sign, or is combust <note>too close to the sun</note> and does not aspect that house, one should declare a lack of results, laziness, or enmity regarding that house’s nature. 
When the house lord and the **significator** <term>karaka</term> are in their own signs, exaltation, or a friend's sign, or are in each other's signs, or are joined or aspected by benefics, those houses flourish. If they are weak, placed in difficult houses, or joined/aspected by malefics, or are in enemy signs, depression, or combustion, there is loss. 
If any house lord aspects or occupies the house, or is in an exaltation or a friend’s sign, and is aspected by benefics, the house yields its fruit; if the house lord is in a bad position, depressed, in an enemy's house or combust, and is joined or aspected by malefics, it does not.</margin>

---

->When the lord of a house is in the 6th, 12th, or 8th, or a lord of a difficult house is in that house,<-
->the experts say that house is destroyed; if aspected by a benefic, the result is different. ॥ 4 ॥<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "यद्भावनाथो रिपुरिःफरन्ध्रे दुःस्थानपो यद्भावस्थितो वा । तद्भावनाशं कथयन्ति तज्ज्ञाः शुभेक्षितश्चेत्फलमन्यथा स्यात् ॥ ४ ॥"</note>

### Sloka 4.
When a **house** <term>bhava</term> has its lord in the 6th, the 8th, or the 12th <note>these are known as *Dusthanas* or houses of suffering</note>, or is occupied by the lord of one of these three, it suffers annihilation, say those that know.

<summary>This page details the specific planetary conditions—such as placement in signs of exaltation versus depression and the influence of the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses—that determine whether a life area (house) flourishes or is ruined.</summary>
<keywords>house lords, planetary strength, exaltation, depression, Dusthanas, Moolatrikona, Jataka Parijata, Uttara Parasara</keywords>

--- Page 90 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of Chapter 11, focusing on Slokas 5 and 6. It follows the discussion from page 710 regarding how the placement and relationships of "house lords" determine whether an astrological house (bhava) flourishes or decays.</meta>

<page-num>711</page-num>
<header>Slokas 5-6 Chapter 11 711</header>

...the properties of a **house** <term>bhava</term>. If such a **house** <term>bhava</term> be aspected <note>viewed by the astrological influence of another planet</note> by a **favorable** <term>benefic</term> planet, the effect will be different.

### Sloka 5. 
If the lord of a **house** <term>bhava</term> occupying a **quadrant** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, considered pillars of strength</note> or **trine** <term>Trikona</term> <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses, considered auspicious</note> position from the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> possesses abundant strength—being either aspected by **favorable** <term>benefic</term> planets or placed in its **exaltation** <note>the sign where a planet is at its most powerful</note> and other **divisional charts** <term>Vargas</term>—that **house** <term>bhava</term>, say the wise astrologers, is in a flourishing condition.

<insert>From the *Uttara Parashara* <note>original: "उत्तरपाराशरे"</note></insert>

> If the lord of the house and the **significator** <term>karaka</term> <note>a planet that naturally represents a specific life area, regardless of the chart</note> are in their own houses or signs of exaltation, or if they have exchanged places; if they are situated between benefic planets or are in each other's signs, the house will flourish. However, if they are mutual enemies, or if they are in their sign of **depression** <note>the sign where a planet is weakest</note>, or are **combust** <note>rendered invisible or weak by being too close to the Sun</note>, or are joined with **unfavorable** <term>malefic</term> planets or in malefic subdivisions, astrologers account it as the destruction of that house.
<note>original Sanskrit: "भावेश: कारकोऽपि स्वगृहगुणगतौ तुङ्गगौ वा मिथस्थौ... भावनाशं गणन्ति ॥"</note>

### Sloka 6.
Astrologers pronounce the strength of a **house** <term>bhava</term> (such as the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>) to be ample and thoroughly beneficent when a **trine** <term>Trikona</term>, the 4th, the 7th, or the 10th place from it is occupied by an auspicious planet or the **house lord** <term>bhavapa</term> <note>the planet that rules the sign on the cusp of that house</note> and is unoccupied or unaspected by **unfavorable** <term>malefic</term> planets. 

A **house** <term>bhava</term> suffers decay when the positions referred to above are differently occupied and aspected <note>meaning, occupied by malefics or devoid of its lord's support</note>. The effect of a **house** <term>bhava</term> will be mixed when the positions noted above are occupied or aspected by both **favorable** <term>benefic</term> and **unfavorable** <term>malefic</term> planets promiscuously.

<summary>This page defines the criteria for the "flourishing" or "decay" of an astrological house, emphasizing that placement in quadrants or trines, and the strength of the house lord, are essential for positive results.</summary>
<keywords>bhava, Lagna, Kendra, Trikona, house lord, bhavapa, Uttara Parashara, Varga, benefic, malefic</keywords>

--- Page 91 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter 11 of the Jataka Parijata, transitioning from the flourishing of houses (Slokas 5-6) to the specific planetary conditions that cause a house to lose its strength or fail to produce results (Slokas 7-10).</meta>

<page-num>712</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata Chapter 11</header>

<warning>Image is slightly grainy with some ink bleed-through from the reverse side of the page.</warning>

->When the lord of a house is in the place of destruction, or hidden by the sun’s rays,<-
->Or in its sign of depression or an enemy's sign, without the view or company of favorable planets,<-
->The assembly of sages declares the total destruction of that house; even if joined by other planets,<-
->It is unable to produce its proper results for the physical self and other houses in order. || 7 ||<-

### Sloka 7. 
Regarding the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> and the other **astrological houses** <term>bhavas</term> examined in succession, whichever house has its lord occupying the 8th place from that house, or is **combust** <note>original: "दिवाकरकरैश्छन्न" - obscured by the solar rays, rendering the planet invisible and weak</note>, or is in **depression** <note>the specific zodiac sign where a planet's power is at its lowest</note>, or is in an **inimical house** <note>the sign belonging to a planet's natural enemy</note>, while no **favorable** <term>benefic</term> planets **aspect** <note>view through astrological influence</note> or are associated with it, the astrologers declare the total destruction of such a house. The lord of the house concerned, even if associated with other planets, is incapable of producing any good effect.

->When the lord of a house is in a difficult position, an enemy's sign, eclipsed, or weak,<-
->Those planets residing within that house are unable to bring about its prosperity. || 8 ||<-

### Sloka 8. 
Planets occupying a **house** <term>bhava</term> cannot advance or improve it when its lord is in a **difficult position** <term>Duḥsthāna</term> <note>specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses from the house in question</note>, or in an inimical house, or in a state of **eclipse** <note>combustion by the sun</note> or **impotence** <note>original: "दुर्बले" (durbalē) - lacking strength due to various astronomical factors</note>.

->Even if a planet is poorly placed or in a sub-division of a malefic, enemy, or its depression,<-
->If it occupies its own, exaltation, or a friendly sign or sub-division, or is aspected by benefics, it is good. || 9 ||<-

### Sloka 9. 
A planet may even be badly placed or occupy a **nine-fold sub-division** <term>Navamsa</term> <note>a 3°20' division of a sign used for fine-tuning planetary strength</note> owned by a **harmful** <term>malefic</term> planet, an enemy, or its own sign of depression; it will nevertheless prove beneficial when the **sub-division** <term>Navamsa</term> or **zodiac sign** <term>Rasi</term> occupied by it is its own, or that of its **exaltation** <note>the sign where a planet is at its peak power</note>, or that of a **friendly planet**, as well as when **favorable** <term>benefic</term> planets aspect it.

->If the lord of the sign occupied by the house-lord is in a difficult place, the house is weak.<-
->If he is in his exaltation, friend's, or own sign, the wise say the house is strengthened. || 10 ||<-

### Sloka 10. 
If the **dispositor** <note>the lord of the sign where a house-lord is currently located</note> is in a **difficult position** <term>duḥsthe</term>, the wise say the **house** <term>bhava</term> is weak. However, if that dispositor is in its **exaltation**, its **friendly sign**, or its **own sign**, the learned astrologer should declare the **strengthening** <term>bhava-pushtim</term> of that house.

<summary>This page details the conditions under which an astrological house is destroyed or weakened, specifically focusing on the placement of the house-lord in difficult houses (6th, 8th, 12th) or its state of combustion. It also explains how a planet's strength in divisional charts (Navamsa) can mitigate poor placement in the main chart.</summary>

<keywords>Jataka Parijata, bhava, house lord, combustion, depression, Duhsthana, Navamsa, dispositor, astrological strength</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the 11th chapter of Jataka Parijata, shifting focus from the factors that destroy a house’s influence to the specific planetary configurations and mathematical calculations required to determine when a house flourishes or produces full results.</meta>

<page-num>713</page-num>
<header>Slokas 10-12 Chapter Eleven 713</header>

### Sloka 10.
When the planet owning the sign occupied by the lord of a **house** <term>bhava</term> is badly placed, the house becomes weak. But when the planet referred to above is in its **exaltation** <note>the zodiac sign where a planet's influence is at its strongest</note>, in a **friendly sign** or in its **own house**, the astrologer may pronounce the house to be flourishing.

->Those planets who have gone to the 11th, 2nd, or 3rd signs from a house,<-
->And are friends of the lord of that house, or the lord of the house-lord's exaltation sign,<-
->They produce the flourishing and purity of that house,<-
->Provided they are not **combust** <note>original: "मूढ" (mudha) — literally 'stupefied', referring to a planet being so close to the Sun that its light and power are lost</note>, in an **enemy's sign**, or in **depression**.<-

### Sloka 11. 
When the lord of a house, the friends of the **house-lord** <term>bhavapa</term>, and the lord of the exaltation sign of the **house-lord** occupy the 11th, the 2nd, and the 3rd places in respect to the house, those planets advance the strength of the house, provided they be not eclipsed or in an unfriendly sign or in **depression** <note>the specific zodiac sign where a planet's influence is at its weakest</note>.

->Uttara Parashara<-
<note>A classical Sanskrit astrological treatise attributed to the tradition of the sage Parashara.</note>

If the **significators** <term>karakas</term> and the lords of the houses are in the **angles** <note>houses 1, 4, 7, and 10</note>, the **trines** <note>houses 1, 5, and 9</note>, the 11th, or the 2nd house, and the house-lords have reached their respective houses; and if the lords of the 12th, 8th, and 6th houses are not in difficult placements—or even if they are in the 12th, 6th, or 8th, but are in their exaltation, friendly, or own signs and are mutually well-disposed—then indeed great prosperity results. Otherwise, the effect is different.

->A planet whose position is equal to the degree of the house-point<-
->Truly produces the full results arising from that house.<-
->When the planet is at a distance less than or greater than that point,<-
->The result should be evaluated here by the **rule-of-three** process. || 12 ||<-

### Sloka 12. 
A planet produces the full effect of the **house** <term>bhava</term> in which it is when its distance from the **house-junction** <term>bhavasandhi</term> is equal to that of the **house-degree** <term>bhavamsa</term> <note>original: "भावांश" — the midpoint or most effective point of an astrological house</note>. When the planet has a less or greater distance from a house-junction than a **bhavamsa**, its effect must be ascertained by a **rule-of-three** <note>original: "त्रैराशिकेना" (trairashika) — a mathematical proportion used to calculate the percentage of a planet's strength based on its proximity to the house center</note> process.

->NOTES.<-

This sloka is taken from the **Sripatipaddhati** <note>A foundational 11th-century manual on the mathematical calculation of planetary and house strengths, written by the astronomer Sripati</note>.

<sig>1</sig>

<summary>This page details the conditions under which an astrological house flourishes, emphasizing the placement of the house-lord's friends and the mathematical calculation of a planet's "full effect" based on its proximity to the house midpoint.</summary>
<keywords>Bhava, Bhavapa, Bhavamsa, Bhavasandhi, Uttara Parashara, Sripatipaddhati, rule-of-three calculation, house strength, planetary exaltation</keywords>

Having previously established the general rules for house strength, the text now begins a detailed examination of the First House—the Lagna—which represents the self and the physical body.</meta>

<header>In the Jataka Parijata, Chapter 11</header>

## ## Now, the Results of the Ascendant House

->**The physical body, its complexion, form, and distinguishing marks; fame, character, social standing, and general happiness or misery; travels abroad, splendor, strength, and weakness—these are the fruits of the first house, as declared by the wise.** — Sloka 13<-

*Sloka 13.* <note>original: "शरीरवर्णाकृतिलक्षणानि..."</note> The body, its hue, its form, its characteristics, fame, qualities, happiness or unhappiness, residence abroad, splendor, strength and weakness are the products, say the wise astrologers, of the 1st **house** <term>bhava</term>.

->NOTES.<-

This verse also appears in the *Jataka Tattva* <note>OCR originally read "जातिकरत", likely a transcription error for this famous astrological compendium</note>. In that version, the third **quarter** <term>pada</term> of the verse reads: "Affliction by the fire of anxiety during travel" <note>original: "प्रवासचिन्तानलदुर्बलानि" (pravāsa-cintānala-durbalāni)</note>.

->**A person will be long-lived, honored by authorities, and happy if the Ascendant falls in the sign of a benefic planet. If the Ascendant is also aspected by its own planetary lord, the person will be wealthy, sharp-witted, and bring great honor to their family.** — Sloka 14<-

*Sloka 14.* <note>original: "नरश्चिरायुर्नृपपूजितः सुखी..."</note> The more auspicious the **rising sign** <term>Lagna</term> is at a person's birth, the longer will he live; the more will he command the respect of the rulers and the happier in consequence will he be. And if the rising sign be also **aspected** <note>the 'glance' or mathematical influence of one planet upon a specific area of the zodiac</note> by its lord, he will be a master of riches, quite a genius and a great credit to his family.

->NOTES.<-

The following additional information regarding length of life is extracted from the *Uttara Parashara* <note>A classical Sanskrit astrological treatise attributed to the lineage of the sage Parashara</note>:

<note>The following is a translation of the Sanskrit verse provided in the OCR:</note>
When the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, the lord of the Ascendant, the Moon, or the lord of the sign occupied by the Moon are in their **exaltation** <note>the zodiac sign where a planet is most powerful</note> or in a friendly sign, a full life is indicated. This is especially true if the Ascendant is aspected by or joined with very auspicious planets. However, if one of these factors is joined by a **malefic** <note>a planet like Mars or Saturn that typically indicates challenges</note> planet, the lifespan is medium. If there is a heavy concentration of malefic influences, the wise say the life will be very short. 

Furthermore, if the lords of the 1st and 8th houses are in their own signs, in a friend's house, or in their exaltation, or if they are aspected by or joined with a benefic planet, a full life is granted. The wealth, status, happiness, physical health, and complexion of the person will flourish in accordance with the strength and luster of these house lords.

<vocab>Lagna-bhava-phalam (results of the first house), Jataka Parijata, Sloka (verse), Uttara-Parashara, Bhava (astrological house), Pada (verse quarter), Lagna (Ascendant/Rising sign), astrology</vocab>

<summary>This page defines the primary significations of the First House (Lagna), which include the physical body, character, and longevity, and details how the influence of benefic planets and the house lord can ensure a long, prosperous, and honorable life.</summary>
<keywords>Ascendant, First House, longevity, physical appearance, prosperity, Lagna, house lord, Jataka Parijata, Uttara Parashara</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the 11th chapter of the Jataka Parijata. While the previous page introduced the general significance of the First House (Lagna), this section delves deeper into the calculation of longevity (Ayu) and the determination of physical appearance based on planetary subdivisions.</meta>

<page-num>715</page-num>
<header>Chapter Eleven</header>
<sig>IS 15</sig>

If there is a balance of influences, a person with many sins will reap little reward; if malefic influences are in abundance, the result is the opposite.

When the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lagnathesa</term> is in its highest state of exaltation, joined with or aspected by benefic planets, and when Mercury, Jupiter, or Venus <note>original: "चान्द्रेयादि" (Chandreyadi)—the son of the Moon (Mercury) and the other benefics.</note> are in conjunction with the Moon, one should declare a full span of life. If these conditions are fewer and mixed with malefic influences in a single house, the life is half or middle-length. If the two are equal, and many planets provide malefic influence, then the life of such men is brief.

Wise men declare a life of one hundred years when the Lord of the Ascendant is at its highest exaltation and the **first house** <term>Tanubhava</term> is aspected by a benefic planet, provided the Moon is also associated with and aspected by benefics.

If **Saturn** <term>Shani</term> is an enemy to the Lord of the Ascendant, one should predict a short life. If they are neutral, a medium life is spoken of; if they are friends, a long life should be predicted.

When Saturn, the Moon, and the lords of the 8th, 10th, and 1st houses are mutually in exaltation, situated in friendly signs, or occupy houses aspected and joined by benefics, the life is abundant. If one of these is joined by a malefic, it is slightly less; if there is a balance of malefic and benefic, the life is medium; and if malefics predominate, the life is declared to be short, in that order.

When the Lord of the Ascendant and the **Son of the Sun** <note>original: "दिवाकरसुतः" (Divakarasuta)—an epithet for Saturn.</note> are both strong and situated in each other's friendly or exalted signs, and if they are neither aspected by nor joined with malefics—and the same applies to the Moon and the Ascendant—astrologers seeking to examine longevity should weigh their relative strengths. The noble-minded understand that the life of the person born under these conditions will be of medium length.

An excellent category of friendship results in the best life; neutral or mixed enmity results in a medium life. The lowest state of friendship or enmity is poor, and a category of pure enmity brings death.

---

->**The physical form, appearance, and distinguishing marks are determined from the lord of the Navamsa of the Ascendant or from the planet occupying the first house. The complexion is declared from the lord of the Navamsa occupied by the Moon. All good and bad fortunes are known from the Ascendant.** — Sloka 15<-

**Sloka 15.** <note>original: "लग्नांशपाद्यैर्यद्युतग्रहाद्वा..."</note> An astrologer may declare the nature and peculiar features of a person from the lord of the rising **Navamsa** <term>a 1/9th division of a zodiac sign</term> at his birth or from a powerful planet occupying the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>. His hue should be guessed from the lord of the Navamsa occupied by the Moon. All auspicious and inauspicious outcomes are determined through the Ascendant.

<summary>This page outlines the astrological formulas for determining a person's lifespan (longevity) based on the relationship between the Ascendant lord and Saturn, and transitions into predicting physical appearance using the Navamsa subdivisions.</summary>
<keywords>longevity, Ayu, Saturn, Navamsa, physical appearance, Ascendant Lord, planetary friendship, Shani</keywords>

<page-num>716</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter XI</header>

<note>This section continues the discussion that</note> good and evil in the life of the person is to be ascertained through the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>.

### NOTES.
*compare with Chapter III, verse 76, mentioned above.*

If the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lagnatha</term> is in a trine <term>Trikona</term> or an angular <term>Kendra</term> house, is associated with or aspected by benefic planets, is located in the house of a benefic planet, and is possessed of great strength, the person’s fame will reach as far as the four oceans. || 16 ||

<note>original Sanskrit: त्रिकोणकेन्द्रे यदि लग्नाथे शुभान्विते शोभनवीक्षिते वा । शुभग्रहागारगते बलाढ्ये चतुःसमुद्रान्तयशः समेति ॥ १६ ॥</note>

*Verse 16.* If at a person's birth the lord of the rising sign being in great strength in the house of a benefic planet occupy a **Kendra** <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses.</note> or **Trikona** <note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses.</note> position from the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> and be aspected by or associated with auspicious planets, the fame of the person born will extend up to the four oceans.

### NOTES.
This verse <term>sloka</term> appears in the *Jataka Ratna*. The reading there uses the phrase "occupying the subdivision of a benefic planet" <note>original: "शुभग्रहांशगते" (shubha-graha-amsha-gate)</note> instead of "occupying the house of a benefic planet" <note>original: "शुभग्रहागारगते" (shubha-graha-agara-gate)</note>.

*Compare also the following verse from the same work:*

If the Lord of the Ascendant is joined with a benefic, is in its highest state of exaltation, is situated in an angle or trine, and is aspected by benefics; or if it is joined with the Lord of the 10th house <note>The house of profession and action.</note> or resides in its own home, the person attains good character, fame, wealth, grain, and a long life.

<note>original Sanskrit: लग्नाधिपे शुभयुते यदि तुङ्गभागे केन्द्रत्रिकोणसहिते शुभदृष्टियुक्ते । कर्माधिपेन सहिते यदि वा स्वगेहे सद्भावकीर्तिधनधान्यचिरायुुरेति ॥</note>

A person becomes obscure if the Lord of the Ascendant is in the house of a malefic, occupies the 6th, 8th, or 12th house, and is influenced by malignant planets. || 17 ||

<note>original Sanskrit: होराधिनाथे रिपुरन्ध्ररिःफे पापान्विते पापनिरोक्षिते वा । पापग्रहाणां भवनोपयाते जातोऽप्रकाशो भवतीह मर्त्यः ॥ १७ ॥</note>

*Verse 17.* A person becomes obscure if, at his birth, the lord of the rising sign being in the house of a malefic planet, occupies the 6th, 8th or 12th place from the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> and is aspected by or associated with a malignant planet.

### NOTES.
1. For the first quarter <term>pada</term> of the verse, the *Jataka Ratna* reads: "The Lord of the Ascendant being in the 8th house and aspected..." <note>original: "लग्नेश्वरे रन्ध्रगते च दृष्टे" (lagneshvare randhra-gate cha drishte)</note>.

When the Lord of the **House of Fame** <note>The 10th house.</note> is in the house of the Ascendant, the person born is himself famous; if it is in the **2nd house** <term>vitta-sthana</term>, he is possessed of exceptional fame, especially if the planet is in its own subdivision of exaltation.

<note>original Sanskrit: कीर्तिस्थानपतौ विलग्नभवने जातः स्वयं कीर्तिमान् वित्तस्थे तु विशेषकीर्तिसहितः स्वोच्चादिवर्गान्विते ।</note>

<vocab>Lagna (Ascendant), Sloka (Verse), Kendra (Angular house), Trikona (Trine house), benefic planet (auspicious influence), malefic planet (difficult influence), Jataka Parijata (Birth-fame-flower-set), Jatakaratna (Gem of Birth-horoscopy)</vocab>

<summary>This page describes how the placement and strength of the Lord of the Ascendant determine whether a person will achieve global fame or remain obscure. High status is linked to positions in angular or trine houses, while obscurity is linked to the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses.</summary>
<keywords>fame, social status, Lord of the Ascendant, Jataka Parijata, Jataka Ratna, Kendra, Trikona, planetary aspects</keywords>

This page transitions into determining a person’s fame, social standing, and general success in life based on the placement of the Lord of the Ascendant in various houses and its relationship with benefic or malefic planets.</meta>

<page-num>717</page-num>
<header>Verses 18-19 Chapter Eleven 717</header>

## If the body is worn out by the miseries of restless wandering, or if one is born a wicked person; 
## when placed in an angle or a trine and joined with a benefic planet, comfort in travel arises. || 18 ||

*Verse 18.* If the lord of the **House of Fame** <term>Keerthibhava</term>, which is the 10th house, be in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, the person born will become famous. If that planet be in the 2nd **house** <term>bhava</term> and has attained its exaltation and other benefic **divisional strengths** <term>vargas</term>, he will have remarkable celebrity. If the planet be in a bad place <note>Usually referring to the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, known as Dusthanas.</note>, he will be exhausted by ceaseless travelling or will be a knave. But if the planet occupy an **angular** <term>Kendra</term> or **trine** <term>Trikona</term> house in conjunction with a benefic planet, the person will have ease in travelling.

->NOTES.<-

This verse <term>sloka</term> is also found in the *Jataka Ratna* <note>A significant historical treatise on Vedic astrology.</note>.

## If the lord of the Ascendant is in the fifth or if joined with the lord of the house of children,
## or in the ninth, or if in the Ascendant with the lord of the ninth, the father is illustrious.
## If the lord of the house of brothers is in the house of the Ascendant, and the significator and lord of brothers
## are together and possessed of strength, then his brother is famous. || 19 ||

*Verse 19.* If the lord of the 1st **house** <term>bhava</term> occupy the 5th or the 9th, or being in conjunction with the lord of the 5th or the 9th occupy the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, the father of the person born will be a famous man. If the lord of the 3rd **house** <term>bhava</term> be in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> or if the **significator** <term>Karaka</term> <note>Specifically Mars, who governs siblings.</note> as well as the lord of the **House of Brothers** <term>Bhratrubhava</term> be together in one house and possess strength, a brother of the person born will become renowned.

->NOTES.<-

For the latter half of this verse <term>sloka</term>, the *Jataka Ratna* provides the following:

## If the Ascendant is in a fixed sign, joined with a benefic planet, and in the divisional charts of a gentle planet,
## the one born shall obtain all happiness and much wealth through them.
<note>original Sanskrit: लग्ने स्थिरभे शुभग्रहयुते सौम्यग्रहैर्वर्गे जातः सर्वसुखं धनं च बहुलं संप्राप्नुयात्तैर्युतः ॥</note>

<summary>This page details astrological combinations (Yogas) that determine the fame of a person, their father, and their brothers based on the positioning of the Lords of the 1st, 3rd, and 10th houses.</summary>
<keywords>House of Fame, Ascendant, house, angular house, trine house, divisional strengths, significator, House of Brothers, Jataka Ratna</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the 11th chapter of the Jataka Parijata, which focuses on the analysis of the First House (Lagna). The previous page established criteria for determining the length of life (longevity) based on the strength of the Lord of the Ascendant and the influence of Saturn. This page transitions into determining a person’s fame, social standing, and general success in life based on the placement of the Lord of the Ascendant in various houses and its relationship with benefic or malefic planets.</meta>

<page-num>718</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata Adh. XI.</header>

->If the Lord of Knowledge or Mercury is in the Ascendant, in the house of happiness, or joined with the Lord of the Ascendant;<-
->And if strong and free from the gaze of malefic planets, the person born will be famous for their learning. || 20 ||<-

*Verse 20* When the lord of the 2nd **house** <term>bhava</term> or Mercury <note>The Sanskrit text uses "Chandrasunau," meaning "Son of the Moon," a traditional name for the planet Mercury.</note> is in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> or when the 2nd **house** <term>bhava</term> is occupied by the lord of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> and when the **house** <term>bhava</term> in either case is strong and free from malefic **aspect** <note>In astrology, an "aspect" is the angular influence one planet has upon another.</note>, the person born will become famous for his learning.

---

->NOTES.<-

**Lord of Knowledge** <term>Vidyadhipa</term> here means the lord of the 2nd **house** <term>bhava</term>; (*see Verse 49 below*) and not that of the 4th **house** <term>bhava</term> referred to in XII—59 *below*.

The same remark applies to the term **Happiness** <term>Sukha</term> <note>The word "Sukha" is a technical term for the 4th house in a birth chart, which governs domestic comfort and education.</note>.

---

->If the lords of fortune and the Ascendant are in the 9th and 1st, or the lords of the 1st and 9th occupy the 9th and 1st;<-
->If they are aspected by or joined with the one worshipped by the King of the Gods, the person attains long-lasting luck. || 21 ||<-

*Verse 21.* If the lords of the 9th and the 1st **houses** <term>bhavas</term> from the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> be in their respective **houses** <term>bhavas</term> or if the lords of the 1st and the 9th occupy the 9th and the 1st respectively, and if the planets in their several positions be aspected by or associated with Jupiter <note>The Sanskrit "Surendravandya" refers to Jupiter as the guru or preceptor of the gods.</note>, the person born will enjoy good luck for a long time.

---

->When a planet in the 12th from the Lord of the Ascendant is in its exaltation, a friendly sign, or its own sign;<-
->Or if a friendly planet is in that sign, the person's dwelling will be in the land of their birth. || 22 ||<-

*Verse 22.* A person will abide in the place of his birth when a planet occupying the 12th place from that of the lord of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> happens to be in **exaltation** <note>The position where a planet is at its maximum strength.</note> or in a friendly house or is aspected by a planet that is friendly or is in **exaltation** <note>An "exalted" planet is considered to be functioning at its highest potential.</note> or when that **Zodiac Sign** <term>Rashi</term> is occupied by a friendly planet.

---

->If the Lord of the Ascendant is in the house of the Lord of the 12th, or if the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the 12th are enemies, debilitated, or weak;<-
->The person born goes to a foreign land, especially if aspected by Mercury and Jupiter.<-

<vocab>Vidyadhipa, bhava, Lagna, Mercury, Jupiter, Rashi, Jataka Parijata</vocab>

<summary>This page outlines astrological conditions for a person to achieve fame through learning, attain long-term good fortune, and determine whether they will remain in their birthplace or move to a foreign land.</summary>
<keywords>Vedic astrology, Vidyadhipa, Lagna, Jupiter, 9th house, 12th house, learning, fame, travel, foreign lands</keywords>

--- Page 98 ---
<meta>This page continues Chapter 11 of the *Jataka Parijata*, a classic Sanskrit text on Vedic astrology. Having previously discussed learning and fame, the text now shifts to determining whether a person will reside in their native country or travel abroad, and the quality of the places where they will live based on the strength of the Lord of the 12th house relative to the Lord of the Ascendant.</meta>

<page-num>710</page-num>
<header>Verses 23–25 Chapter Eleven 710</header>

->If the Lord of the 12th from the Lord of the Ascendant stays in that same place, but is eclipsed by the rays of the Sun—<-
->The person will wander in small villages; yet if that Lord is strong, they may reside in a wealthy town. || 23 ||<-

*Verse 23.* When the lord of the 12th <note>original: "Vyayapa"</note> house from the position occupied by the lord of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna: The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of birth</term> is the latter's enemy, or is in a state of depression <note>A planet is "in depression" when it is in the zodiac sign where its influence is weakest.</note> or is otherwise weak, the person born goes to a foreign country. He will abide permanently there if the **Lord of the 12th** <term>Vyayapa: The ruler of the house of loss and foreign residency</term> referred to above be aspected by Venus in the capacity of a friend. His haunt will be a small township if the same **Lord of the 12th** <term>Vyayapa</term> be eclipsed by the Sun; if the **Lord of the 12th** <term>Vyayapa</term> be possessed of strength, the haunt will be a wealthy municipality.

---

->If the Lord of the 12th from the Lord of the Ascendant is in a Square or Trine from the Ascendant house,<-
->In a friend’s house, his own, or his exaltation, and flanked by auspicious planets on both sides, that man—<-
->Will travel to lands pleasing to the heart. If gazed upon by the Preceptor of the Gods, the Moon, or Venus,<-
->He reaches divine regions; but if that Lord is an enemy in the second house, he dwells in his birth-land. || 24 ||<-

*Verse 24.* When the lord of the 12th place from that occupied by the lord of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> is in a **Square** <term>Kendra: The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, considered pillars of the chart</term> or **Trine** <term>Trikona: The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses, considered very auspicious</term> from the 1st **house** <term>Bhava</term>, and in a **Zodiac Sign** <term>Rasi</term> which is a friend's house, its own, or its exaltation sign and has auspicious planets on each side <note>This "flanking" is known as *Subha Kartari Yoga*, a protective configuration.</note>, the person born will be a sojourner in regions pleasing to the heart. If the **Lord of the 12th** <term>Vyayapa</term> referred to above be aspected <note>An "aspect" is the influence one planet casts upon another from a specific distance.</note> by **Jupiter** <note>original: "Diticharacharyendu" - refers here to Jupiter as the teacher of the gods.</note>, the **Moon**, or **Venus**, he goes to a charmingly beautiful land; but if this **Lord of the 12th** <term>Vyayapa</term> occupy the 2nd place from the lord of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> and be unfriendly to the latter, the person will abide in his native land.

---

->Fortune in foreign lands occurs if the Ascendant is a moveable sign, its lord is in a moveable sign, and gazed upon by moveable planets.<-
->In a fixed sign, one stays in their own country with much fortune; with fixed planets, one shall be endowed with great wealth. || 25 ||<-

*Verse 25.* When a moveable sign <note>Moveable signs include Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn; they indicate change and travel.</note> is the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <unclear>and its lord is likewise in a moveable sign and aspected by planets occupying moveable signs, the person will find fortune in foreign lands. Conversely, if the Ascendant is a fixed sign (such as Taurus or Leo), the person remains in their home country, attaining great wealth and success through the influence of fixed planets.</unclear>

<summary>This page details the astrological conditions for living abroad versus remaining in one's native country, focusing on the relationship between the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the 12th house. It also distinguishes between living in small villages or wealthy cities based on planetary strength and the "moveable" or "fixed" nature of the signs.</summary>
<keywords>Ascendant, Lagna, Vyayapa, 12th house, foreign travel, native land, Kendra, Trikona, moveable signs, fixed signs, Jataka Parijata</keywords>

--- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page continues the Eleventh Chapter of the *Jataka Parijata*, focusing on the influence of the Ascendant (Lagna) and its ruling planet on a person's social standing, physical health, and whether they will prosper at home or abroad.</meta>

<page-num>720</page-num>
<header>In the *Jataka Parijata*, Chapter XI</header>

...and its lord is in a <term>Moveable Sign</term> <note>original: "Chara Rasi"; these are Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn, associated with change and movement.</note> and aspected by planets occupying <term>Moveable Signs</term>, the person born will have his fortune in a foreign country. When an <term>Immoveable Sign</term> <note>original: "Sthira Rasi"; these are Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius, associated with stability.</note> is the <term>Ascendant</term> <term>Lagna: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth.</term> and its lord is also in an <term>Immoveable Sign</term> and aspected by planets occupying <term>Immoveable Signs</term>, the person will be in his own country, exceedingly prosperous and possessed of abundant wealth.

->NOTES.<-

This <term>sloka</term> <note>A Sanskrit verse form.</note> appears also in the *<term>Jataka Ratna</term>* <note>An influential classical treatise on gems and astrology.</note>.

<note>original: "होराधीशे पापखेटे रिपुस्थे पापक्षेत्रे भानुपुत्रेण युक्ते । शूद्रप्रायो राहुणा केतुना वा जातश्चाण्डालोऽथवा नीचतुल्यः ॥२६॥"</note>
*Verse 26.* When the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is a malefic planet <note>A planet with a naturally harsh influence, such as Mars or Saturn.</note> and occupies the 6th place from the <term>Ascendant</term> being associated with **Saturn** in a malefic sign, the person born will be like a <term>Sudra</term> <note>Historically the laboring class; here it implies a life of manual service or lower social standing.</note>. And if **Rahu** or **Ketu** <term>Lunar Nodes: The points where the moon's path crosses the ecliptic, often considered shadow planets in Vedic astrology.</term> take the place of **Saturn** in the above, the person will be an <term>Outcaste</term> <note>original: "Chandala"; historically referring to those outside the traditional social order, suggesting someone of very low social status or base habits.</note> or some equally low person in his habits.

<note>original: "लग्नाधिपे शोभनराशिभुक्ते बलोपयावे तनुपुष्टिमेति । लग्नस्थखेटे रिपुनीचभागे दुःस्थानपे देहसुखं न याति ॥ २७ ॥"</note>
*Verse 27.* When the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is in an auspicious sign and is possessed of **strength**, the person born will be in good ease having a command of all bodily comforts. When the planet in the <term>Ascendant</term> is in a depression <note>The sign where a planet's influence is at its weakest.</note> or inimical <term>Navamsa</term> <note>A one-ninth sub-division of a sign used to calculate a planet's hidden strength.</note> and happens to be the lord of an <term>Evil House</term> <term>Dusthbana: Specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, which are associated with suffering and loss.</term>, the person born will lack bodily comforts.

<margin>Compare with the *Jataka Ratna*</margin>
<note>original: "लग्नेश्वरः क्रूरसमन्वितश्चेज्जातस्य सौख्यादि विनाशमेति । तादृक्फले नारागतेऽपि तस्य शुभेसितश्चेत्फलमन्यथा स्यात् ॥"</note>
If the Lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is associated with a cruel or malefic planet, the happiness and prosperity of the person born is destroyed. Even if such a negative result is indicated, if that Lord is aspected by a benefic <note>A planet with a positive, supportive influence.</note> planet, the result will be different <note>meaning the negative influence is cancelled or mitigated.</note>.

<summary>This page discusses how the placement and strength of the Ascendant's ruler determine a person's physical comfort, social status, and whether their success occurs at home or abroad. It specifically notes that malefic influences can lead to a life of service or hardship, while benefic aspects provide bodily ease.</summary>
<keywords>Ascendant (Lagna), Moveable Signs, Immoveable Signs, malefic planets, Sudra, Rahu, Ketu, Dusthbana, physical health, social status</keywords>

<page-num>721</page-num>
<header>Verses 28-30: Chapter Eleven</header>

<note>original: "विलग्नदर्शी तनुपो विलग्नगस्त्रयोऽपि षष्ठाष्टमरिःफराशिपाः । सपत्ननीचोपगताश्च दुर्बला यदि स्वपाके न फलं प्रकुर्युः ॥ २८ ॥"</note>
**Verse 28.** If the three planets connected to the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth.</note>—namely, the one aspecting it, the one ruling it, and the one occupying it—are the lords of the 6th, the 8th, or the 12th <term>houses</term> <note>bhavas: These three houses (dusthanas) are traditionally associated with challenges, illness, and loss.</note>, and if these planets are weak, located in inimical signs, or in their signs of <term>depression</term> <note>debilitation: the zodiac sign where a planet’s influence is at its weakest.</note>, they will produce no beneficial results during the ripening of their respective <term>planetary periods</term> <note>dasa: cycles of time in a person's life ruled by specific planets.</note>.

<note>original: "लग्ने जलर्क्षे शुभखैचरैर्युक्ते तनोः स्थौल्यमुदाहरन्ति । लग्नाधिपे तोयखगे बलाढ्ये सौम्यान्विते तत्तनुपुष्टिमाहुः ॥ २९ ॥"</note>
**Verse 29.** When the <term>Ascendant</term> is a <term>watery sign</term> <note>Cancer, Scorpio, or Pisces.</note> and is occupied by <term>benefic</term> <note>planets with a positive influence, like Jupiter or Venus.</note> planets, astrologers state that the person will have a stout or heavy physical build. If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> occupies a <term>watery sign</term>, possesses great strength, and is joined by <term>benefic</term> planets, the person born is said to have a well-nourished and sound constitution.

->centered text<-
NOTES.
->centered text<-

In the third line of this verse, the text *<term>Jataka Tattva</term>* <note>An 18th-century compendium of Sanskrit astrology.</note> uses the reading "occupying a watery sign" <note>original: "तोयगते" (toyagate)</note> instead of "a watery planet" <note>original: "तोयखगे" (toyakhage)</note>.

<note>original: "लग्नाधिपे नाशगते तु शुष्कराशौ तनोः कष्टमतिव कृच्छ्रम् । लग्नाधिपस्यांशपराशिनाथः शुष्कग्रहः स्यात्तनुशुष्कमाहुः ॥ ३० ॥"</note>
**Verse 30.** When the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is placed in the 8th <term>house</term> <note>The house of longevity and hidden things, often associated with physical trials.</note> and in a <term>dry sign</term> <note>Aries, Gemini, Leo, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Aquarius.</note>, astrologers predict that the person will endure great bodily suffering. If the lord of the sign containing the ruler of the <term>Navamsa</term> <note>A one-ninth divisional chart used to see the hidden strength of planets.</note> occupied by the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is a planet ruling a <term>dry sign</term>, the physical result will be emaciation or a very thin frame.

->centered text<-
NOTES.
->centered text<-

Some manuscripts provide an alternative reading regarding the "lord of the sign of the lord of the <term>Ascendant's</term> <term>Navamsa</term>." <note>original: "लग्नांशपस्वांशपराशिनाथः"</note>

<sig>91</sig>

<vocab>Ascendant (Lagna), House (Bhava), Planetary Period (Dasa), Verse (Sloka), Zodiac Sign (Rasi), Ninth-division (Navamsa), Watery Sign, Dry Sign, Emaciation, Stoutness</vocab>

<summary>This page details how the Ascendant's relationship with specific houses and elemental signs (watery vs. dry) determines a person's physical build and health. It notes that weak lords of the 6th, 8th, and 12th houses fail to give results during their active periods if they are poorly placed.</summary>

<page-num>722</page-num>
<header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter XI</header>

->**Verse 31**<-

->If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth, representing the self and the body.</note> is situated between two inimical planets, the person born will live in fear of their enemies.<-
->If <term>Ketu</term> or <term>Rahu</term> <note>the lunar nodes, often associated with karmic challenges or "shadow" influences.</note> occupies the <term>Ascendant</term> while its ruler is placed in a <term>difficult house</term> <note>Dusthana: specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, which govern obstacles and loss.</note>,<-
->the person will suffer from the loss or impairment of a limb during the major <term>planetary period</term> <note>Dasa: a long cycle of time ruled by a specific planet.</note> of the Ascendant’s lord and the sub-period of the lord of the 6th house.<-
->If the lord of the 6th house is located in the <term>Ascendant</term> alongside <term>Rahu</term>, the "King of Serpents," the person will suffer from bodily ulcers.<-

<note>original Sanskrit: "मध्यस्थे रिपुखेटयोस्तनुपतौ जातोऽरिभीतो भवेत्... लग्ने शत्रुपतौ फणिराजयुते देहव्रणं देहिनाम् ॥ ३१ ॥"</note>

->**NOTES**<-

This verse also appears in the <term>Jatakabharana</term> <note>A classic 16th-century astrological treatise by Dhundhiraja.</note>. In the third line of that version, the word *tadadhisha* <note>meaning "the lord of that [house]"</note> is used instead of *tadarisha* <note>meaning "the lord of the enemy [house]"</note> which appears in our current text.

---

->**Verse 32**<-

->If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> occupies a <term>Trine</term> or an <term>Angle</term> <note>Trikona (1st, 5th, 9th) or Kendra (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th): these are the most auspicious and powerful positions in a birth chart.</note> but is devoid of strength, the person born will be prone to illness.<-
->If the ruler of the sign in which the Ascendant lord is placed is itself located in a <term>difficult house</term>, the person will have a weak or frail constitution.<-

<note>original Sanskrit: "खलैर्विहीने यदि लग्ननाथे केन्द्रत्रिकोणे सति रोगभाक् स्यात्... ॥ ३२ ॥"</note>

->**NOTES**<-

Regarding the first half of the verse, a similar <term>yoga</term> <note>a specific planetary combination or "union" that produces a predictable result.</note> leading to the same effect is mentioned in the *Jatakabharana*:

> ->If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is joined by the lord of a <term>difficult house</term> while situated in the <term>Ascendant</term> itself, the person will be subject to disease.<-

---

->**Verse 33**<-

->If the lord of the body <note>the Ascendant lord</note> is joined by a <term>malefic</term> <note>a planet with a harsh influence, like Mars or Saturn.</note> and is placed in the 12th, 6th, or 8th house, physical well-being is destroyed.<-
->If the lord of the body is in a <term>difficult house</term> and the <term>Ascendant</term> is influenced by Saturn, the person will quickly become sickly.<-

<note>original Sanskrit: "सक्रूरो देहपो देहसौख्यहान्त्यारिन्ध्रगः । सार्कीशे देहपे दुःस्थे लग्नस्थे चाशु रोगवान् ॥ ३३ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page describes specific planetary alignments involving the Ascendant lord that lead to physical ailments, fear of enemies, and bodily injuries like ulcers or the loss of limbs.</summary>
<keywords>Ascendant lord, Lagna, Rahu, Ketu, Dusthana, physical health, Jatakabharana, planetary periods, ulcers, constitution</keywords>

--- Page 102 ---
<meta>This page continues the Eleventh Chapter of the Jataka Parijata, which outlines the planetary conditions that determine a person's physical constitution, temperament, and susceptibility to specific ailments. It follows the discussion of how the Ascendant’s lord interacts with beneficial and harmful houses.</meta>

<page-num>729</page-num>
<header>Verses 33-35 Chapter Eleven 729</header>

**Verse 33.** If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon at birth, representing the physical body and self.</note> occupies the 12th, 6th, or 8th <term>house</term> <note>Bhava: an astrological house or sector of the sky.</note> from the Ascendant in conjunction with a <term>malefic planet</term> <note>planets like Mars or Saturn that are traditionally associated with challenges.</note>, it destroys the bodily health of the person born. They will be prone to illness if the lord of the 1st house, in conjunction with the lord of the 6th house, occupies a <term>difficult house</term> <note>Dusthhana: specifically the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses, which govern obstacles, disease, and loss.</note> or the Ascendant itself.

->If a malefic planet is in the <term>Ascendant</term> and the lord of the Ascendant is without strength, the person born will be sickly.<-
->If the lord of the Ascendant is weak, they will have a fretful temper; but they will be free from disease if the lord of the Ascendant is in an <term>Angle</term> or <term>Trine</term>. **Verse 34**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्ने सपापे लग्नेशे बलहीनेऽपि रोगवान् । लग्नेशे दुर्बलो कोपी निर्व्याधिः केन्द्रकोणगे ॥ ३४ ॥"</note>

**Verse 34.** When a malefic planet is in the <term>Ascendant</term> and its ruler is lacking strength, the person born will be sickly. He will be of a fretful temper if the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is weak; however, he will be exempt from ailment if the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is located in a <term>Kendra</term> <note>Kendra: the "angular" or "pivot" houses—1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th—considered the pillars of a chart.</note> or <term>Trikona</term> <note>Trikona: the "trine" houses—1st, 5th, or 9th—associated with good fortune and health.</note>.
<note>The number "31" appearing in the original English transcription is likely a printer's error for "34" based on the preceding Sanskrit verse numbering.</note>

->If the lord of the sign occupied by the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is in the house of destruction, one will be weak.<-
->Houses generally become weak when the lords of the signs they occupy are themselves poorly placed. **Verse 35**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "देहेष्टस्थितराशीशे नाशगे दुर्बलो भवेत् । भावेशाक्रान्तराशीशैर्दुःस्थैर्भावाश्च दुर्बलाः ॥ ३५ ॥"</note>

**Verse 35.** If the lord of the <term>sign</term> <note>Rasi: a zodiac sign.</note> occupied by the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is in the 8th place from the Ascendant, the person will be physically weak. <term>Houses</term> <note>Bhavas: life departments like wealth, family, or career.</note> generally become weak when their lords are located in <term>signs</term> whose owners are themselves badly placed—that is, in a <term>difficult house</term> <note>Duhsthana: the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses.</note>.

->Through the conjunction of the "Serpent" and the "Enemy," or the "Serpent" and "Slow-mover,"<-
->Mars in the <term>Ascendant</term> indicates swelling of the navel, ankle, and testicles. **Verse 36**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "सर्पारियोर्विद्विजौ योगे वा सर्पमन्दयोः । लग्ने कुजे नाभिगुल्फवृषणस्थौल्यमादिशेत् ॥ ३६ ॥"</note>

**Verse 36.** A person may suffer from an enlarged <term>scrotum</term> as a consequence of the conjunction of <term>Rahu</term> <note>the north node of the moon, called "the Serpent."</note> and <term>Mars</term>, or of <term>Rahu</term> and <term>Saturn</term> <note>Manda: the "slow-mover," a common name for Saturn.</note>. When <term>Mars</term> <note>Kuja: the planet Mars.</note> occupies the <term>Ascendant</term>, an astrologer may predict swelling in the navel, the ankle, and the testicles.

->If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> is in the 12th house joined with the lord of the 6th house,<-
->the person has a wife, a very hairy body, a lean frame, and a reddish-fair complexion.<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्नेशे यदि रिःफगे दिनकरस्यारातिनाथान्विते जायावान् बहुरोमवान् कृशतनुः संरक्तगौरद्युतिः ।" Note: 'Riphage' refers to the 12th house; 'Arati-natha' refers to the lord of the 6th or enemy house.</note>

<summary>This page describes various planetary combinations (yogas) that lead to physical weakness, irritability, or specific physical ailments like swelling, while emphasizing that a strong Ascendant lord in an Angle or Trine grants health.</summary>
<keywords>Ascendant lord, physical health, Dusthana, malefic planets, Rahu, Mars, Saturn, medical astrology, planetary strength</keywords>

# <page-num>724</page-num>
## <header>Jataka Parijata, Chapter XI</header>

->When the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, representing the self and the physical body.</note> is joined with the ruler of the sixth house from that sign, the person born has dark-colored limbs. If the lord of the Ascendant is accompanied by <term>Rahu</term> <note>The North Node of the Moon, often associated with shadows, deception, or sudden changes.</note>, they will certainly encounter danger from weapons or poison. **37**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्नर्क्षादरिनाथपेन च युते जातोऽसिताङ्गो यदा । साहौ लग्नपतौ तु शस्त्रनविषादङ्गीर्ति समेति ध्रुवम् ॥ ३७ ॥"</note>

**Verse 37.** If the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> occupies the 12th <term>house</term> <note>Bhava: one of the twelve sectors of the horoscope.</note> and is in conjunction with the lord of the 6th house (counted from the Sun’s position), the person will marry easily, possess significant body hair, have a slender figure, and a complexion combining red and white tones. If the lord of the 6th house from the Ascendant is also in conjunction with the lord of the Ascendant in this same position, the person will have a dark physical appearance; however, if **Rahu** is with the lord of the Ascendant, the individual will surely face the risk of betrayal or poisoning.

---

->When the Ascendant is auspicious and viewed by <term>benefic planets</term> <note>Planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon that traditionally bring positive influences.</note>, there is happiness from childhood; this is not so if malefic planets are joined. One becomes miserable with a multitude of malefic influences; if they are in the Ascendant, they say this lasts from childhood until death. **38**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्ने शुभे शोभनदृष्टियुक्ते बाल्यात्सुखं तन्नहि पापयोगात् । दुःखी भवेत्पापबहुत्वयोगे लग्ने तु बाल्यान्मरणान्तमाहुः ॥ ३८ ॥"</note>

**Verse 38.** A person will enjoy a happy childhood when the **Ascendant** is well-placed and receives the "glance" or aspect of benefic planets, provided there are no <term>malefic planets</term> <note>Planets like Saturn or Mars that are traditionally associated with hardship.</note> located within the Ascendant. But if several malefic planets should be positioned in the Ascendant, the person born will be unhappy from the beginning of their life until the very end.

---

->When Venus has reached <term>Devalokamsa</term> and the lord of the Ascendant is in <term>Gopuramsa</term>, and the Ascendant is viewed by benefic planets, one attains happiness in the middle and concluding portions of life. **39**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "देवलोकांशगे शुक्रे लग्नेशे गोपुरांशके । लग्ने शुभग्रहैर्दृष्टे मध्यान्ते सौख्यमाप्नुयात् ॥ ३९ ॥"</note>

**Verse 39.** A person will experience happiness in the middle and final stages of their life if, at the time of their birth, **Venus** has attained the strength of <term>Devalokamsa</term> <note>A high state of planetary dignity achieved when a planet is in its own or exalted sign in seven of the divisional charts.</note>, the lord of the Ascendant has attained <term>Gopuramsa</term> <note>A state of planetary dignity achieved when a planet is in favorable signs in four of the divisional charts.</note>, and benefic planets aspect the rising sign.

<summary>This page describes how the lord of the Ascendant and various planetary dignities determine a person's physical appearance, susceptibility to poison, and the timing of happiness throughout their life.</summary>
<keywords>Jataka Parijata, Lagna, Rahu, Devalokamsa, Gopuramsa, childhood happiness, physical appearance, astrology, Sanskrit translation</keywords>

# <page-num>725</page-num>
## <header>Verses 40-44, Chapter Eleven</header>

->When a <term>benefic</term> <note>A planet naturally inclined toward positive outcomes, like Jupiter or Venus.</note> planet is in the 1st <term>house</term> <note>Bhava: the sector of the sky representing the self and physical presence.</note>, but a <term>malefic</term> <note>A planet associated with hardship, like Saturn or Mars.</note> planet is in the 2nd house as well as in a <term>Kendra</term> <note>A "quadrant" or "pivot" house (1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th), which are the pillars of a birth chart.</note>, and when the lord of the <term>Ascendant</term> <note>Lagna: the zodiac sign rising at the moment of birth.</note> has attained the <term>Uttamamsa</term> <note>A position of high dignity where a planet is placed in three favorable divisional charts.</note>, the person born suffers misery in early life and is happy thereafter. **40**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्ने शुभे धने पापे केन्द्रे पापसमन्विते । लग्नेश्वरे तूत्तमांशे चादौ दुःखं ततः सुखम् ॥ ४० ॥"</note>

---

->When the lord of the Ascendant occupies an auspicious sign and is aspected by a beneficial planet or has attained a <term>Gopuramsa</term> <note>A planet is in Gopuramsa when it occupies four favorable divisional charts, indicating great strength and protective power.</note>, the person born will be happy after their 16th year. **41**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्नेशे शुभराशिस्थे शुभग्रहनिरीक्षिते । गोपुरांशगते वाऽपि षोडशाब्दात्परं सुखम् ॥ ४१ ॥"</note>

---

->When the planet ruling the <term>Navamsa</term> <note>The 9th-part divisional chart, often used to see the hidden strength or the "fruit" of a planet's placement.</note> occupied by the lord of the Ascendant is in a quadrant, a <term>trine</term> <note>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses, considered the most auspicious sectors.</note>, or its sign of exaltation, or is in the 11th house possessed of strength, the person born will be happy after the first 30 years of their life. **42**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्नेशस्यांशनाथे तु केन्द्रकोणोच्चसंयुते । लाभे वा बलसंयुक्ते त्रिंशद्वर्षात्परं सुखम् ॥ ४२ ॥"</note>

---

->When the Ascendant is aspected by the Sun and other planets, the effect on the person born is given in order for each planet:<-

->If the **Sun** is the viewing planet, they will serve the king; if the **Moon**, they will possess wealth inherited from their father; if **Mars**, they will trade in liquid goods and be very wealthy. **43**<-

->If **Mercury**, they will be righteous and physically robust <note>The original term "sthula-shishrah" is a traditional physiognomic marker referring to physical anatomy, often used to predict vitality or progeny.</note>; if **Jupiter**, they will be endowed with learning, craftsmanship, and fame; if **Venus**, they will be honored by royalty and follow sacred vows; and if **Saturn**, they will be attracted to courtesans, yet remain wealthy and happy. **44**<-
<note>original Sanskrit: "लग्ने रव्यादिसंदृष्टे पादशः फलमुच्यते । राजसेवी पितृधनो जलपण्यो महाधनः ॥ ४३ ॥ धार्मिकः स्थूलशिश्रः स्याद्विद्याशिल्पयशोन्वितः । राजपूज्यो व्रतयुतो वेश्यासक्तो धनी सुखी ॥ ४४ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page details the specific planetary conditions that determine whether a person's happiness arrives in youth, after age 16, or after age 30. It also provides a list of character traits and career paths—ranging from royal service to merchant trade—based on which planet aspects the Ascendant.</summary>
